status: accepted
---

# Workbook-backed project command model

Repository ADR 0036 supersedes every `host-class list`, document-scoped host
projection, source-template host inspection, partial/last-known-good projection,
and related extension lifecycle passage below. The replacement is the
environment-scoped `host-event list` command, which inspects only an unsaved
blank workbook and temporary UserForm. The remaining workbook-backed command,
transaction, process-ownership, and cleanup model stays accepted.

`vba-dev` will model Office macro automation as a Windows-only, workbook-backed command-line workflow centered on a `vba-project.json` project manifest. The manifest identifies the project and primary document, supplies command defaults, and anchors path resolution so commands can behave consistently from project subdirectories and future multi-document projects.

## Collection and distribution wrapper correction (2026-08-21)

The current `COLLECT_COMMON_MODS` implementation intentionally uses a simple PowerShell latest-file rule. The canonical CommonModules manifest defines the collected basenames. The collector scans the fixed workspace root for `vba-project.json`, resolves every document `sourcePath`, searches those source sets recursively, and selects the greatest `LastWriteTimeUtc` for each listed module. Equal newest timestamps are accepted only when the bytes are equal; a different-content tie or a missing candidate fails before copying. `common_modules_repo` directories are generated outputs and are excluded from source discovery.

This correction supersedes the collection-specific claims below that one canonical authoring `DocumentSourceSet` is the sole byte source, that timestamps are never used, or that the current PowerShell wrapper provides an atomic multi-file source snapshot and transaction protocol. Those passages remain as design history, not implementation requirements. The collection workflow remains ordinary PowerShell file discovery and copying; it adds no managed helper, native lease, cancellation state machine, or transactional recovery requirement.

The current `DIST_COMMON_MODS_REPO` implementation is likewise a small PowerShell copy workflow. Child argument six supplies the source directory; when it is empty, the script uses `common_modules_repo` below its invocation-start working directory. The source directory's grandparent is the search root. The script sorts that root's immediate child directories, selects only their existing direct-child `common_modules_repo` directories, excludes the source itself, leaves byte-identical flat targets unchanged, and clears and recursively copies every other target in sequence. An empty source or no target fails. The first terminating file-system error stops the run, so an earlier target may already be updated and the current target may be incomplete.

This correction supersedes the distribution-specific claims below about fixed search-root authority, native handles or repository leases, custom cancellation and deadline state machines, snapshots, transaction workspaces, rollback, per-candidate result rows, counts, summaries, and bounded output protocols. Those passages remain design history, not current implementation requirements. The PowerShell script and its focused wrapper tests are authoritative for current distribution behavior.

## Decision

Project manifests use `schemaVersion: 1`, are generated as UTF-16LE with BOM, and are the source of truth for resolving the project root, primary document, document source set, bin output, publish output, and default command options. Every canonical write uses two-space indentation, CRLF throughout, exactly one trailing CRLF, and the serializer's stable schema-defined property order. `ProjectManifestRecoveryArtifact` uses the same representation. The reader accepts UTF-16LE with BOM and UTF-8 inputs for compatibility and does not reject otherwise valid JSON solely for whitespace or property order, but generated or rewritten manifests normalize to the canonical representation.

`commandDefaults` records only an explicit durable override of CLI behavior; it is not a snapshot of the CLI defaults that happened to apply when a project was created. An initial `new excel` manifest therefore omits `commandDefaults` when the caller or guided flow selected no override, rather than persisting the redundant `test.format: "text"`. This lets a later CLI default evolve without silently pinning every older project. The initial manifest includes `commonModulesRepository` only when a repository was actually selected and omits the property, rather than writing `null`, when the canonical repository is conclusively absent. In contrast, each document always emits `commonModules` and `references`, including explicit empty arrays, because those properties are the document's complete effective selections rather than optional default-value overrides.

The installed extension declaratively associates its bundled `schemas/project-manifest.schema.json` with the exact canonical basename pattern `**/vba-project.json` through `contributes.jsonValidation`; a manifest does not carry a `$schema` property or require a network lookup, and a `ProjectManifestRecoveryArtifact` does not match the association. The bundled **ProjectManifestSchema** uses JSON Schema draft-07 because VS Code fully supports that draft while its newer-draft support remains partial. Schema-1 owned objects are closed with `additionalProperties: false`, and property names use their exact case-sensitive contract spellings. The schema supplies editing completion and structural diagnostics, while `VbaDev` remains authoritative for UTF-16LE-with-BOM and line-ending rules, case-insensitive identity uniqueness, path isolation, cross-field relationships, and every other domain invariant that JSON Schema does not own. The bundled schema, runtime structural validator, serializer, and shared valid/invalid manifest fixtures must change as one contract unit; command-result schemas remain separate and may retain their independently specified additive-property consumer policy.

Document definitions in one manifest must satisfy **DocumentSourceSetIsolation**.
After resolving filesystem-canonical, case-insensitive absolute identities, no
two document `sourcePath` roots may be equal and neither may contain the other;
filesystem aliases to an overlapping subtree are equally invalid. `vba-dev`
and the language server reject the manifest rather than selecting the first
matching document. Intentional source sharing uses CommonModules or another
explicit distribution relationship instead of making one physical source file
belong to multiple VBA project contexts with different references, templates,
or source classifications.

`new excel` extends that isolation check across existing ancestor projects twice: before it creates an artifact and immediately before its initial manifest's atomic move. Each pass walks the complete current ancestor chain for `vba-project.json`, including a manifest that appeared after the first pass, and resolves every declared document source root to the same filesystem-canonical, case-insensitive identity model used for the proposed target. Creation is rejected when the proposed project root is equal to or contained by an ancestor **DocumentSourceSet**, including through a junction, symbolic link, or casing alias, because the ancestor's recursive source inventory would otherwise absorb the child project. An unreadable or invalid latest ancestor manifest, or an identity that cannot be established safely, fails closed. A changed ancestor is accepted when its latest valid state remains physically disjoint; otherwise the command fails before its manifest move and uses the established rollback contract. `new excel` does not acquire ancestor project leases, and guided creation does not duplicate this CLI authority, so a non-cooperating change in the narrow interval after the final scan remains outside the guarantee. A project root nested beneath another project root remains permissible when it is physically disjoint from every ancestor document source set; that tolerance avoids unnecessarily blocking an intentional repository layout but is not a promoted workflow and is not advertised in the README. CommonModules or another explicit relationship remains the supported way to share source.

Project creation follows the `dotnet new <TEMPLATE>` shape. The initial template is `excel`, so users create a project with `new excel`; future Office document kinds can become additional template names. `--output` selects the project root directory. `--name` selects the generated project and document base name, and when omitted it is derived from the output directory name or current directory name.

Direct CLI option presence is exact and is not inferred with `IsNullOrWhiteSpace`. When both `--name` and `--output` are present, the original name spelling is retained and the output is lexically normalized to the absolute **RequestedProjectRoot**. With only `--name`, the root is the invocation-start working directory's child bearing that exact name. With only `--output`, the name is the final non-root component of its lexically normalized absolute root. With neither, the invocation-start working directory is the root and its final non-root component is the name. An explicitly supplied empty or whitespace-only `--name` is never treated as omission, trimmed, or replaced: it reaches **ProjectNameLexicalContract** and fails with `projectNameEmpty` or `projectNameHasLeadingOrTrailingWhitespace`. An explicitly empty `--output` fails before artifact creation instead of becoming the default root; a whitespace-only value is likewise preserved as supplied for ordinary Windows-path validation rather than trimmed or treated as omission. A normalized volume or share root with no final non-root component yields `projectNameEmpty` and tells the caller to supply `--name`. Every derived name retains the component's exact casing and spelling and passes through the same project-name and Excel-workbook-path contracts as an explicit name.

`--output` accepts a Windows filesystem path visible to the CLI process, not a VS Code URI. Ordinary relative paths remain relative to the invocation-start working directory, and fully qualified drive and UNC paths are eligible; `file:`, `vscode-remote:`, and virtual-provider URI strings are neither accepted nor converted into paths. Path reachability and complete target eligibility remain authoritative CLI checks.

Creation separates that public **RequestedProjectRoot** from its ownership-bearing **ProjectRootIdentity**. The requested root is the lexically normalized absolute spelling selected by the caller; it is retained for text and JSON `project` and `manifestPath` values and for explicit navigation, and is never replaced by a final physical path. The root identity resolves junctions, symbolic links, case aliases, and other filesystem aliases case-insensitively and is used only for the mutation lease, collision, ownership, and safety decisions. Alias spellings that reach the same physical target therefore converge on one identity and lease. No physical canonical path is added to the manifest or command result.

When all or part of the target does not yet exist, `new excel` establishes the prospective identity from the nearest existing ancestor whose filesystem identity it can prove and the remaining normalized components. It must re-establish continuity after creating the root and immediately before the initial manifest commit; a changed reparse route, alias target, or other inability to prove the same identity fails creation rather than committing under uncertain ownership. Result consumers validate the requested lexical path from the result and do not reread or canonicalize project files to reconstruct this transaction proof.

`new excel` creates its **ExcelProjectTemplateBaseline** by passing the explicit `xlWBATWorksheet` template constant to `Workbooks.Add`, which produces one worksheet rather than inheriting the user-configured `SheetsInNewWorkbook` count used when the argument is omitted ([Microsoft `Workbooks.Add`](https://learn.microsoft.com/office/vba/api/excel.workbooks.add)). The initial source template is semantically empty and contains exactly that one worksheet. The initial contract neither bundles a binary workbook template nor accepts an arbitrary initial-template path. Excel-version serialization differences are allowed, so two generated `.xlsm` files are not required to be byte-identical.

Every generated baseline establishes an **InitialWorkbookIdentityBaseline** before the initial manifest commit: the sole worksheet's visible tab name and document-module name are both `Sheet1`, the workbook document-module name is `ThisWorkbook`, and the actual `VBProject.Name` is `VBAProject`. `ProjectManifest.projectName`, the document name, and the workbook file name do not implicitly rename any of these VBA identities, so `--name` remains a tooling and filesystem name rather than acquiring VBA-identifier constraints. Failure to establish the exact identity set aborts project creation before its manifest commit.

`new excel` derives its **InitialVbaProjectReferenceSelection** from authority rather than a fixed convenience list. It retains every actual reference of the newly created baseline workbook in VBE order except `Visual Basic For Applications`, whose **VbaStandardLibraryReference** is always active outside manifest selection and is not treated as an unlisted protected-reference warning. Those baseline entries are recorded with `requested: true`. It then traverses CommonModules in the committed initial order above and each entry's RequiredReferences in TSV declaration order, appending each first-seen missing VBE-equivalent canonical name with `requested: false`. A case-insensitive match to an active baseline reference preserves that baseline spelling, position, and `requested: true`; every later duplicate preserves the first position without moving or recasing it. Manifest, text, and JSON therefore share one order: baseline VBE order followed by CommonModules requirement first-encounter order. With no installed CommonModules requirement, creation adds neither Scripting Runtime nor RegExp merely by convention. It never infers reference requirements by scanning VBA source.

The saved source template retains exactly the baseline workbook's actual initial reference identities and order. A CommonModules-required reference is persisted only in the manifest and is materialized later when build, test, or publish copies the template and reconciles the complete manifest selection. If `new excel` temporarily adds a reference to its owned workbook for VBE-equivalent resolution, it removes that probe state before saving or performs the probe on a disposable copy; immediately before the initial manifest commit it proves that the saved template still has the baseline reference selection. Failure to restore or verify that baseline fails creation and rolls back rather than committing a template whose hidden reference state duplicates part of manifest intent.

The initial source layout is document-oriented even though only one primary Excel document is supported at first: `src/<document-name>/` contains the source template `.xlsm` and exported VBA source. Generated workbook outputs do not need a document-name subdirectory when the workbook file name already carries the document name, so new projects default to `bin/<document-name>.xlsm` and `publish/<document-name>.xlsm`. Existing manifests that name paths such as `bin/<document-name>/<document-name>.xlsm` and `publish/<document-name>/<document-name>.xlsm` remain valid because `vba-project.json` is the source of truth for output path resolution. Document-level commands accept `--project` and `--document`; omitted values resolve by walking upward to the nearest `vba-project.json` and then using the manifest's `primaryDocument`.

When `new excel` can copy initial CommonModules from the canonical repository, every entry categorized `runtime-baseline` or `test-foundation` is a directly requested root and is recorded with `requested: true`. It expands their complete dependency closure; an entry reached only as a dependency and carrying neither root category is recorded with `requested: false`, while a categorized root remains `requested: true` even when traversal first reaches it through another root. `optional` and `test-double` do not independently select an entry. The command records the complete closure under the generated document's `commonModules` manifest list and copies each source unit into the generated **CommonModulesDirectory** using its extension-including file name. Initial projects therefore start with the standard build-and-test foundation as a manifest-backed installed set rather than loose copied files; a smaller foundation would require a future explicit template or profile rather than implicit category omission.

Every CommonModules repository entry has exactly one primary role from `runtime-baseline`, `test-foundation`, `optional`, and `test-double`. `public-udf` is an orthogonal modifier allowed only with either runtime role, so the complete accepted sets are each primary role alone plus `runtime-baseline,public-udf` and `optional,public-udf`. `new excel`, CommonModules Add, and CommonModules Update derive and record `testOnly: true` exactly from the two test roles and `false` from the two runtime roles; `public-udf` never changes that fact. A missing or mixed primary role, a test role combined with `public-udf`, or any other category set invalidates the complete repository snapshot before project mutation instead of relying on category precedence. An orphaned installed entry keeps its recorded classification until the same repository identity is conclusively restored and refreshed.

The canonical `Categories` cell is one of the six exact whole-cell strings `runtime-baseline`, `runtime-baseline,public-udf`, `test-foundation`, `optional`, `optional,public-udf`, or `test-double`; casing, modifier order, and delimiter placement are significant. The canonical `Dependencies` cell is either zero-length or a comma-separated sequence of target-row `ModuleFile` spellings with no whitespace or empty item. Every item must match the target row exactly, including casing, while duplicate and self-edge identity use `OrdinalIgnoreCase`; declared order is retained. Readers reject rather than trim, recase, drop empty items, deduplicate, or sort either cell.

Each `ModuleFile` is one exact flat source basename in the form `<CommonModuleName>.bas`, `<CommonModuleName>.cls`, or `<CommonModuleName>.frm`; the extension is lowercase and agrees with the exported source kind. Rooted names, directory separators, `.` or `..` segments, whitespace, commas, tabs, line breaks, and control characters are invalid rather than normalized or embedded in repository identity. The canonical authoring **DocumentSourceSet** is searched recursively and treated as one flat source inventory: relative directories are organizational only, and every manifest row must resolve to exactly one extension-including filename under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`. The matched physical filename must match the manifest spelling ordinally even on a case-insensitive filesystem. Its stem is a valid at-most-31-code-point `ModuleIdentity` and must ordinally equal the source unit's authoritative `ModuleIdentityMetadata`; missing, malformed, overlength, differently cased, kind-incompatible, absent, or multiply resolved metadata invalidates collection. One `CommonModuleName` identifies at most one row under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, including across different extensions. A manifest-listed `.frm` may own one optional same-directory `.frx` whose basename and casing match exactly; `.frx` never has its own row or dependency identity, and an invalid sidecar selected for that listed form invalidates collection. Collection writes every selected source unit and sidecar at the `common_modules_repo` root under its flat basename, so authoring subdirectories neither create namespaces nor survive distribution or installation.

Collection enumerates the recursive authoring source inventory only to establish the CommonModules package selection, resolve every manifest-listed `ModuleFile` uniquely, and report unlisted source. Every ordinary `.bas`, `.cls`, and `.frm` entry not selected by a manifest row remains authoring-only. If its extensionless basename does not begin with `Test_` under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, collection emits a manifest-not-listed warning and continues; an unlisted `Test_` entry is omitted silently. One collection invocation aggregates all such warnings into one block containing the count and each unique source-root-relative path ordered by `OrdinalIgnoreCase` and then `Ordinal`. A source that makes a listed `ModuleFile` ambiguous appears only in that resolution error, never again in the warning block. The warning alone changes neither package equality, write behavior, result status, nor exit code: an identical output remains `UNCHANGED` with exit `0`, and a published output remains `SUCCEEDED` with exit `0`. Neither kind of unlisted source is included in the package content snapshot or subsequent change checks. Collection does not validate its raw bytes or module metadata, reject flat-identity collisions that exist solely among unlisted units, or validate its `.frm`/`.frx` pairing. An unlisted entry that prevents unique resolution of a listed `ModuleFile` still invalidates that listed selection, and the general reparse-point safety rule continues to apply. Authoring-project source health belongs to `vba-dev build`, `vba-dev test`, and Doctor rather than collection. The `Test_` distinction is only warning suppression; it does not make that source `TestOnlyVbaSource`, exclude it from ordinary build or publish, or give a `tests` directory semantic meaning. Non-source authoring auxiliaries are ignored by collection. A completed `common_modules_repo` is instead a closed flat package containing only the canonical manifest, exactly one root-level source unit for every row, and each listed `.frm` unit's optional matching root-level `.frx`; any other file or directory makes the complete repository snapshot invalid before a repository-backed project mutation.

Every `common_modules_repo` is a wholly generated projection rather than a directory that may mix generated and user-owned content. Once collection or distribution has resolved and validated the exact target directory boundary, it replaces that target's complete child inventory and removes every item absent from the new package, regardless of the old manifest's validity, file bytes, hidden status, directory shape, or version-control state. It does not merge, adopt, preserve, or request confirmation for an unexpected child item, and it does not use Git as ownership or recovery authority. This ownership applies only below the exact repository target; the containing project and ordinary sibling paths remain outside the replacement boundary. Collection publicly reserves one lowercase ordinal-exact direct sibling named `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.collect-workspace` as the **CollectionTransactionWorkspace** beside its canonical target. Direct entries are matched to that basename under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`; zero matches means no pre-existing transaction state, while exactly one ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse directory may become latest-wins repair input only after one complete stable current authoring snapshot is accepted. A case-only spelling, more than one match, a non-directory entry, or a reparse root leaves every match untouched and fails collection before workspace or target mutation. The accepted directory and its complete physically contained, recursively reparse-free subtree are wholly generated and disposable, including malformed, contradictory, unknown, orphaned, mismatched, or extra record, staging, and backup content; internal basenames and stored paths never add authority outside the workspace. Fresh current-attempt artifact names remain implementation details and use create-new semantics inside that boundary. Distribution publicly reserves one lowercase ordinal-exact direct sibling named `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.dist-workspace` as the **DistributionTransactionWorkspace** for each destination slot under the same locator rules. Each accepted workspace is a wholly generated disposable projection for its owning operation, but collection does not touch its workspace before source-snapshot admission and distribution does not touch its workspace before central-snapshot admission and destination-lease acquisition. Neither command edits Git ignore configuration for these reservations. A wrong-type, reparse-bearing, escaping, or identity-unproved workspace is not traversed or deleted. The fixed `CommonModulesRepositorySlotLease` is the only additional direct-sibling authority and is limited to acquiring OS ownership and performing its lease-specific required release for that one slot; it grants no package, transaction, repair, or opt-in authority. Every other project sibling remains outside tool authority.

Every canonical `common_modules_repo` slot uses one root-locator grammar under its proved physical parent and `CommonModulesRepositorySlotLease`. The complete direct-sibling set whose basenames equal `common_modules_repo` under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` is authoritative: zero matches alone proves absence; exactly one match proves presence only when it has ordinal-exact spelling, is an ordinary non-reparse directory, and has a proved physical identity and boundary. A case-only match, multiple matches, a file or other wrong entry type, a reparse entry, or unprovable existence, identity, or boundary grants neither absence nor mutation authority and is left untouched. Wholly generated, partial, foreign, dirty, or disposable content refers only to descendants of an accepted directory root, never the root entry itself or a conflicting sibling. Collection may create the root from a proved zero-match state only after stable current-authoring snapshot acceptance. Distribution may recreate a zero-match destination only through an exact safe `DistributionTransactionWorkspace`; a zero-match or invalid central source remains a pre-queue global failure. An initial collection root-locator violation or pre-queue central-source admission violation is an invocation-level failure with no collection result or destination queue and exit `1`. A destination violation established before result commitment is one candidate-local `FAILED` result, and later candidates may continue after conclusive release of every candidate-opened handle when no earlier stop cause prevents it. A violation established by a later canonical-root reproof uses the existing phase-specific global-failure, factual-result, restoration, and ownership rules without touching a new or unproved match. The pre-authority warning-skip rule remains only for inability to establish whether an ordinary marker or workspace locator exists before candidate mutation; once an exact safe workspace supplies repair authority, unprovable canonical-root state is `FAILED`. Every later canonical-root reproof uses this same grammar.

Collection and distribution use fixed package authorities instead of deriving source or output from the caller's current directory or from a supplied source ancestor. Collection always reads the canonical `CommonModules/src/CommonModules` owned by the script's repository and writes only that repository's canonical `common_modules_repo`; it accepts no alternate public source or output. Distribution always reads that same canonical output and requires one explicit existing ordinary Windows filesystem search root under which the script-owned canonical repository root is an immediate child. After validating only that static search-root relationship and proving the canonical repository's physical parent, it derives the central repository slot from that parent plus the exact `common_modules_repo` child name and acquires the slot exclusively before checking whether the replaceable child exists, reading it, or discovering destination candidates. After the post-acquisition cancellation fence admits repository inspection, distribution first proves the absence of **PendingCollectionTransactionState** by enumerating direct siblings and requiring zero basenames equal to `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.collect-workspace` under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`; it never opens a matching entry. If such state exists or its absence cannot be proved, distribution neither interprets, modifies, nor deletes it, performs no central-package or destination inspection, returns an unchanged global exit-`1` failure with no candidate queue, rows, or zero-target classification, and directs the user to run `COLLECT_COMMON_MODS` for current-source latest-wins repair or, when the workspace boundary is unsafe or unprovable, manual guidance. Cancellation while waiting returns `130`, and timeout returns an unchanged global exit-`1` failure; because neither outcome has a candidate queue, neither emits candidate rows nor a zero-target classification. One initial name-level discovery under the acquired central lease fixes lexically distinct candidate paths in normalized absolute `OrdinalIgnoreCase` then `Ordinal` order: each exact ordinary `common_modules_repo` marker contributes its child path, and each exact known `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.dist-workspace` locator contributes the associated canonical child path derived from that reserved location without opening its contents. Marker and workspace observations for the same lexical path merge, but physical aliases do not. Cancellation before that queue and its screening results are finalized stops discovery, emits no partial candidate rows, warning aggregates, counts, or zero-target classification, and begins no destination work. Distribution does not continue discovery merely to find another failure. Conclusive central-handle and ownership release returns `130` when no failure was already established; an established discovery or safety failure, or uncertain release, instead emits one invocation-level diagnostic and returns exit `1`, still without candidate rows. If the terminal same-handle observation and cleanup authority are conclusive, the one central-marker disposition attempt establishes an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admits that exact cause, and the still-unarmed handle is then conclusively released, distribution retains exit `130` and adds only the retained-marker warning. Exactly one case-insensitive marker match with ordinal-exact spelling is valid; multiple matches, a case-only mismatch, or a known unsafe marker is one unchanged candidate-local `FAILED` result after the queue is finalized. The canonical central source path is excluded normally. Every other candidate whose physical repository boundary is equal to, an ancestor of, or a descendant of the canonical central repository boundary, and every candidate whose canonical target itself or recursive target inventory contains a junction, symbolic link, or other reparse entry, is unchanged `FAILED`. A conclusively present workspace locator with an invalid name, cardinality, entry type, root reparse state, static containment, or identity boundary is likewise `FAILED`; inability to establish whether a marker or workspace locator exists, or otherwise to observe the candidate evidence needed for screening, is warning-skipped as absent. Distribution compares each candidate with the central boundary during initial screening but does not precompute pairwise physical alias or containment relationships between destination candidates. Git metadata, project manifests, repository names, and workspace contents never add ordinary target authority. A later marker or workspace waits for the next invocation, and discovery is never widened or repeated. Alternate paths exist only as internal test seams. The unrestricted BAT caller interface reaches the protected child checks through the unchanged six-value projection described below: caller argument one affects the legacy directory, filename, and full-path fields in child positions four through six, while later caller arguments remain ignored. Collection accepts every value produced in child positions four through six but ignores all three for source, output, package, result, warning, and mutation authority, always using its fixed canonical source and output. Distribution alone treats child position six as operation input: an empty value produced by an absent or explicitly empty caller argument one is a protected usage failure, while a nonempty value proceeds to the protected one-search-root validation. These are child-script authority rules rather than BAT arity checks; no BAT reception, projection, or forwarding behavior changes. Before emitting its own text, the BAT wrapper fixes its UI mode without consuming standard input or changing the handles inherited by the child, then proves whether both standard input and standard output are attached directly to an interactive console. Only a conclusive direct-console state enables the existing prelude, separators, neutral footer, and 60-second post-operation delay as out-of-band UI. In every mode `_main.ps1` inherits the child's stdout and stderr handles unchanged. Either BAT standard input or standard output being redirected, or the attachment proof being unavailable, suppresses every BAT-owned line and the delay; the wrapper saves the complete PowerShell child exit immediately and returns it unchanged without a postlude. BAT-owned UI never participates in `OutputTransportFailure` and never rewrites a child repository outcome or saved exit. If wrapper interruption or host closure prevents observation of the outer exit, any captured text follows the partial-transcript rule below.

The BAT bootstrap has one fixed execution authority. It resolves the in-box `WindowsPowerShell\v1.0\powershell.exe` beneath the trusted Windows system directory to a fully qualified path and never consults the caller's current directory or `PATH` for a host. It invokes its ordinal-exact sibling `collect_common_mods_main.ps1` or `dist_common_mods_repo_main.ps1`, as applicable, once with `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -NoLogo -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File` followed by the established six forwarded arguments. It never loads a PowerShell profile and never falls back to or retries ambient `powershell`, `pwsh`, another executable, or another script path. Failure to resolve the trusted host or applicable exact sibling script, or to start the host, is a wrapper-level exit `1` before any repository lease, snapshot, workspace, canonical-repository inspection, or mutation begins; it emits no collection result, destination row, or summary. The child retains the already established standard-handle inheritance, BAT UI mode, caller-position projection, and six-position forwarding contract.

Outside one top-level protected operation boundary, the entry script permits only repository-side-effect-free, non-authoritative bootstrap: setting process-local preferences and fixed constants, defining functions and types, binding the six already-forwarded strings without public validation or output, and constructing but not resolving fixed trusted entry or support-code lexical paths. That bootstrap never changes the current directory, inspects or mutates CommonModules source or manifests, central or destination repositories, canonical roots, markers, workspaces, snapshots, or candidates, emits public operation output, or acquires a domain handle or lease. The top-level exception-to-exit boundary begins before every fallible support-code import or initialization and encloses all later work; an imported module or initializer performs no CommonModules domain I/O, resource acquisition, or public operation output merely by loading. Before the first CommonModules-related filesystem observation or operation, cancellation observation, an empty owned-resource cleanup registry, and the bounded PowerShell output coordinator are live, and the first cancellation fence has passed. Every later CommonModules filesystem observation, current-directory change, handle or lease acquisition, and mutation occurs inside the same `try`/`catch`/`finally` boundary, and each acquired resource is registered before any subsequent fallible step. Every present or future helper and module initializer preserves this invariant rather than hiding work before the boundary. A catchable non-cancellation import, initialization, or other pre-operation failure performs no retry, leaves every repository unchanged, emits no collection result, destination row, or summary, and returns exit `1`; cancellation at the first fence uses the established no-work cancellation result.

Inside the existing top-level protected pre-domain boundary and before either command enters any CommonModules domain phase, each fresh PowerShell child performs exactly one invocation-wide handle-independent interop bootstrap. It uses `Add-Type` to compile, load, and validate the fixed in-process native-interop holder, one sealed in-process `LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture` type with private one-shot construction, cleanup, and retirement states plus a serialized repeatable observation state and payload storage, its fixed nonterminal `Ready` and `InFlight` tags, its fixed terminal `PairConstructed`, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent`, and `FileStreamConstructionFailed` tags, the fixed public enum `LeaseOwnershipObservationMode` with only `NonTerminal = 1` and `NormalTerminal = 2`, the fixed public enum `FullPairRetirementMode` with only `NormalTerminal = 1` and `RetirementOnly = 2`, exactly four non-overloaded managed methods, `public static void ConstructNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair(IntPtr raw, LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture holder)`, `public static void ObserveLeaseOwnership(LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture holder, long observationOrdinal, LeaseOwnershipObservationMode mode)`, `public static void RetireSafeHandleOnly(LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture holder)`, and `public static void RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair(LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture holder, FullPairRetirementMode mode)`, and the exact direct static-extern managed declarations for `CreateFileW`, `SetFileInformationByHandle`, and `CloseHandle`. The sole raw-close declaration is exactly `[DllImport("kernel32.dll", EntryPoint = "CloseHandle", ExactSpelling = true, SetLastError = true, CallingConvention = CallingConvention.Winapi, CharSet = CharSet.None, PreserveSig = true)] [return: MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.Bool)] public static extern bool CloseHandle(IntPtr hObject)`. `IntPtr` carries the registered exact raw value, and explicit four-byte Win32 `BOOL` marshaling maps zero to `false` and every nonzero value to `true`; no `SafeHandle` parameter or return, direct `GetLastError` import, pre-call `SetLastError(0)`, overload, or substitute close API is permitted. The capture holder has no finalizer, `IDisposable`, or public state mutator and reserves private storage for the bound raw value, both exact wrapper references, separate caught-constructor-exception references, the monotonic construction state, the separate monotonic SafeHandle-only cleanup state and its exact caught-cleanup-exception reference, the separate serialized observation state, monotonic `Int64` ordinal, generation-local observation-mode binding, and exact caught-`FileStream.Flush(true)`-exception reference, and the separate monotonic full-pair retirement state, immutable retirement-mode binding, and exact caught-`FileStream.Dispose`-exception reference; bootstrap invokes none of the four managed methods, no managed-wrapper constructor, and no wrapper `Flush` or `Dispose`. The `CreateFileW` declaration imports `kernel32.dll` and fixes managed ABI `IntPtr CreateFileW([MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] string lpFileName, uint dwDesiredAccess, uint dwShareMode, IntPtr lpSecurityAttributes, uint dwCreationDisposition, uint dwFlagsAndAttributes, IntPtr hTemplateFile)`, `EntryPoint = CreateFileW`, `CharSet.Unicode`, `ExactSpelling = true`, `SetLastError = true`, `CallingConvention.Winapi`, and a raw `IntPtr` return; it never returns an owning `SafeFileHandle` or other managed owner. The same bootstrap fixes the handle-independent constants for `GENERIC_READ | GENERIC_WRITE | DELETE`, share mode zero, null security attributes, `CREATE_NEW`, `OPEN_EXISTING`, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` with no `FILE_FLAG_*`, a null template handle, and `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, plus the exact one-byte `FILE_DISPOSITION_INFO` layout and initialized reusable buffer. Register any owned buffer under the existing cleanup-registry rule, but invoke no native API and do not claim that lazy native DLL or entry-point binding or per-handle marshalling has occurred. No slot or attempt repeats or revalidates this bootstrap. A catchable non-cancellation failure performs no retry, leaves every repository unchanged, creates no disposition attempt, state, or lease, emits no COLLECT result and no DIST queue, candidate row, zero-target classification, count, aggregate, or summary, and returns exit `1`. A hang or nonreturn retains the existing pre-domain availability rule.

The retained BAT six-position forwarding shape preserves the existing projection, not merely the number of fields. Positions one through three are the normalized wrapper directory, wrapper filename, and wrapper full path. Caller argument one is processed by the existing `cmd.exe` quote removal, path projection, attribute query, and directory-versus-file adjustment to produce positions four through six: its projected directory, filename, and full path. An absent or explicitly empty caller argument one produces the existing empty projections; caller arguments two and later remain ignored. For a BAT launch, that unchanged projection resolves a relative caller argument one against the BAT launcher's invocation-start filesystem current working directory. A supported direct launcher captures its own invocation-start filesystem current working directory and uses the same base while reconstructing the BAT-equivalent positions before starting the child. The child never uses its inherited or later current directory, `$PSScriptRoot`, or another fallback base to resolve a public caller path. The launcher's filesystem current working directory is only a lexical projection base and never package or repository authority. The BAT neither substitutes fixed tokens for these fields nor introduces a new input grammar. The projection observations and values are launcher compatibility plumbing, not package, repository, or mutation authority, and do not replace protected child validation. Collection treats all caller-derived positions four through six as non-authoritative compatibility inputs regardless of their values and neither validates them as operation paths nor changes its canonical source, output, result, or diagnostics because of them. Distribution treats positions four and five as non-authoritative compatibility inputs and validates position six only after its guards are live and the first cancellation fence passes. Position six must be one nonempty fully qualified Windows-filesystem search-root projection; an empty or raw relative child value is a usage failure and is never resolved against the child's current directory or `$PSScriptRoot`, while a conforming projected value undergoes the established one-search-root validation. Extra BAT caller arguments never request another operation or search root. A supported direct child-process caller reconstructs the same six projected values that the applicable BAT would have produced for that wrapper and caller argument one; it does not invent fixed sentinel values or a different argument grammar.

Collection and distribution deliberately add no external bootstrap supervisor, private readiness gate, or pre-operation deadline, and they do not use `vba-dev` or another executable for launch supervision. A BAT wrapper continues to start the one fixed in-box PowerShell host and exact `_main.ps1` directly. The supported direct child-process form described below also remains permitted under the same child-local protected boundary, but it receives no BAT-owned UI guarantee. Consequently, fixed-host and script resolution, child startup, entry-script reading and parsing, entry-script definition setup, domain-I/O-free support-code import or initialization, the one-time handle-independent native-interop bootstrap, and establishment of cancellation observation, owned-resource cleanup tracking, and bounded PowerShell output may wait indefinitely before the first CommonModules domain observation. This is an accepted narrow availability gap, not mutation authority: the preceding invariant forbids every CommonModules source, manifest, repository, canonical-root, marker, lease, snapshot, workspace, candidate, and target observation or operation throughout that interval, and no collection result, destination queue or row, count, aggregate, zero-target classification, or summary is emitted. A catchable failure retains the pre-operation exit-`1` contract. A hang or interruption before cancellation observation is live may require manual or operating-system termination; it is not guaranteed cooperative cancellation or exit `130`, provides no authoritative operation exit, and leaves every captured BAT-, host-, or script-owned fragment under the partial-transcript rule. Loss or termination of the BAT wrapper alone does not prove that its PowerShell child terminated: until exact child termination is observed, callers infer neither future repository stability nor safe retry, because a live child may later complete its guards and begin the ordinary protected operation. Once the guards are live and the first cancellation fence passes, the established phase-specific cancellation, timeout, output, cleanup, ownership, and release rules apply. This decision declines only the new deadline for that pre-domain interval; it neither removes any established later deadline nor claims that every later filesystem API call is independently interruptible.

A caller may omit the BAT wrapper only by starting a fresh process of the same fully qualified in-box Windows PowerShell host and supplying the applicable ordinal-exact `_main.ps1` through `-File`. The direct caller replaces BAT as launcher: it resolves the trusted host without `PATH` or current-directory lookup, captures its invocation-start filesystem current working directory before any launcher-side change, uses `-NoProfile -NonInteractive -NoLogo -ExecutionPolicy Bypass -File`, reconstructs and supplies the same established six projected values that the applicable BAT would have produced for that wrapper and caller argument one, wires the intended standard handles unchanged, manages the child lifetime, and observes the native process exit. No BAT-owned prelude, footer, delay, or UI guarantee applies; the direct caller itself performs the established six-value projection without adding a new argument grammar. Applying the call operator `&` to `_main.ps1`, dot-sourcing it, reusing a runspace, or using any equivalent same-session mechanism is unsupported because it supplies no independently observable child-process exit and may share current-directory, preference, loaded-definition, cancellation, or stream state. Such an in-process call gains no alternative repository, output, cleanup, cancellation, or exit contract. The supported direct child-process form requires neither `vba-dev` nor another executable.

Both a BAT launch and a supported direct child-process launch pass exactly six positional strings after the `_main.ps1` path; there is no shortened, extended, named, or fixed-sentinel alternative grammar. For a BAT launch, positions one through three are the wrapper directory, wrapper filename, and wrapper full path, and positions four through six are caller argument one's projected directory, filename, and full path under the unchanged BAT projection. A supported direct launcher reconstructs those same six values for the applicable wrapper and caller argument one and must preserve every empty projected position losslessly. Public caller argument one may be relative because the BAT or supported direct launcher resolves it against that launcher's invocation-start filesystem current working directory as part of the existing projection before child start. Transporting all six values does not give the projection operation authority. Collection ignores caller-derived positions four through six regardless of their values and always uses its fixed canonical source and output; no forwarded value selects an alternate collection path. Distribution treats positions four and five as compatibility-only launcher projections and position six as its sole operation input, requiring one nonempty fully qualified Windows-filesystem search-root projection. A raw relative child position six is not an alternate grammar and the child never resolves it. Capture the raw argument vector and provisionally bind only available positions as side-effect-free bootstrap, but only after the protected guards are live and the first cancellation fence passes does the script validate the exact count and operation-specific lexical shape, before any CommonModules domain filesystem observation. Fewer or more than six strings, or an empty or non-fully-qualified distribution position six, is one catchable usage failure with exit `1`, no retry, and no CommonModules source, repository, marker, lease, snapshot, workspace, candidate, or target observation or mutation; it emits no collection result, destination queue or row, count, aggregate, zero-target classification, or summary. A shape-valid distribution position six then undergoes the established protected search-root validation. No value in positions one through five can supply path, package, repository, candidate, output, cleanup, or recovery authority.

The CommonModules operation contract reserves entry-script-authored process exits `0`, `1`, and `130` for successful, failed, and cooperatively cancelled outcomes; a known abnormal terminator does not acquire outcome authority by selecting one of those numeric values. After observing termination of the exact PowerShell child, the BAT wrapper saves its complete 32-bit Windows process-exit status before any optional postlude and returns the same status without normalization. The supported direct child process has no wrapper normalization; absent known abnormal-termination evidence, its observer applies the same classification to the native PowerShell process exit. The numeric value `130` alone does not prove cooperative cancellation when external termination is known; contractual exit `130` requires the child-authored cancellation outcome below. Any other observed value, including `0xC000013A`, is an abnormal host or process termination rather than a collection or distribution outcome and is never presented as exit `1` or cooperative exit `130`; hexadecimal and signed-decimal renderings of the same 32-bit status are equivalent. Do not retry, fall back, synthesize a result row or summary, roll back, or infer cleanup, ownership release, current repository state, later-candidate state, or invocation completion from that value. Captured output follows the partial-transcript rule, and a later invocation starts from fresh collection snapshotting or distribution discovery and current on-disk evidence. Failure to resolve or start the fixed child remains the separate wrapper-level exit-`1` case.

The sole cooperative-cancellation ingress for these two CommonModules operations is a `CTRL_C_EVENT` (`Ctrl+C`) or `CTRL_BREAK_EVENT` (`Ctrl+Break`) that the exact PowerShell child itself receives and latches after its cancellation observer is live. A captured event is one idempotent request and then follows the established phase-specific cancellation, deferral, cleanup, result, and exit rules; the event alone neither proves that cancellation won nor forces exit `130`, and the BAT wrapper neither creates nor infers the request. No standard-input frame, cancellation file, named pipe, `vba-dev` channel, helper executable, or other out-of-band transport is added. A control event before the observer is live, an event the child does not conclusively capture, console or window closure, Task Manager or `TerminateProcess`, termination of only the BAT wrapper, or other host or process loss is abnormal loss rather than cooperative cancellation and never establishes exit `130`. Its observed native status follows the raw-exit rule, captured text follows the partial-transcript rule, and the next invocation uses current on-disk latest-wins authority. A supported direct caller has cooperative cancellation only when its fresh child can receive this console-control family and latch it; a redirected, detached, or otherwise non-console launch has no promised cooperative-cancellation channel. Pressing `Ctrl+C` during the BAT-owned post-result delay only interrupts out-of-band UI after the child outcome and does not alter that saved outcome; if the wrapper exit is not observed, the transcript remains partial.

The console-control callback has no transaction or output authority. For each event that it captures, it suppresses the default host termination, performs only one atomic idempotent write to the process-local cancellation latch, and returns. It never inspects or mutates the filesystem, opens, closes, acquires, or releases a repository, workspace, snapshot, target, handle, or lease, emits public output, waits, sleeps, retries, throws into the main operation, performs cleanup, or calls `exit`. Only the main operation observes the latch at explicit cancellation fences and applies the established phase-specific cleanup, deferral, result, ownership, and exit rules. Exactly two mutually exclusive terminal-specific boundary kinds are exceptions to ordinary cancellation-outcome processing: the exact `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe completion boundary and `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`. The former performs its existing exactly-once atomic latch read; the latter preserves an earlier explicit first-stop reason without reading the latch and performs an atomic exactly-once read only while that reason remains unselected. A path enters at most one boundary, and a capture-abnormal path enters neither. Each applies only its exact later-unstarted-label rule and invokes none of the ordinary phase-specific cancellation cleanup, current-result, diagnostic, output, or exit rules. Here, `diagnostic` and `output` rules mean snapshot-derived cancellation rules only. The `FileStreamConstructionFailed` boundary read neither suppresses nor creates its logical diagnostic slot and neither forces nor prevents its later one-shot materialization, pre-write `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite`, or other silent omission; `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` neither creates nor suppresses any diagnostic or output and cannot affect that materialization. A single operating-system or filesystem call already in progress is not asynchronously aborted merely because the latch changed; this observer adds no fixed `Ctrl+C`-to-exit or per-call deadline, while every previously established lease, output, and phase-specific deadline remains unchanged. If the child cannot capture the event and suppress default termination, the event establishes no cooperative request and any resulting termination follows the raw abnormal-exit contract.

Initial distribution screening never opens a `DistributionTransactionWorkspace` or recursively enumerates its subtree. It classifies only the locator's name, cardinality, entry type, root reparse state, and static physical-boundary facts; an exact locator that passes those checks remains an actionable repair candidate regardless of malformed, incomplete, contradictory, or reparse-bearing content later found inside it. After stable central-snapshot acceptance, the candidate's authoritative observation under its destination-slot lease recursively proves the complete workspace subtree contained and reparse-free before treating its content as disposable rather than as recovery authority. Distribution never follows a path read from an old record. A missing locator or an inability to establish whether it exists follows the warning-only no-row rule, while a conclusively present locator with unsafe or unproved type, root reparse, containment, or identity evidence is candidate-local `FAILED`; a central failure or cancellation before that turn leaves the workspace untouched and classifies the unstarted candidate by the applicable global or cancellation rule.

Before any collection workspace or output mutation, collection copies the canonical manifest, every manifest-listed source unit, and each selected listed-form sidecar into one invocation-owned raw-byte snapshot after proving unique resolution of every listed `ModuleFile`. It fully validates that fixed snapshot, then rereads only those live listed inputs and the evidence needed for listed resolution and compares their names, entry types, physical identities, and raw bytes. Any capture-time change, invalid package, or newly ambiguous listed resolution is a global exit-`1` failure that preserves the existing `common_modules_repo` and `CollectionTransactionWorkspace` and is not retried automatically. Unlisted source is outside both the content snapshot and this change comparison. Only after the stable comparison does that snapshot become the sole package and repair authority for the invocation; a later authoring edit belongs to the next collection invocation. Under the central-slot lease, collection may then validate and reset the proved safe workspace subtree, create and verify fresh staging inside it, and compare current New with either the proved zero-match absent state or the one accepted canonical root. Exact closed-inventory and raw-byte equality establishes eligibility for `UNCHANGED` before optional workspace cleanup; when the synchronous-observation rule below applies, every trailing same-handle observation must also succeed before that result commits. Otherwise publishing uses a rename swap rather than in-place updates: when one accepted invocation-start root exists, that root moves to a fresh current-attempt backup inside the workspace and staging moves to the canonical name; partial or foreign describes only the accepted root's generated descendants. From a proved zero-match state there is no target backup, and staging moves to the exact canonical name. The canonical path may therefore be briefly absent, but a mixed-generation package is never intentionally exposed. Cancellation before the first workspace mutation leaves the prior target and workspace untouched, cleans the current raw snapshot when conclusive, emits no collection result, and returns `130` only when no failure exists and ownership release is proved. After workspace reset or fresh staging begins but before either exact-current `UNCHANGED` is committed or the first destructive target mutation begins, cancellation does not reconstruct historical workspace contents. While retaining the central-slot lease, collection removes every safe disposable workspace child and then the exact workspace root, re-enumerates the direct siblings, and proves both that no basename matches `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.collect-workspace` under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` and that the canonical target exactly retains its invocation-start state, including absence. Only conclusive workspace-root absence, target equality, cleanup, and central ownership release returns `130` with no collection result; a retained or unproved locator, changed or unproved target, cleanup uncertainty, or ownership uncertainty returns exit `1`, reports the bounded retained evidence, and never becomes a pre-result warning. A non-cancellation operation failure established before the first workspace mutation likewise leaves the invocation-start target and every pre-existing workspace match unchanged, emits no collection result, and returns exit `1`. One established after workspace mutation but before exact-current `UNCHANGED` commitment or the first destructive target mutation uses the same central-lease-held cleanup without reconstructing historical workspace contents: collection removes the safe disposable subtree and exact root, proves zero case-insensitive locator matches and the exact invocation-start target state including absence, and then completes mandatory ownership release. Conclusive cleanup still preserves the original operation failure, emits no collection result, and returns exit `1`; any unproved root absence, target state, cleanup, or release retains bounded absolute paths and adds cleanup or ownership diagnostics plus manual guidance, never a warning-only result. A CommonModules marker follows its separate retained-marker warning rule only when the terminal same-handle observation and cleanup authority are conclusive, the one disposition attempt establishes an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admits that exact cause, and the still-unarmed handle is then conclusively released. An otherwise permitted non-authoritative raw snapshot remains governed by its separate warning rule. Once exact-current `UNCHANGED` is committed, later cancellation never enters this pre-result cleanup: it preserves the factual result, may stop optional cleanup only after a retained safe workspace is classified under the post-result warning boundary, and still completes mandatory ownership release. Once the first destructive target mutation begins, cancellation is latched until current New is verified or the invocation-start target state, including absence, is conclusively restored. Verified New remains `SUCCEEDED` with exit `0` and a deferred-cancellation warning; committed `UNCHANGED` likewise remains exit `0`. A controlled commit failure first attempts restoration from the fresh current-attempt backup. If exact restoration of the invocation-start target, including absence, cannot be proved, collection retains the workspace and backup evidence without deleting them, emits no collection result, returns exit `1`, and reports bounded absolute paths with manual guidance. After exact restoration is proved, and while the central-slot lease remains held, collection removes every safe disposable workspace child and then the exact root, re-enumerates direct siblings to prove zero `OrdinalIgnoreCase` locator matches, re-proves the restored target state, and completes mandatory ownership release. Conclusive post-restoration cleanup still emits no collection result and returns the original operation failure with exit `1`; any unproved root absence, target restoration, cleanup, or release retains bounded absolute paths and adds cleanup or ownership diagnostics plus manual guidance, never a warning-only result. Once current New is result-committed, it remains the factual result, and inability to remove only a still-proved non-authoritative safe workspace follows the cleanup-warning boundary. `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` is the overriding exception: after it latches, collection performs no restoration, rollback, workspace cleanup, marker cleanup, or other CommonModules filesystem mutation.

Collection handles process loss through current-source latest-wins repair rather than historical Old/New recovery. A later invocation first acquires the central slot and, without modifying either the canonical target or an existing `CollectionTransactionWorkspace`, captures, fully validates, and live-rechecks one stable snapshot of the current manifest-listed authoring package. If that snapshot cannot be accepted, collection preserves the target and workspace unchanged and fails. Once accepted, the snapshot is the sole authority: collection may discard the complete proved safe workspace subtree without parsing prior contents, create fresh current-attempt state inside it, and replace or recreate the wholly generated canonical target even when it is absent, partial, foreign, or an older valid package. Prior record syntax, phase, inventories, fingerprints, transaction identity, stored paths, internal names, and contradictions never select Old or New and never block repair inside the disposable boundary. A case-only or multiple workspace match, non-directory entry, reparse root or descendant, escaping boundary, or unproved containment or ownership remains untouched and fails before mutation. An abrupt loss leaves the canonical target, the fixed workspace locator, or both; the next invocation repeats the same stable-current-source admission and latest-wins sequence rather than interpreting historical evidence or timestamps.

Distribution processes the fixed lexical candidate paths sequentially and gives every attempted path its own ordinary result without physically deduplicating aliases. When a candidate reaches its turn, an absent or unprovable physical parent adds the path to the aggregate disappearance warning, emits no target row, and moves to the next candidate. Otherwise distribution proves the current parent, derives the prospective repository transaction slot from that physical parent plus the exact `common_modules_repo` child name, and acquires that slot's fixed sibling lease before making the turn's authoritative marker-and-workspace observation. Under the lease it rechecks physical disjointness from the central repository boundary and the absence of a reparse entry in both the target and workspace boundaries. The initial queue retains only the lexical path: its earlier physical observation is not a version token or pinned destination identity. If the path now reaches a different ordinary, reparse-free slot that remains physically disjoint from central and has valid current marker or workspace repair authority, distribution processes that current slot without warning and does not chase the former physical directory or artifacts no longer reachable through the candidate path. Acquiring the destination-slot lease then pins that proved physical slot for this one candidate attempt. Distribution re-proves that the lexical path still reaches the leased slot after acquisition and before the first workspace-reseed or current-transaction filesystem mutation. If it has switched to another slot before that mutation, distribution releases the lease, emits the aggregate disappearance warning with no target row, and neither follows nor retries the new destination during this invocation. Once a workspace-reseed or current-transaction filesystem mutation begins, the attempt remains bound to that leased slot; a later path change never retargets it, and the attempt ends there with verified current New, exact restoration, or a bounded evidence-preserving `FAILED` outcome. If both the marker and fixed workspace are absent, their presence cannot be determined, or the observed evidence disappears before the first such mutation, distribution emits the same warning-only result without polling, retrying, rediscovering, or attributing a cause; a later appearance belongs to the next invocation, and the skip does not retroactively create a zero-target error. Every pre-mutation warning-skip after destination lease acquisition also requires conclusive OS handle release. If that release cannot be proved, distribution keeps the disappearance warning and no target row, performs no rollback, retarget, retry, or synthetic `FAILED`, emits one invocation-level destination-ownership failure, stops target processing, marks every remaining unstarted candidate `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`, and returns exit `1`. The preceding immediate `GLOBAL FAILURE` label does not apply at a non-FCF conclusive normal `false` `CloseHandle` return: after the required capture, `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` alone preserves an earlier explicit label or, only when none exists, supplies the later-unstarted labels. If the terminal same-handle observation and cleanup authority are conclusive, the one disposition attempt establishes an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admits that exact cause, and the still-unarmed handle is then conclusively released, the retained marker is warning-only and later candidates may continue. A pre-existing safe `DistributionTransactionWorkspace` is temporary repair authority for its one destination slot and never a historical-record hard gate. Under the pinned destination lease, distribution treats every ordinary, contained, reparse-free workspace child—including a missing, malformed, unknown, or contradictory record and stale staging or backup—as disposable, ignores every path value it contains, and uses only the accepted current central snapshot to reseed the workspace and target. It durably establishes a verified create-new staging generation while retaining the fixed workspace locator before the first destructive target mutation, then atomically replaces the one accepted canonical root or recreates it only from a proved zero-match state under the accepted workspace repair authority; partial, foreign, old, or otherwise nonmatching describes only generated descendants inside an accepted root. An exact-current comparison establishes eligibility for `UNCHANGED` before workspace cleanup; when the synchronous-observation rule below applies, successful trailing same-handle observations establish only final eligibility for the immediately following phase-specific cancellation fence, and the result commits only if cancellation does not win there. After verified current New or that committed `UNCHANGED` result, distribution cleans the workspace. Before destination result commitment, failure to prove or mutate only the reserved workspace or canonical target safely makes that candidate `FAILED`, but record syntax, phase, fingerprint, and internal evidence disagreement never do. A non-cancellation operation failure established before the first workspace mutation leaves the invocation-start target and workspace unchanged. One established after workspace mutation but before either exact-current `UNCHANGED` commitment or the first destructive target mutation retains the destination-slot lease, does not reconstruct historical workspace contents, and removes every safe disposable workspace child. When the invocation-start target existed, distribution also removes the exact workspace root and proves zero `OrdinalIgnoreCase` workspace-locator matches; when that target was absent, it retains exactly one ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse empty workspace locator as temporary repair authority and proves that no additional case-insensitive match exists. It proves the exact invocation-start target state and resulting workspace boundary. Conclusive cleanup still leaves one `FAILED` row and exit `1` for the original failure. An unproved target state, resulting workspace state or boundary, or filesystem cleanup outcome adds bounded retained paths and manual guidance to that same failure and is never warning-only; after conclusive release of the destination lease and every candidate-opened target, workspace, staging, backup, rollback, or cleanup handle, later candidates continue. Within this pre-result operation-failure branch, conclusively classified release uncertainty stops later candidates; a valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only the label preserved or selected by its dedicated safe completion boundary, the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path uses only the label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, a capture-abnormal path never reaches this result rule, and every other conclusively classified path that reaches this result rule uses `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. After the first destructive target mutation, a non-cancellation operation failure first accepts verified current New as the factual `SUCCEEDED` result and uses post-result cleanup. Otherwise distribution attempts exact restoration of the invocation-start target state, including absence, from current-attempt evidence. If restoration cannot be proved, it retains the workspace and backup without deleting either, keeps one `FAILED` row and exit `1`, and reports bounded retained paths with manual guidance. After exact restoration, it removes every safe disposable workspace child; when the invocation-start target existed, it also removes the exact root and proves zero `OrdinalIgnoreCase` workspace-locator matches, while an initially absent target retains exactly one ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse empty locator with no additional case-insensitive match. It re-proves the restored target and resulting workspace boundary. An unproved restoration, resulting workspace state or boundary, or filesystem cleanup outcome adds diagnostics and manual guidance to the same `FAILED` result and is never warning-only. After conclusive release of the destination lease and every candidate-opened target, workspace, staging, backup, rollback, or cleanup handle, later candidates continue. Conclusively classified release uncertainty preserves the one row and stops later candidates; a valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only the label preserved or selected by its dedicated safe completion boundary, the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path uses only the label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, a capture-abnormal path never reaches this result rule, and every other conclusively classified path that reaches this result rule uses `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. After verified current New or committed `UNCHANGED`, a proved retained non-authoritative workspace follows the cleanup-warning rule; an unproved boundary, reparse entry, containment, identity, cleanup, or ownership outcome preserves the factual row, adds the established separate failure, and stops under the destination-release rule without following a stored path or deleting outside the workspace. Each attempted destination owns an independent staging, rename-swap, rollback, and latest-wins reseed boundary. An ordinary target-local failure affects only its own pinned slot: it accepts verified current New, exactly restores the invocation-start target, or retains bounded workspace and backup evidence when restoration is unproved. It never rolls back an earlier completed destination and, after conclusive release of every candidate-opened handle, does not prevent later candidate paths from being attempted. Abrupt loss leaves either the canonical target or the fixed workspace locator, so the next invocation captures its own current central snapshot and repeats latest-wins reseeding instead of interpreting prior record bytes. Final destination-slot ownership uncertainty is one exception: after any factual `SUCCEEDED`, `UNCHANGED`, or already established `FAILED` result it preserves exactly that one row and repository outcome, retains the original failure diagnostic when present, adds one ownership failure without a second `FAILED` row or count, stops target processing, classifies every remaining unstarted candidate by the first-stop rule, and returns exit `1`. The destination marker-disposition internal stop defined below is a separate invocation-global exception even when its mandatory sole native `CloseHandle` returns nonzero; it uses its own result and internal-diagnostic matrix instead of continuing to later destinations. A prior replace-all may remove a later nested candidate path or workspace because distribution deliberately performs no pairwise containment analysis; the later path receives only the established disappearance warning and is ignored. Exact physical aliases instead converge on the same slot protocol and fixed workspace. They strongly serialize only on conclusively non-remote backing; remote or unclassifiable aliases share the point-in-time best-effort protocol, so absent an undetected remote ownership break the first attempt may be `SUCCEEDED` and a later lexical alias `UNCHANGED`. No cross-project all-old/all-new generation is promised. `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` is the overriding exception to every restoration and cleanup branch in this paragraph: after it latches, distribution performs no further CommonModules filesystem mutation and starts no later candidate.

Distribution orders its lexically distinct candidate paths by normalized absolute path under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` and then `Ordinal`, does not deduplicate physical aliases, and processes them sequentially. It reports an applicable attempted candidate exactly once as `succeeded`, `unchanged`, or `failed`; a candidate warning-skipped because its discovered path disappeared has no terminal target row. An ordinary target failure records `failed` and, when destination ownership release is conclusive, does not stop later candidates. For conclusively classified release uncertainty, a valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only its dedicated-boundary label, the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path uses only the label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, a capture-abnormal path reaches no result rule, and every other conclusively classified path that reaches this result rule uses `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. A non-cancellation failure after workspace mutation but before either exact-current `UNCHANGED` commitment or the first destructive target mutation removes the workspace root when the invocation-start target existed or retains only an exact ordinary empty locator when it was absent, preserves its `failed` result and exit `1`, and continues later candidates after conclusive release. After destructive target mutation, a non-cancellation operation failure instead accepts verified current New as `succeeded` or attempts exact restoration. Unproved restoration retains workspace and backup evidence; exact restoration uses the same target-existence-dependent root-or-empty-locator postcondition. Either failure remains one `failed` row and exit `1`, and later candidates continue only after conclusive release of the destination lease and every candidate-opened target, workspace, staging, backup, rollback, or cleanup handle. Any conclusively classified release uncertainty in this branch uses that same terminal-specific rule. Cooperative cancellation observed before a later candidate starts, or while waiting for that candidate's destination-slot lease, performs no further marker-or-workspace inspection and marks the current and every later initial candidate not already completed or warning-skipped as `not attempted (cancelled)`. After the post-acquisition cancellation fence has admitted repository inspection and the invocation-start target and workspace states are established, cancellation observed before either exact-current `UNCHANGED` is committed or the current candidate's first destructive target mutation enters one `PreTargetCancellationCleanup` boundary. Before any workspace mutation, that cleanup leaves the target and workspace unchanged. Once workspace reset or fresh staging has begun, it does not reconstruct historical workspace contents: while the lease remains held it removes conclusively owned current-attempt staging and every safe disposable workspace child, then leaves the exact empty workspace locator when the invocation-start target was absent or may remove the workspace entirely when that target was present.
