It proves the canonical target's exact invocation-start state—including absence—together with a conclusive resulting workspace state and boundary, then proves destination ownership release. Only then does it make the current and later initial candidates `not attempted (cancelled)` and return `130` unless an earlier failure wins. Unproved cleanup, invocation-start state, workspace state or boundary, or ownership makes the current candidate `failed`, leaves later candidates `not attempted (cancelled)`, and returns exit `1`; a transient deletion error that final re-observation proves harmless does not count as this failure. Once exact-current `UNCHANGED` is committed, later cancellation during workspace cleanup or destination ownership release never reclassifies that factual row. It may stop optional cleanup after a proved retained workspace is classified under the cleanup warning and must still complete mandatory ownership release. Every later unstarted candidate becomes `not attempted (cancelled)` and the invocation returns `130`; when no later candidate remains, the late request preserves ordinary exit `0`. Workspace-boundary or release uncertainty preserves the `unchanged` row but adds the established ownership failure and returns exit `1`. Cancellation after the first destructive target mutation is latched until the accepted current New is verified, the invocation-start target state is conclusively restored, or an operation failure is conclusively classified with unproved restoration and bounded evidence preserved. Verified New makes the current candidate `succeeded` with a deferred-cancellation warning. An operation failure remains `failed`; conclusive restoration uses the same post-target target-existence-dependent workspace cleanup, while unproved restoration retains workspace and backup evidence. Any later candidates are `not attempted (cancelled)` under the established first-stop rule. Earlier completed targets are never rolled back. Repeated cancellation is idempotent, while abrupt host or process loss leaves the fixed workspace locator for a later latest-wins attempt instead of becoming historical Old/New recovery. Any failed or outcome-uncertain candidate wins over concurrent cancellation and returns exit `1`; otherwise cancellation that leaves at least one initial candidate uncompleted returns `130`. A request arriving after every initial candidate already has a final outcome does not replace the ordinary exit `0` or `1` result. `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` overrides every cancellation cleanup, restoration, and workspace-cleanup step in this paragraph; after it latches, those mutations are not attempted.

When cancellation and destination ownership failure could both explain why later candidates were not started, the first established stop cause fixes each row's reason. Cancellation observed or latched first preserves `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)` even when later ownership uncertainty makes the invocation exit `1`; destination ownership failure established first preserves `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)` despite a later cancellation. One narrow exception applies outside the valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` partial branch: a conclusive normal `false` `CloseHandle` return fixes failed release, continuity, and exit `1` immediately but deliberately does not fix a later-destination reason; after the required capture, `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` preserves an earlier explicit reason without reading the latch, or, only if no reason exists, reads it exactly once and supplies `CANCELLED` when set or `GLOBAL FAILURE` when clear. Neither case rewrites the current candidate's factual row or warning-only no-row classification. Failure still takes precedence for the process exit. Because unproved OS release is outside the cleanup-only success boundary, every retained path belongs to the ownership diagnostic and does not produce a separate success warning.

When one candidate has both a distinct workspace, transaction, or cleanup failure and a later destination-ownership failure, its logical stderr diagnostic group preserves the original failure block first and adds exactly one ownership block immediately afterward within the immutable diagnostic core. An original failure that already represents the same release or ownership failure, and multiple release exceptions for that one condition, are aggregated instead of duplicated. Conclusive workspace reseeding or supersession information remains a count-free stdout informational line; an uncertain outcome emits no success information. Retained paths that no longer meet the warning-only cleanup boundary belong to the group's mutable artifact annex, appear there in stable order, and never also produce a cleanup success warning. The core and annex follow the scope-final emission rule below, so they need not be contiguous in the physical stderr stream. Ordering is deterministic within each stream, while no total interleaving order between stdout and stderr is part of the human-text contract.

For collection before repository result commitment, an original operation failure accompanied by restoration or workspace-cleanup failure and central ownership or release failure produces one stderr diagnostic group. The original failure block remains first, at most one distinct restoration/workspace-cleanup block follows, and at most one distinct central-ownership block follows last. A cause already represented by an earlier block, including multiple exceptions for one condition, is aggregated rather than repeated. The group lists every retained absolute path once in the existing stable path order and ends with manual guidance. It emits no collection result or synthetic `FAILED` row, never adds a cleanup-success warning, and returns exit `1`. Ordering is deterministic within stderr, while no total interleaving order with stdout is part of the human-text contract.

After a distribution candidate begins its first workspace-reseed or current-transaction filesystem mutation, its fixed lexical candidate path remains the deterministic attempt label even if an ancestor alias later retargets; the factual result still describes the pinned physical slot, and distribution emits one candidate-scoped identity warning that the label no longer resolves there. A retained-path list includes only absolute locators whose final observation, made while pinned slot and handle evidence remains available, re-proves the same retained artifact identity inside that old slot or workspace. A last-known or candidate locator that cannot be re-proved appears only in a separate `UNVERIFIED — DO NOT ACT ON THIS PATH` section; distribution never substitutes its current foreign resolution, and no displayed string grants delete, move, rerun, or recovery authority. Manual action requires an independent identity check. When retained evidence is bounded but no safe current locator can be proved, distribution prints no guessed artifact list and states that the retained evidence may not be safely path-addressable. These presentation rules do not change result, exit, continuation, cancellation, or release semantics. An unverified locator never satisfies the unchanged-identity requirement for warning-only cleanup.

Lexically distinct aliases that reach one physical destination slot remain independent attempts, so each terminal row, count, order position, repository outcome, and original operation-failure block remains fixed; an earlier `FAILED` attempt continues to require exit `1` even when a later alias repairs the same slot. Before the remaining buffered artifact diagnostics are emitted, distribution instead finalizes retained-artifact claims, retained-path and manual-cleanup guidance, and cleanup-only warnings at invocation end against physical artifact identity rather than path spelling. If a later alias conclusively proves that its latest-wins reseed removed or safely superseded one or more exact artifact identities from an earlier attempt, distribution removes only those identities from the earlier retained set and never transfers a claim to a later artifact merely because the absolute path text is identical. It omits the earlier artifact-specific retained paths, manual-cleanup guidance, or cleanup warning only when no unresolved identity supporting that text remains, and emits exactly one candidate-scoped, count-free workspace-supersession informational line for that resolved earlier claim; the later attempt keeps its own independent row and immutable diagnostic core. Every identity whose removal or supersession cannot be proved remains under the verified-path and `UNVERIFIED` rules above, and no supersession information is emitted when none can be proved. Conclusive later cleanup of an earlier cleanup-only warning removes only that stale warning and does not change the otherwise ordinary exit; conclusive supersession of evidence from an earlier failed attempt likewise never erases that failure or exit `1`. This finalization neither deduplicates, reorders, retries, nor retargets candidates and does not change result counts, cancellation, continuation, ownership, or release rules.

Collection and distribution apply the same identity-safe presentation to invocation-scoped cleanup artifacts outside a destination retained set, including a collection raw-source snapshot, a distribution central snapshot, and a marker retained after a conclusive unarmed central- or destination-slot release because its disposition step established an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`. Immediately before the remaining buffered cleanup diagnostics are emitted, one nonmutating, no-wait, no-retry read-only observation of each cleanup locator neither reacquires ownership nor follows a reparse point or current foreign resolution. Only a normalized absolute locator re-proved to identify the same ordinary non-reparse artifact inside its established physical boundary appears as a `VERIFIED` retained path. Conclusive absence, replacement by another identity, or reuse of a released marker by a later owner—even when that owner reuses the same file identity—removes only the stale retained claim and its cleanup warning when no other unresolved artifact supports that warning; the current entry is untouched. If the locator relationship cannot be proved, the last-known locator appears only as `UNVERIFIED — DO NOT ACT ON THIS PATH`, no alternate or guessed locator is substituted, and manual action requires an independent immediate re-proof of ordinary type, physical identity, boundary, and containment. These final presentation observations never retroactively change the established result or exit threshold: ownership, boundary, required-handle release, or cleanup identity that was unproved when classified remains failure, while an artifact already proved non-authoritative and cleanup-only keeps its established warning or failure classification when only its final locator becomes unverified. Every displayed path is descriptive rather than move, delete, retry, or recovery authority. `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` is the exception: an affected locator receives no final filesystem observation and appears only as an `UNVERIFIED — DO NOT ACT ON THIS PATH` last-known locator under the fail-stop rule below.

Once the terminal collection repository outcome or every applicable distribution candidate outcome is fixed, cancellation never interrupts the bounded terminal identity finalization above. Collection or distribution completes in-memory same-slot alias artifact correlation from already captured evidence, each required one-shot read-only locator observation, stale-warning suppression, and `VERIFIED` or `UNVERIFIED` classification, then attempts deterministic rendering of the remaining buffered artifact annexes and invocation-wide diagnostics only until an `OutputTransportFailure` is latched under the rule below. It starts no filesystem mutation, another deletion or cleanup retry, lease acquisition, repository or content inspection beyond those observations, or retarget; its only new wait is the bounded output handoff below. Cancellation itself is not identity evidence and never makes an otherwise unobserved locator `UNVERIFIED`; the actual final observation alone supplies the established conclusive or unproved classification. Optional deletion retries for non-marker artifacts may stop once an artifact conclusively satisfies the cleanup-only warning boundary; CommonModules marker disposition is one-shot and never participates in those retries. The internal terminal proof pass still completes, and rendering remains subject to the output-failure rule. A cancellation first observed after all applicable outcomes are terminal adds no result, warning, or informational line solely because of cancellation and does not replace the ordinary exit `0` or an existing exit `1`; an earlier cancellation that already fixed `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)` rows and exit `130` remains fixed unless an independently established failure requires exit `1`. The `FileStreamConstructionFailed` boundary snapshot likewise is never rendered independently: it supplies only an existing `NOT ATTEMPTED` reason label for later unstarted destinations and creates no cancellation diagnostic or other presentation for a central slot or a last destination with none. `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` likewise renders neither its conditional snapshot nor the absence of one: only already frozen later-unstarted `NOT ATTEMPTED` labels may appear, and a central slot or last destination has no snapshot-derived presentation. No later renderer, classifier, diagnostic, result path, or exit path consumes either boundary snapshot or rereads the live latch. The logical path-free `FileStreamConstructionFailed` internal diagnostic is separate from that snapshot rule: after its safe boundary and classifications close, perform its one-shot materialization and handoff state machine; render the whole item exactly once only after terminal `FcfOutputCompleted`, render nothing after catchable assembly failure or `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite`, and never retry, synthesize, augment, or repeat it during identity or artifact finalization. The separate path-free `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` diagnostic likewise uses only its already-fixed one-line materialization and ordinary bounded handoff authority; terminal identity or artifact finalization never reprojects a field, appends a locator, retries, augments, or repeats it. `FcfOutputTransportFailed` remains subject to the ordinary advisory partial-transcript and `OutputTransportFailure` rules. Repeated cancellation is idempotent. A real ownership, required-release, boundary, or outcome-classification failure found during mandatory finalization remains the established exit-`1` failure and is never hidden by cancellation, while presentation-only locator uncertainty retains the previously established warning or failure classification under the rule above.

Distribution emits each piece of terminal output at the earliest scope that makes it immutable instead of buffering all terminal text until invocation end. Candidate paths are processed in fixed lexical order; once one candidate's repository outcome and the release classification of every handle opened for that attempt are conclusive, and before the next candidate starts, distribution emits that candidate's terminal row and immutable candidate-local diagnostic core. The row uses only that candidate's discovery-frozen lexical path and its frozen escaped representation; same-slot alias correlation, physical identity, locator finalization, or later spelling never rewrites, deduplicates, retracts, or supplements it. Collection selects one of its two fixed path-free rows only after its repository outcome is factual. Materialize each whole row exactly once before its ordinary bounded stdout handoff; a catchable formatting or whole-line assembly failure establishes the existing `OutputTransportFailure` with no partial row, fallback, retry, or alternate stream, while materialization nonreturn or unknown completion reaches no handoff or later action. Successful materialization hands the same immutable string reference once, and only a newline-complete successful handoff exposes the row. The core includes any original operation failure, destination ownership or release failure, fixed lexical-path identity warning, and other fact that no later alias can retract. Create the logical ownership-diagnostic slot when the continuity latch becomes conclusive, but materialize its fixed one-line form only after the applicable safe-completion or terminal release boundary and all fields and classifications are immutable; hand it off once at that earliest immutable candidate or invocation scope. When it coexists with a logical `FileStreamConstructionFailed` diagnostic, preserve the original-failure-first and ownership-second stderr ordinals above; omission of either item creates no placeholder and does not suppress, reorder, or synthesize the other. Do not emit the `FILE_STREAM_CONSTRUCTION` entry as an early candidate-local diagnostic core. Buffer the one logical invocation-wide `FileStreamConstructionFailed` diagnostic until cleanup and release classification plus its safe boundary and result matrix fix the authorized entry set, then perform the one-shot in-memory materialization with no sink authority. A normal assembly return publishes the same immutable string reference into fresh `FcfOutputNotWriteStarted`; terminal `FcfOutputCompleted` renders it exactly once, catchable assembly failure or `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` renders nothing, and terminal finalization never retries, synthesizes, or repeats the item. When cancellation or a global stop conclusively fixes several remaining candidates as `NOT ATTEMPTED`, distribution emits those rows immediately in their existing lexical order. An aggregate is likewise emitted as soon as its complete scope is closed. Only output that later evidence can still change remains buffered: retained-artifact paths, artifact-specific manual-cleanup guidance, cleanup-only warnings, candidate-scoped workspace-supersession information, an aggregate whose scope is still open, invocation-wide central-snapshot or retained-marker disposition-warning diagnostics, and the final summary. Exactly one distribution summary is authorized only after an ordered queue is finalized and every queued candidate has a conclusive result-row or warning-only no-row classification. Initial zero-target configuration failure, failure or cancellation before queue finalization, every central or pre-queue no-result branch, and every stronger no-later-action branch admit no count-tuple finalization and emit no summary. After terminal identity finalization fixes every count input, the main flow consumes the preprepared count-finalization boundary exactly once, even when an earlier `OutputTransportFailure` has already closed ordinary rendering. Only its conclusive normal valid outcome freezes the immutable ordered tuple above. Its conclusive normal failed outcome establishes the invocation-global internal result-finalization stop, preserves the frozen rows, warning-only no-row classifications, reasons, continuity state, count inputs, and repository, emits no summary, starts no summary materialization or handoff, and returns exit `1`. That stop suppresses only the summary and does not retract or replace independently authorized rows, diagnostics, warnings, aggregates, or annexes or by itself close ordinary rendering; it neither establishes, clears, nor replaces `OutputTransportFailure`, and any independently established earlier or later output failure remains controlling for its own scope. A binder or outer-call failure, an exception escaping the boundary, boundary nonreturn, or unknown or unproved completion establishes no tuple or result-finalization stop and permits no summary, handoff, authoritative exit, or later action. Only the conclusive normal valid tuple outcome while ordinary rendering remains open authorizes the whole fixed summary to be materialized exactly once and the same immutable string reference handed to the ordinary bounded stdout handoff as the last ordinary PowerShell stdout item, after all applicable result rows. This includes screening-only completion with at least one established `FAILED` row and late completion in which every initially actionable candidate became warning-skipped; the latter requires the valid exact all-zero tuple and uses the exact all-zero form. An earlier `OutputTransportFailure` still permits the required in-memory boundary to reach its conclusive valid or failed outcome but starts no summary materialization or handoff. Terminal identity finalization emits only the surviving buffered annex under its original candidate or invocation scope and never repeats, retracts, or corrects an already emitted immutable row or core diagnostic. Collection may retain its single terminal result and diagnostics until its own finalization. Each stream remains deterministic, but no total stdout/stderr interleaving is promised.

A catchable write or flush failure while the PowerShell collection or distribution operation emits public terminal text latches one invocation-global `OutputTransportFailure`; it is neither cancellation nor a repository outcome. For the `FileStreamConstructionFailed` item, catchable field projection, formatting, whole-item assembly failure, pre-publication preparation failure, and conclusive `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` all occur before output authority and never establish `OutputTransportFailure`; only a catchable failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion after conclusive transition to `FcfOutputWriteInFlight`, `FcfOutputWriteReturned`, or `FcfOutputFlushInFlight` uses this rule. `FcfOutputCompleted` is success, while a worker still in `FcfOutputNotWriteStarted` at the deadline is atomically abandoned without transport failure and can never issue a late write. For the ownership diagnostic, catchable formatting or whole-line assembly failure occurs before handoff and silently omits only that item without `OutputTransportFailure`; once its ordinary bounded handoff is attempted with the whole immutable string, any catchable preparation, admission, queue, start, write, or required-flush failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion uses this `OutputTransportFailure` rule. An already committed collection result and every conclusive distribution candidate row and repository state remain factual, are never rolled back or rewritten as `FAILED`, and create no synthetic result. After the failure is latched, the command starts no new candidate lease, repository inspection, or filesystem mutation. Every remaining distribution candidate not already fixed becomes internally `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)` when output failure is the first stop cause; an earlier cancellation or global stop keeps its established reason, while output failure still forces exit `1`. Both commands complete mandatory release of every open handle and lease, snapshot and transaction-state classification, and the bounded terminal identity finalization before returning. The first failure stops ordinary terminal rendering on both streams. The failed stream is never retried because a partial write could otherwise duplicate text, and an item assigned to one stream is never resent through the other. Only after the first `OutputTransportFailure` has stopped ordinary rendering, mandatory handle or lease release, snapshot or transaction-state classification, and terminal identity finalization are complete, and any admitted required count-finalization boundary has reached a conclusive normal valid or failed outcome may the invocation make the sole final-output exception: one best-effort stderr diagnostic attempt when the first failed stream was stdout and no stderr failure is already conclusive. Its exact path-free, case-sensitive, CR/LF-free human-text form is the fixed literal `OutputTransportFailure: stream=STDOUT`; it has no optional field and includes no phase, exception, message, path, item identity, timeout value, byte count, cancellation state, or reconstructed evidence. Fix one private monotonic diagnostic state initially `OutputTransportDiagnosticNotAttempted`. If the first failed stream was stderr, both streams failed, stderr failure is already conclusive, or diagnostic eligibility is conclusively absent or unproved before materialization starts, change it once to absorbing terminal `OutputTransportDiagnosticSuppressed` and issue no diagnostic write. A conclusively returned catchable whole-line materialization failure makes the same transition without fallback or retry. If materialization is in flight, nonreturning, or of unknown or unproved completion, establish no diagnostic terminal or handoff and perform no later action from that in-flight materialization. Only a conclusive normal materialization return may change `OutputTransportDiagnosticNotAttempted` to `OutputTransportDiagnosticAttemptInFlight`, start one fixed 30-second deadline, and permit the sequence `OutputTransportDiagnosticWriteInFlight`, `OutputTransportDiagnosticWriteReturned`, `OutputTransportDiagnosticFlushInFlight`, terminal `OutputTransportDiagnosticCompleted`; each in-flight transition occurs immediately before its exact stderr `Write` or required `Flush` with no intervening fallible action. A catchable preparation, admission, queue, start, write, or flush failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion in any attempt state atomically fixes absorbing terminal `OutputTransportDiagnosticFailed`; the deadline controller makes every delayed transition fail, a late write return cannot start flush, and later completion is ignored. `OutputTransportDiagnosticFailed` never recursively establishes another `OutputTransportFailure`, diagnostic, warning, result, reason, count, summary, notification, output attempt, or exit change. Never retry, rebuild, resend, switch to stdout, or append a second line. `Completed`, `Failed`, and `Suppressed` are presentation-only terminal outcomes; the original `OutputTransportFailure`, frozen repository and candidate outcomes, first-stop reason, continuity state, and exit `1` remain controlling. Concurrently observed write failures aggregate into the same condition. The distribution count-finalization boundary precedes summary and output authority. Its conclusive normal failed outcome uses the invocation-global internal result-finalization stop above, emits no summary, and never itself establishes, clears, or replaces `OutputTransportFailure`; any independently established earlier or later output failure retains its existing rule. Only after the boundary returns conclusively and normally with the valid immutable tuple may a catchable exact tuple-member read or projection failure, invariant-ASCII-decimal formatting failure, or whole-line assembly failure before handoff establish this same `OutputTransportFailure`; summary materialization nonreturn or unknown or unproved completion reaches no handoff or later action. After the whole immutable summary enters its ordinary bounded stdout handoff, every preparation-through-required-flush failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion follows this rule. Emit no fallback, partial replacement, retry, rebuilt summary, or alternate-stream copy. Any such output-stage summary failure changes only the invocation exit to `1` and leaves every factual row, no-row classification, validated tuple, count input, and repository unchanged. Successful local write or flush means only that the local output API accepted the bytes, not that a downstream reader received or durably stored them. An uncatchable host or process loss remains the existing crash case rather than this cooperative output-failure path.

No. Give each PowerShell operation terminal item's write and required flush one fixed, non-configurable 30-second completion deadline through a worker that owns only immutable text and no repository, snapshot, workspace, candidate-opened filesystem handle, lease, mutation, or result authority. For a successfully assembled `FileStreamConstructionFailed` item, publish one fresh private monotonic handoff state as `FcfOutputNotWriteStarted` with the exact same immutable `System.String` reference and start that deadline before any worker admission, queue, or start attempt. The worker receives no exception reference, holder, field object, or materialization authority and never clones, serializes, reformats, rebuilds, or substitutes the text. Its atomic `FcfOutputNotWriteStarted` to `FcfOutputWriteInFlight` transition immediately before exact stderr `Write` is the sole output-admission boundary. Before that transition, a conclusive catchable preparation, admission, queue, or start failure changes the state to absorbing `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite`; if the deadline expires while the state is still exactly `FcfOutputNotWriteStarted`, the deadline controller performs the same atomic transition. Either case silently omits the item, starts no sink call, latches no `OutputTransportFailure`, and prevents a delayed worker from transitioning or writing. After admission, the only normal sequence is `FcfOutputWriteInFlight` to `FcfOutputWriteReturned`, then `FcfOutputFlushInFlight`, then terminal `FcfOutputCompleted`; the transitions into the two in-flight states occur immediately before their exact sink calls, with no intervening fallible operation. Catchable failure or deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion in any admitted nonterminal state fixes absorbing `FcfOutputTransportFailed` and the existing `OutputTransportFailure`. When the deadline wins during `FcfOutputWriteInFlight`, a later write return cannot start the required flush; when it wins during `FcfOutputWriteReturned`, the worker cannot begin a late flush; and when it wins during `FcfOutputFlushInFlight`, later completion is ignored. A timed-out admitted call may already have delivered no, partial, or complete bytes; infer none, accept no late success, retry nothing, and abandon that stream. Conclusive `FcfOutputCompleted` by the deadline alone is transport success and requires no downstream receipt or durability acknowledgement. Catchable assembly failure creates no handoff state or deadline; `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` ends the FCF-specific deadline without changing result, reason, continuity, diagnostic count, or exit and does not stop other independently authorized output. This pre-write silent-abandonment rule is the sole `FileStreamConstructionFailed` exception to the ordinary rule that an admitted or post-write incomplete handoff is `OutputTransportFailure`. Observe cancellation separately. Cancellation latched first keeps remaining candidate reasons `CANCELLED`, but conclusive `FcfOutputTransportFailed` still forces exit `1`; without an earlier stop, it fixes `GLOBAL FAILURE`. At the deadline observation, re-observe cancellation before fixing that first-stop reason, while timely `FcfOutputCompleted` avoids output failure and pre-write `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` creates no new stop reason. Apply the existing bounded handoff to every other terminal item; their failure or deadline retains the existing output-failure rule. The best-effort `OutputTransportFailure: stream=STDOUT` diagnostic instead uses only its closed `OutputTransportDiagnostic*` state machine above: its attempt starts a fresh 30-second deadline, any preparation-through-flush failure or unproved completion ends in `OutputTransportDiagnosticFailed`, and that absorbing terminal never invokes the ordinary output-failure rule recursively or authorizes another write. The `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` line is one such ordinary stderr item: successful materialization supplies the same immutable `System.String` reference exactly once, starts one ordinary 30-second handoff, and has no `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` or other silent pre-write worker terminal; any failure or unproved completion after handoff attempt is `OutputTransportFailure`, with no retry or alternate stream. Direct-console-only BAT UI remains outside this worker and failure classification.

An uncatchable host or process loss may leave only a partial human transcript. In an abnormal transcript, only a newline-complete single-line terminal result row actually captured in full and emitted in one of the exact closed forms defined by this ADR remains a historical receipt of the candidate classification or collection repository outcome at that emission boundary: collection's factual `SUCCEEDED` or `UNCHANGED` line, or distribution's `SUCCEEDED`, `UNCHANGED`, `FAILED`, or `NOT ATTEMPTED (<reason>)` candidate row. Reversing the exact presentation escapes identifies only the discovery-frozen lexical candidate; it proves no current path, physical identity, alias uniqueness, containment, or repository state and authorizes no action. The loss does not retroactively make such a row false. Even a newline-complete exact summary remains only an advisory fragment, whether or not its displayed counts match the result rows captured in the same transcript; it is never a historical receipt or evidence of a missing row, queue size, invocation completion, authoritative exit, ownership release, or absence of later output. No diagnostic core, warning, error, informational line, retained-path or manual-guidance annex, aggregate, summary, or footer receives historical-receipt status, even when every observed line ends in a newline or appears self-contained, because an omitted continuation cannot be excluded; each remains only an advisory fragment after abnormal loss. This narrow meaning neither adds an end sentinel, length prefix, or JSON framing nor promotes human text into a machine schema, durable journal, generation token, or resume checkpoint. A partial result row and every missing item are non-evidence; absence never proves that a candidate was uninspected, unmodified, or not attempted. Even a complete earlier result row authorizes no inference about current repository state, later aliases, retained artifacts, cleanup, handle or lease release, remaining candidates, counts, or invocation completion, and it never authorizes skip, resume, rollback, or recovery. A visible summary or the BAT wrapper's neutral footer does not substitute for observing the authoritative process exit. If an interactive wrapper is closed or interrupted before its saved child exit is observed, this same partial-transcript rule applies. The next invocation never parses a prior transcript or cursor: collection captures and accepts a fresh current authoring snapshot, distribution repeats fresh candidate discovery and accepts a fresh central snapshot, and only those new package authorities plus current on-disk canonical and fixed-workspace evidence govern latest-wins repository or repair work.

Collection has no separate startup phase that recovers a prior transaction's recorded Old or New. A pre-existing safe `CollectionTransactionWorkspace` is current-invocation latest-wins repair input, but collection does not mutate it until the current authoring package snapshot is complete, stable, and accepted. Cancellation before the first workspace mutation preserves the prior workspace and canonical target, releases central ownership, emits no collection result, and returns `130` when snapshot cleanup and release are conclusive. Once workspace reset or fresh staging begins but before `UNCHANGED` commitment or destructive target mutation, cancellation uses the pre-target cleanup boundary above: it does not reconstruct historical workspace contents and succeeds as cancellation only after the exact workspace root is absent, the case-insensitive locator set is empty, and the invocation-start target state and ownership release are conclusive. A retained or unproved workspace at this pre-result boundary is failure rather than a cleanup warning. A non-cancellation operation failure uses the same phase boundary but never becomes cancellation or success: before the first workspace mutation it preserves the prior target and workspace, while after workspace mutation but before either `UNCHANGED` commitment or the first destructive target mutation it must prove exact root absence, an empty case-insensitive locator set, the invocation-start target state, cleanup, and ownership release. Conclusive cleanup still emits no collection result and returns the original exit `1`; uncertainty adds bounded retained paths and manual cleanup or ownership guidance and remains exit `1`. After `UNCHANGED` commitment, cancellation preserves that factual result and follows post-result cleanup and release; after target mutation starts it is deferred until verified current New or conclusive controlled restoration of the invocation-start target. A non-cancellation operation failure after target mutation first accepts verified current New as the factual result or proves exact restoration of the invocation-start target, including absence. Unproved restoration preserves workspace and backup evidence; after exact restoration, collection must prove workspace-root absence, an empty case-insensitive locator set, the restored target state, cleanup, and ownership release before returning the original failure with no collection result and exit `1`, while any uncertainty adds retained paths and manual guidance. Failure, boundary uncertainty, or ownership uncertainty wins with exit `1`; abrupt loss is handled by a later fresh latest-wins invocation. Distribution destinations use the same current-authority principle with their accepted central snapshot, while retaining their independently defined candidate results and cancellation rows.

Cooperating collection and distribution invocations coordinate repository ownership through OS-backed fixed sibling leases. On a conclusively non-remote slot, a continuously valid required handle serializes repository ownership: collection and distribution acquire the same central repository slot exclusively before inspecting or snapshotting its replaceable child or resetting its `CollectionTransactionWorkspace`, so at most one cooperating COLLECT or DIST invocation proceeds through that local ownership interval. Remote-backed slots instead use the point-in-time best-effort profile below. Distribution additionally acquires only its current destination repository slot exclusively. A slot is identified by the proved physical parent plus the exact `common_modules_repo` child name rather than the lexical candidate spelling or replaceable target-directory identity; ancestor aliases that reach the same physical parent therefore converge on the same lease and fixed transaction-workspace locator even while the canonical directory is temporarily absent. Each slot publicly reserves one lowercase ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse direct-sibling file named `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.lock` as its **CommonModulesRepositorySlotLease**. Direct siblings are matched to that basename under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`: zero matches immutably selects native `CREATE_NEW` for the exact name, and exactly one ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse file immutably selects native `OPEN_EXISTING` for exclusive reuse even when its bytes are empty, malformed, or stale. Only an outcome admitted by the closed operation-specific retry rule below returns to the same cancellation-aware acquisition loop; no other create-new or open failure is a collision or retryable acquisition race. After each bounded locator classification and before the next pre-open cancellation fence, the main flow preallocates and strongly captures one fresh `Ready` `LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture` solely for that prospective native open; it never resets or reuses a holder. A conclusively returned catchable holder-allocation or `Ready`-initialization failure starts no open or handle and is the existing invocation-global shared internal preparation stop; a nonreturn starts no open and produces no later result or action. The main flow then runs the existing pre-open cancellation fence; cancellation wins there and starts no open. Otherwise it samples the one Stopwatch defined below at that same safe admission boundary: only `Elapsed < 30 seconds` admits exactly one synchronous direct `CreateFileW` call on the exact marker path with `GENERIC_READ | GENERIC_WRITE | DELETE`, share mode zero, null security attributes, the preselected disposition, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` with no `FILE_FLAG_*`, and a null template handle, while `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` fixes timeout and starts none; an admitted call is never asynchronously interrupted for later cancellation or deadline passage. Exclusive open does not itself complete acquisition. When direct `CreateFileW` returns a raw `IntPtr` other than `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE`, before any bounded acquisition-proof observation or other fallible helper, cancellation or timeout action, logging, public output, or native call, the main flow never reads last error and, without creating an owning `SafeFileHandle` or `FileStream`, captures the exact raw handle value, establishes and registers unique sole-close ownership and a not-yet-issued release state, and records the provisional handle. Only after that safety prefix may PowerShell invoke exactly once, as a statement, the bootstrap-proved `void ConstructNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` method with the registered original raw value and that open attempt's fresh holder. A conclusive normal method return plus the holder's matching bound raw and valid terminal and payload combination is the sole construction and strong-reference-publication authority. Terminal `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` follows its rule below and starts no `FileStream` construction. Terminal `FileStreamConstructionFailed` registers holder-owned `SafeHandleOnly` and the exact returned second-constructor-failure boundary, immediately establishes the invocation-global shared internal preparation stop under the closed empty slot-specific-cause allowlist below, and then follows the partial-construction and hazard-neutralization rule. Establishing that cause fixes the global stop and exit `1` but neither selects nor rewrites the later-candidate first-stop reason while mandatory partial cleanup and release remain incomplete. Terminal `PairConstructed` registers the exact strong non-owning `SafeFileHandle` and `FileStream` pair with `LeaseOwnershipObservationReady`, observation ordinal `0`, observation mode `Unselected`, a null caught-Flush-exception reference, `FullPairRetirementReady`, an `Unselected` retirement-mode binding, and a null caught-retirement-exception reference; only this terminal may proceed to the remaining acquisition proof and preparation. PowerShell directly constructs or receives neither wrapper and performs no outer wrapper assignment. Every abnormal helper, binder, tag, raw, payload, or observation branch follows the no-later-action authority rule below, and no owning managed wrapper ever exists. An `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` return conclusively proves that no valid native handle returned and is never passed to `CloseHandle`; on that branch only, the same main-flow managed thread calls `Marshal.GetLastWin32Error()` exactly once before another P/Invoke or native call, thread switch, logging, cancellation or deadline action, exception construction, or any other fallible action, and immutably binds the captured code to the preselected disposition. From open issuance through acquisition classification, cancellation and deadline passage are latch or timing evidence only; no callback or timer closes, disposes, cleans up, or classifies a result, and the main flow does not interrupt the open or handle-safety sequence. Only after that safety prefix and a valid terminal `PairConstructed` succeed may the main flow perform the bounded acquisition-proof observations: it re-proves the same physical parent, re-enumerates the case-insensitive locator set, and proves that the handle still identifies its sole ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse member. The provisional state authorizes no other marker-content or marker-metadata observation, repository or workspace inspection, post-acquisition marker use, or mutation. The main flow then fixes every typed concrete handle argument and binding to the bootstrap buffer and preallocates the attempt-local execution state. Immediately after the last required acquisition-proof or preparation step completes and before the post-acquisition cancellation fence, the main flow captures that same Stopwatch once for timeliness: captured `Elapsed < 30 seconds` is timely, while captured `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` is late. It then reaches the safe wait-completion boundary, re-observes cancellation, and resolves timeliness and timeout under the rule below; cancellation or timeout starts no inspection. If managed control has returned and the fully constructed pair remains unretired, first enter the retirement-only mode below; if managed-wrapper construction was never begun, no wrapper retirement is needed. A conclusive normal combined-helper return with valid terminal `FileStreamConstructionFailed` never reaches this ordinary acquisition wait-completion boundary and follows the exact partial-construction rule below. Cancellation remains latch-only throughout its mandatory cleanup and release sequence; only conclusive completion of that sequence reaches the distinct sole `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe completion boundary below, where the main flow reads the latch exactly once. An in-flight, nonreturning, unknown, invalid, mismatched, or unproved construction, cleanup, or raw-close invocation reaches none of the ordinary acquisition wait-completion boundary, the `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe completion boundary, or `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` and follows the stronger no-later-action authority rule. Only after that step may the sole-close path be evaluated: raw release is allowed only when managed-wrapper construction was never begun, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` is conclusive, a fully constructed pair has conclusive retirement, or `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` is conclusive; otherwise required release is unproved, no close is synthesized, and `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` applies. A conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with bound `RetirementOnly` and valid terminal `FullPairRetired` preserves the cancellation or timeout classification; a conclusive normal return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` under either bound mode instead adds the invocation-global internal stop below. A case-only spelling, multiple matches, a wrong entry type, a reparse entry, or unproved existence or identity changes nothing and follows the established central-global or destination-candidate acquisition-failure scope. When managed control returns with a fully constructed pair on a handle-specific acquisition-failure branch, perform the retirement-only mode before release classification; a normal retirement preserves the established slot-specific or shared cause, while a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` and exact caught-retirement-exception payload adds the invocation-global internal stop. A conclusive normal combined-helper return with valid terminal `FileStreamConstructionFailed` instead immediately establishes the invocation-global shared internal preparation stop and follows the exact partial-construction rule below before release classification. Only conclusive `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` may proceed to the raw-close guard: a subsequent conclusive normal `true` return completes client-local release, while a conclusive normal `false` return fixes failed release and continuity and must finish the exact immediate same-thread last-error capture before the distinct safe completion boundary. Neither outcome clears that stop or exit `1`, and a capture nonreturn or unknown completion reaches no boundary or later action. Every valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only its dedicated safe completion boundary and never enters `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`. This terminal never enters retry, disappearance-exception, or ordinary slot-specific acquisition classification. Its sole safe completion boundary preserves any first-stop reason already fixed by an earlier explicit boundary; otherwise the boundary snapshot supplies the existing reason label of later unstarted destinations as `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)` when set or `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)` when clear, without changing current factual outcomes, the global stop, continuity, or exit `1`. The snapshot is not itself a public cancellation outcome or diagnostic, and when no later unstarted destination exists it creates no cancellation presentation. Every valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe-completion path retains exactly one logical path-free invocation-global internal-diagnostic slot. After its safe boundary and classifications close, one one-shot materialization attempt fixes the mandatory first `FILE_STREAM_CONSTRUCTION` entry and, only for valid `SafeHandleOnlyDisposeFailed`, second `SAFE_HANDLE_ONLY_DISPOSE` entry; catchable whole-item assembly failure or conclusive terminal `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` supplies a silent zero-item presentation, terminal `FcfOutputCompleted` supplies exactly one whole local stderr item, and a post-admission `FcfOutputTransportFailed` follows the existing partial-transcript rule without creating a second diagnostic. A slot-specific OS or provider failure during handle-specific preparation returns to that same scope only after proving that no native handle returned, or that the wrapper prerequisite—managed-wrapper construction was never begun, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` is conclusive, a fully constructed pair has conclusive retirement, or `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` is conclusive—and a nonzero sole native `CloseHandle` return for the provisional handle are both proved. Outside the valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` partial branch, any conclusive normal `false` return on that authorized close reaches the applicable scope or result rule only after `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`; a capture-abnormal path reaches neither the boundary nor that rule. A shared interop-helper, state-machine, or invariant defect is an invocation-global internal preparation stop even after conclusive release, and every valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses that same stop; neither is ever candidate-local. If a native handle may have returned and its sole nonzero `CloseHandle` release cannot be proved, `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` also latches and stops later destinations without erasing the independent cause. Only a timely conclusive acquisition accepted at that boundary retains the original native marker handle returned by direct `CreateFileW` with `GENERIC_READ | GENERIC_WRITE | DELETE`, share mode zero, null security attributes, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` with no `FILE_FLAG_*`, and a null template handle, together with its exact non-owning `new SafeFileHandle(rawHandle, false)` and `new FileStream(safeHandle, FileAccess.ReadWrite, 4096, false)` pair. It never arms `DeleteOnClose` or any delete disposition at acquisition, retains that exact stream without path reopening or replacement until either the normal terminal final required holder-bound same-handle observation and one-shot retirement or a returned non-normal retirement-only cleanup, and retains the original native handle alone through terminal release. Native-close ownership is unique and never shared with an automatic managed-close path. That handle is intended to deny competing ownership and delete-or-rename sharing. On a conclusively non-remote slot, while its continuous validity is conclusive, it is the sole coordinating ownership authority through that slot's final outcome; on remote backing, each successful required same-handle observation supplies only current coordination evidence under the rule below, while marker bytes, metadata, and stored paths never prove an owner, recovery state, or authority over another filesystem entry. When the existing phase-specific rule admits normal terminal marker cleanup and cleanup authority remains conclusive after all branch-required shared-filesystem work, the invocation fixes the exact next positive observation ordinal and typed observation mode `NormalTerminal`, then invokes `ObserveLeaseOwnership` exactly once; this final holder-bound same-handle `Flush(true)` observation is required even for a conclusively non-remote slot. Once terminal marker cleanup is admitted, later or repeated cancellation does not veto its final observation, wrapper retirement, disposition attempt, or close. Only after the observation helper returns conclusively normally with the exact final ordinal, bound observation mode `NormalTerminal`, valid terminal `LeaseOwnershipFlushReturnedNormally`, null caught-Flush-exception, required provider or protocol semantics, and still-conclusive cleanup authority, and the exact retirement helper then returns conclusively normally with bound retirement mode `NormalTerminal` and valid terminal `FullPairRetired` under the one-shot rule below does the invocation begin exactly one non-retrying in-process P/Invoke disposition step for `SetFileInformationByHandle(FileDispositionInfo)` with `DeleteFile = true` on that same handle; the native-call boundary may be crossed at most once. After all pre-admission preparation succeeds, successful exactly-once initialization of one preallocated process-local monotonic execution state to `NotIssued` constitutes disposition-attempt admission; no distinct admitted-but-uninitialized state exists. Immediately before the first submission-capable operation, it transitions that state once and irreversibly from `NotIssued` to `MaybeArmed`; it does not record the call as issued. A `TRUE` return alone transitions `MaybeArmed` to `Armed` and proceeds immediately to the conclusive required-release sequence defined below without an intervening cancellation action or trailing ownership observation. An `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome` follows the rule below and proceeds immediately to that same required-release sequence. Neither command deletes a CommonModules marker by pathname, reopens or reacquires it for deletion or cleanup, retries or disarms disposition, reconnects, or falls back, and this plumbing adds no helper executable or `vba-dev` feature. An `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome` leaves the handle unarmed; after the sole native `CloseHandle` returns nonzero under the required-release proof below, the retained marker is warning-only only when the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admits that exact cause and no independent failure already controls the outcome. The initial warning-eligible pre-transition cause set is empty, so this conditional warning branch is dormant until an explicit later contract revision admits a specific cause. If `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` latches before disposition, the invocation never arms it. When managed control has returned and the fully constructed pair remains unretired, it first enters retirement-only mode; a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with bound `RetirementOnly` and valid terminal `FullPairRetired` never clears the continuity latch, while a conclusive normal return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` under either bound mode adds the invocation-global internal stop. It then performs the one unarmed close attempt only when managed-wrapper construction was never begun, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` is conclusive, a fully constructed pair has conclusive retirement, or `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` is conclusive, and otherwise synthesizes no close and retains unproved required release. If managed control returns normally or through a catchable exception and the disposition neither returned `TRUE` nor established an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the invocation treats the handle as maybe armed and latches `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure`; the mandatory sole `CloseHandle` attempt then runs, but even a later nonzero return never clears or downgrades that latch. While the managed invocation remains in flight or never returns, the invocation infers no terminal outcome and performs no later disposition, close, cancellation, deadline, or output action. A successful armed disposition followed by failed or uncertain close or release has the same failure classification. The invocation never retries, disarms, undoes, or inspects either state by path. The tools do not edit Git ignore configuration for the marker. Every cooperating version uses this same locator and ownership protocol. A future basename change acquires old and new marker basenames in `Ordinal` order and holds both through the slot's final outcome rather than silently splitting ownership; a pre-lease version remains a nonparticipating writer. Acquiring a destination lease records the last-proved intended physical slot for the current candidate attempt and never authorizes voluntary retargeting to another observed slot. For a conclusively non-remote slot during that continuously proved ownership interval, this invocation-wide central serialization replaces the earlier shared-source model and prevents cooperating distributions from concurrently mutating parent and nested destination paths without requiring a pairwise containment graph. Immediately after each central- or destination-slot lease-acquisition wait begins and before that wait's first pre-open cancellation fence, the invocation starts exactly once one process-local monotonic `System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch`. The same instance's `Elapsed` is the sole clock for the fixed, non-configurable 30-second open/retry admission deadline; it is never restarted, reset, or replaced across a collision or retry, and neither `DateTime` nor any other wall clock is used for this deadline or timeliness decision. Only `Elapsed < 30 seconds` admits a new synchronous create-new or open attempt; `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` admits none and fixes timeout at the safe boundary. The admission clock is not a hard return or terminal-completion bound for an attempt admitted before expiry. Such an attempt is never asynchronously interrupted for deadline passage; its open, immediate handle-safety prefix, fixed-scope acquisition proof, typed-argument binding, and attempt-state preallocation may overrun 30 seconds or never return. If it returns, the main flow first establishes any returned valid handle's exact raw value and sole-close registration and completes or conclusively fails the remaining acquisition classification. An otherwise conclusive acquisition whose captured `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` is not timely: absent earlier cancellation, timeout then wins at the safe boundary, no post-acquisition marker use or repository or workspace domain inspection starts, and any registered handle follows the sole-close release path only under the wrapper prerequisite; otherwise no close is synthesized and required release remains unproved. Only an outcome admitted by the closed operation-specific retry rule reaches the next safe pre-open boundary; after expiry no retry is issued. While a required synchronous step remains in flight or never returns, no authoritative timeout result, exit, or output is emitted and no independent completion deadline, worker, supervisor, or cancellation interruption is added; the invocation remains in an accepted availability gap until the call returns or external termination occurs. The initial contract has no timeout option. Central-slot timeout is an unchanged global failure; destination-slot timeout makes only that candidate failed and later candidates continue. For a conclusively non-remote slot, process death releases OS ownership and an unowned exact marker remains reusable. For a remote-backed slot, process or connection loss does not prove immediate server-side release; every later contender must use the ordinary acquisition wait and proof rather than assuming that the prior open disappeared. If a destination result is already conclusive but its required client-local lease release cannot be proved, the factual row and repository remain unchanged, the invocation fails, and no later destination starts. If candidate authority disappeared before mutation, the existing warning-only no-row classification likewise remains factual while the same ownership failure stops later processing. An `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome` whose exact cause is admitted by the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set and that is followed by conclusive required release remains warning-only and permits later processing when no independent failure already controls the outcome. The tools never force-unlock or terminate an owner. Nonparticipating readers and writers need not honor the lease, so exact current target inventory and bytes are still rechecked immediately before commitment; a noncooperating change in the narrow interval after that comparison and before rename remains outside the guarantee.

For the CommonModules repository-slot lease rule above, the initial PowerShell-only implementation obtains the native open result and error evidence from exactly one synchronous direct `CreateFileW` P/Invoke. The protected bootstrap declares it from `kernel32.dll` with managed ABI `IntPtr CreateFileW([MarshalAs(UnmanagedType.LPWStr)] string lpFileName, uint dwDesiredAccess, uint dwShareMode, IntPtr lpSecurityAttributes, uint dwCreationDisposition, uint dwFlagsAndAttributes, IntPtr hTemplateFile)`, `EntryPoint = CreateFileW`, `CharSet.Unicode`, `ExactSpelling = true`, `SetLastError = true`, `CallingConvention.Winapi`, and a raw `IntPtr` return; it never returns an owning `SafeFileHandle` or other managed owner. Before invocation, the main flow immutably binds the exact marker path and selected `CREATE_NEW` or `OPEN_EXISTING` disposition. It passes `GENERIC_READ | GENERIC_WRITE | DELETE`, share mode zero, null security attributes, the selected disposition, `FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL` with no `FILE_FLAG_*`, and a null template handle. A return other than `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` is the valid raw-handle branch: never read or interpret last error, and immediately enter the existing raw-handle capture and sole-close safety prefix before any other fallible action. An `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` return conclusively proves that no valid native handle returned and is never passed to `CloseHandle`. On that branch only, the same main-flow managed thread calls `Marshal.GetLastWin32Error()` exactly once before another P/Invoke or native call, thread switch, logging, cancellation or deadline action, exception construction, or any other fallible action, and immutably binds the captured code to the preselected disposition. The closed retry allowlist is exactly `CREATE_NEW + ERROR_FILE_EXISTS (80)`, `OPEN_EXISTING + ERROR_SHARING_VIOLATION (32)`, and `OPEN_EXISTING + ERROR_FILE_NOT_FOUND (2)`. The first two alone are collision outcomes and the third is a stale-locator race, but all three re-enter the same cancellation-aware attempt loop at bounded locator classification from sibling enumeration rather than blindly retrying the same path. Every route retains the same slot's `System.Diagnostics.Stopwatch` without restart, reset, or replacement and reaches the next safe pre-open cancellation and deadline boundary before another call. Only the immutable disposition/code pair captured by that exact sentinel branch is native error evidence. An exception class, generic `IOException`, `HResult` or low-word projection, message text, provider label, inferred busy state, later or reconstructed last error, or a similar-looking code is insufficient. Every other captured pair or unproved pair is an immediate non-timeout acquisition failure under the existing cause-provenance, conclusive-handle-nonreturn, and release rules; runtime inference or configuration never converts it to busy, collision, or retry. An exception, nonreturn, or unknown completion from `CreateFileW` never synthesizes `INVALID_HANDLE_VALUE` or an allowlisted pair and follows the existing availability, shared-internal, and continuity rules. The initial allowlist is fixed and non-configurable; adding an entry requires an explicit later contract revision.

A fully qualified Windows filesystem search root or canonical collection slot is not rejected merely because its backing is reached through a UNC path, a network-mapped drive, a DFS namespace, or another remote spelling; path spelling alone neither proves nor disproves remote backing, and URI or non-filesystem-provider input remains outside this path grammar. The strong non-remote profile applies only when affirmative handle-level evidence proves the backing conclusively non-remote; remote or unclassifiable backing uses the best-effort profile and is never upgraded by path spelling. Every admitted remote slot must still provide the existing point-in-time ordinary-entry, non-reparse, physical-identity, boundary, containment, exclusive-open, same-parent replacement, and postcondition evidence; inability to prove any required fact fails closed under the existing collection-global, distribution-central-global, or destination-candidate scope. Remote backing alone emits no warning. On a remote-backed slot, however, the marker protocol is deliberately best-effort across a network partition, server failover, administrative close, DFS referral change, or reconnect. The exclusive marker handle excludes another cooperating owner only while the server-side open remains valid. A successful same-handle observation proves ownership only at that observation; it is not a server-side fencing token that atomically conditions a later path-based open, rename, restore, or delete. The accepted contract therefore does not promise that two cooperating invocations can never overlap after a remote ownership break, and `atomic replacement` describes an individual successful same-parent namespace operation rather than an atomic marker-check-plus-publication transaction. Throughout this CommonModules contract, `pinned`, `held`, `bound`, `same physical slot`, and `never retargets` on remote backing name the attempt's last-proved intended slot and its refusal to chase a newly observed locator; they do not claim that the server namespace, DFS referral, or physical identity cannot change between observations. A detected or unproved mismatch uses the fail-stop rule below, while an undetected retarget remains part of the accepted residual remote risk.

Collection and distribution run no background heartbeat, polling thread, runspace, supervisor, periodic refresh, `vba-dev` feature, or helper executable for repository-slot ownership. After acquisition and before required release, when any still-required slot is remote-backed or not conclusively proved non-remote, the main synchronous flow performs a same-handle ownership observation through every still-required already-open lease immediately before and, once the call returns, immediately after each individual path-based filesystem mutation attempt that can change shared CommonModules repository, transaction-workspace, staging, backup, or marker state, and immediately before and after each exact-current comparison or current-New verification. A compound or recursive helper applies this fence to each underlying path-mutation call rather than only around the batch. Collection observes its central lease; distribution observes both its invocation-wide central lease and the current destination lease while both remain required. Immediately before each leading observation set, the main flow executes the existing phase-specific cancellation fence. If that rule forbids the next ordinary call, it enters the cancellation branch without starting the leading observations. After every leading observation succeeds, it re-observes cancellation immediately before issuing the protected mutation or comparison and admits the call only if the phase rule still permits it. Once admitted, a call is not asynchronously interrupted by a later cancellation; unless the call itself establishes required lease loss, the trailing fence resolves before that request is processed—either every required trailing observation succeeds, or the first non-success latches `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` and no remaining observation is attempted. After successful trailing observations, the next phase-specific cancellation fence runs before an otherwise eligible pre-target `UNCHANGED` result commits or another pre-target call begins. A cancellation first processed at that fence therefore wins and enters the existing pre-result cleanup. After destructive target mutation, cancellation remains deferred through result-committed current New or the established restoration or failure outcome. A cancellation already incorporated into `PreTargetCancellationCleanup`, post-target restoration or failure handling, deferred current-New completion, or mandatory cleanup and release is state rather than a veto on the calls required by that branch; each qualifying mutation still receives its own ownership fence, while optional post-result cleanup retains its existing cancellation rule. A fence passes only when every required observation succeeds. Current-New verification plus a successful trailing fence commits `SUCCEEDED` under the destructive-mutation cancellation deferral. Exact-current comparison plus a successful trailing fence establishes eligibility for `UNCHANGED`, which commits only if cancellation does not win at the immediately following phase-specific fence; throughout this contract, `verified current New` and `Verified New` include the verification and successful trailing fence rather than content verification alone. If a mutation or comparison itself establishes required lease loss, the invocation latches the failure immediately and performs no post-observation; an ordinary mutation or comparison failure alone still performs the required post-observation. Any failed, timed-out, or inconclusive observation latches `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` and uses its existing phase-specific result preservation without reopening or reacquiring a lease. At an ownership-observation completion boundary where cancellation and an observation non-success are first both visible and neither has fixed the stop earlier, cancellation fixes `CANCELLED` for later unstarted destinations; the observation non-success nevertheless latches `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure`, forces exit `1`, and forbids restoration or cleanup. Without cancellation, ownership failure fixes `GLOBAL FAILURE`. Lease acquisition retains its existing proof boundary, and required release uses the exactly-once native proof below; neither is recursively fenced. Once admitted, normal terminal marker cleanup invokes the managed observation helper with the exact next ordinal and mode `NormalTerminal`; only a conclusive normal helper return with valid `LeaseOwnershipFlushReturnedNormally`, matching ordinal and mode, null caught exception, required provider or protocol semantics, and still-conclusive cleanup authority may be followed by exactly one synchronous normal-terminal managed-wrapper retirement, one same-handle disposition attempt, and the immediate sole raw-handle close sequence above. A returned branch that will not reach that successful final observation instead uses the retirement-only mode above, permanently forbids disposition, and proceeds only to the guarded raw close after conclusive wrapper retirement; a normal retirement preserves the branch cause and result, while a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` and exact caught-retirement-exception payload adds the invocation-global internal stop. That final holder-bound observation is required even for a conclusively non-remote slot. The disposition is handle-bound rather than a pathname mutation, receives no recursive leading or trailing ownership observation, and no pathname-based CommonModules marker deletion or cleanup-only path reacquisition exists. These synchronous fences add no background worker or independent deadline, do not interrupt an in-flight call, and do not close the observation-to-operation, in-operation, operation-to-observation, or multi-read comparison races. They remain point-in-time evidence rather than continuous ownership proof or a fencing token.

Each required same-handle ownership observation is one ordinal-bound synchronous evaluation of the exact holder-owned `FileStream.Flush(true)` performed only inside the bootstrap-proved managed authority. The fixed public enum `LeaseOwnershipObservationMode` contains only `NonTerminal = 1` and `NormalTerminal = 2`, and PowerShell invokes exactly `public static void ObserveLeaseOwnership(LeaseWrapperConstructionCapture holder, long observationOrdinal, LeaseOwnershipObservationMode mode)` as a `void` statement with the same holder, an explicitly typed positive one-based `Int64`, and the exact phase-admitted enum; it receives no wrapper and directly invokes no `FileStream` member. The holder's separate serialized observation tuple starts as `LeaseOwnershipObservationReady`, ordinal `0`, mode `Unselected`, and null caught-Flush-exception only for a valid `PairConstructed`; both partial-construction terminals leave it `LeaseOwnershipObservationUnavailable`. The first call is admitted only from `Ready(0)`, and each later ordinal `n` only from the exact preceding `LeaseOwnershipFlushReturnedNormally(n - 1, NonTerminal)` with null exception. Before mutating holder state, the helper validates the preserved `PairConstructed` construction evidence—the same construction terminal, bound raw, exact private wrapper references, and null constructor-exception references—plus `FullPairRetirementReady`, a defined mode, a checked exact-next ordinal without `Int64` overflow, and the permitted prior generation. It binds the requested ordinal and mode, irreversibly publishes `LeaseOwnershipObservationInFlight`, and immediately evaluates exactly the stored `FileStream.Flush(true)` inside a narrow exception-catching boundary containing only that call and managed-local exception capture. A normal call leaves the exception null, publishes `LeaseOwnershipFlushReturnedNormally` last, and returns normally. A contract-catchable exception is captured locally; after the boundary the helper stores that exact exception, publishes `LeaseOwnershipFlushFailed` last, and returns normally without wrapping or rethrowing it. A failed generation and either terminal with mode `NormalTerminal` are absorbing, and retirement start also forbids another generation; a successful `NonTerminal` generation alone may be consumed by the exact next ordinal. Ordinal, mode, state, and payload remain immutable until that sole permitted next-generation transition and are never reset, decremented, retried, or reused; concurrent calls are rejected by the one-holder transition. The sole managed Flush-outcome evidence is the conjunction of the construction helper's conclusive normal return and preserved `PairConstructed` construction evidence (the same construction terminal, bound raw, exact wrapper references, and null constructor-exception references) with the observation helper's conclusive normal return, same holder, raw, and references, the exact requested current ordinal and bound mode, and the matching valid terminal and payload. `LeaseOwnershipFlushReturnedNormally` proves only normal return of that exact synchronous call. For remote-backed or unclassifiable storage, it counts as a successful ownership observation only with independent conclusive proof that the provider or protocol implements the call as a server-authoritative round trip for the same remote open; for SMB, that proof is an SMB2 FLUSH request and successful response using the retained open's `Open.FileId`. Provider or protocol classification never substitutes for the managed terminal. `LeaseOwnershipFlushFailed`, provider-reported failure or timeout, unsupported semantics, or a technically normal return without the required remote-semantics proof is a failed or inconclusive required observation: latch `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure`, admit no later observation or disposition, and, after a conclusive returned boundary, use only `RetirementOnly` wrapper cleanup followed by the existing guarded raw close. The caught exception is bounded diagnostic payload and never independently decides cause provenance. The line contains no path, locator, marker name, raw or wrapper handle, provider or protocol label, exception text, message, symbolic error name, cancellation snapshot, or reconstructed evidence. Only after the applicable safe-completion or terminal release boundary and the continuity, result, later-reason, and exit classifications are conclusive may the PowerShell main flow fix the scope and optional field and attempt the whole CR/LF-free immutable `System.String` materialization exactly once. A catchable formatting or whole-line assembly failure silently omits this item with no fallback line, retry, partial item, second diagnostic, `OutputTransportFailure`, or classification change; `UNKNOWN` never substitutes for such a presentation failure. Materialization nonreturn reaches no handoff and permits no later action from that in-flight materialization. A successful assembly hands the same immutable string reference exactly once to the ordinary bounded stderr terminal-item handoff. Unlike the `FileStreamConstructionFailed` pre-write abandonment exception, any catchable preparation, admission, queue, start, write, or required-flush failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion after that ordinary handoff is attempted follows the existing `OutputTransportFailure` rule; never rebuild, retry, resend, or switch streams. The stderr line writer contributes the sole line terminator. Materialization, omission, and handoff outcome feed back into no terminal, continuity latch, cause or scope, result, first-stop reason, retry, disappearance eligibility, release, disposition, cancellation outcome, diagnostic count, or exit.

At a central- or destination-slot wait-completion boundary, the cause priority is cancellation, then timely conclusive acquisition, then timeout, and the main flow evaluates that priority only at the applicable safe boundary. Immediately before each open or retry, the safe admission boundary evaluates cancellation first; cancellation stops without opening. Otherwise it samples the same Stopwatch: only `Elapsed < 30 seconds` admits the synchronous call, while `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` fixes timeout and starts none. Once it admits a synchronous open, cancellation or deadline passage during that open, the handle-safety prefix, or the remaining acquisition proof and preparation is evidence only: it does not asynchronously interrupt or veto those steps, and no callback or timer closes, disposes, cleans up, or classifies a result. A valid native-handle return is provisional rather than acquisition completion. Before acting on cancellation or timeout, the main flow, if a valid native handle returned, first establishes the exact raw-handle and sole-close registration; in every branch it then completes or conclusively fails the remaining acquisition classification.
