Immediately after the last required acquisition-proof or preparation step completes and before the post-acquisition cancellation fence, the main flow captures the same Stopwatch once for timeliness. Captured `Elapsed < 30 seconds` is timely; captured `Elapsed >= 30 seconds` is late. It then runs that fence; an already requested cancellation wins and starts no inspection. A valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path never reaches this ordinary acquisition wait-completion boundary. After pre-open admission, cancellation remains latch-only through the construction helper and its mandatory partial cleanup and release; the main flow reads it exactly once only at the distinct terminal-specific safe completion boundary after conclusive cleanup and release classification. That boundary performs no Stopwatch, deadline, or timeout evaluation; under the exact partial-construction rule its sole public effect is the existing reason label, if any, of later unstarted destinations, and it creates no cancellation presentation when none exists. Separately, a non-FCF conclusive normal `false` return may reach `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` only after its required immediate capture terminal; that boundary likewise performs no Stopwatch, deadline, or timeout evaluation and reads the latch only while no earlier explicit first-stop reason exists. If managed control has returned and the fully constructed pair remains unretired, the invocation first enters retirement-only mode; a normal retirement preserves cancellation, while a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` and exact caught-retirement-exception payload adds the invocation-global internal stop. It releases through the established sole-close path only when managed-wrapper construction was never begun, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` is conclusive, a fully constructed pair has conclusive retirement, or `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` is conclusive; otherwise no close is synthesized and required release remains unproved. Without cancellation, only timely conclusive acquisition is accepted. A late otherwise successful attempt—even when admitted before 30 seconds—latches timeout only after it reaches that safe classification boundary and starts no post-acquisition marker use or repository or workspace domain inspection. If managed control has returned and the fully constructed pair remains unretired, the invocation first enters retirement-only mode; a normal retirement preserves timeout, while a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` and exact caught-retirement-exception payload adds the invocation-global internal stop. It releases a registered handle only when its sole-close path and either proof that managed-wrapper construction was never begun, conclusive `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent`, conclusive retirement of a fully constructed pair, or conclusive `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` are proved; if any prerequisite, release authority, or completion is unproved, `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` applies and no close is synthesized. The invocation latches exactly one cause for that wait and never changes it afterward; the selected cause follows the existing central- or destination-wait result and exit rules, while release uncertainty and independently established failures retain their existing precedence.

Apply the closed three-pair retry rule above before classifying a non-timeout lease acquisition error. Every other conclusively completed direct `CreateFileW` failure enters this non-timeout acquisition-failure matrix immediately; inability to prove conclusive handle nonreturn or required release retains the existing continuity classification. First apply the fixed terminal-specific cause rules below; classify shared-versus-slot-specific provenance only for the remaining errors. Outside the valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` partial branch, a conclusive normal `false` raw-close path enters this matrix or any applicable phase-specific result rule only after `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`; capture abnormality reaches neither boundary nor matrix. When managed control has returned and a fully constructed pair remains unretired, perform retirement-only mode before applying this matrix; a normal retirement preserves the already established acquisition cause, while a conclusive normal `RetireNonOwningLeaseWrapperPair` return with valid terminal `FullPairRetiredAfterDisposeFailure` and exact caught-retirement-exception payload adds the invocation-global internal stop. A conclusive normal construction/capture-helper return with valid terminal `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` first follows the exact `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` rule; it always remains the invocation-global internal preparation stop and never becomes retryable, slot-specific, or eligible for the disappearance exception, even after a nonzero sole raw close. Only conclusive `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` and the subsequent raw-close result permit its release classification to complete; an unproved release adds continuity, while an in-flight, nonreturning, or unknown construction branch never enters this matrix. A conclusive normal combined-helper return with valid terminal `FileStreamConstructionFailed` immediately establishes the invocation-global shared internal preparation stop and then follows the exact partial-construction rule. Its initial closed slot-specific-cause allowlist is empty and non-configurable; no terminal, exception detail, constructor stage, provider or path inference, later cleanup, or release result changes that classification. Only conclusive `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` and a subsequent conclusive normal `true` sole raw close complete client-local release, but they never clear the internal stop or exit `1`. A conclusive normal `false` return instead fixes failed release and continuity and must complete the immediate same-thread last-error capture before the safe boundary; its captured or unavailable code changes no classification and enters no `FileStreamConstructionFailed` diagnostic. A conclusive normal `RetireSafeHandleOnly` return with valid terminal `SafeHandleOnlyDisposeFailed` and exact cleanup payload retains that stop, adds the continuity failure, and permits no raw close. An abnormal, nonreturning, unknown, invalid, mismatched, or unproved cleanup or raw-close invocation, or a binder or outer-call failure around the post-`false` capture, an exception escaping its narrow boundary, capture nonreturn, unknown or unproved completion, or invalid or mismatched capture state or payload, follows the stronger no-later-action rule, reaches neither the `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe completion boundary nor `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, and never enters this matrix. A contract-catchable capture exception absorbed by the narrow boundary is excluded and instead follows the code-unavailable safe-boundary path above. This terminal never enters the retry, disappearance-exception, ordinary slot-specific, or destination-continuation rules below. After the conclusive cleanup and release classification permitted by that rule reaches the sole `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe completion boundary, atomically read cancellation exactly once and preserve any previously fixed first-stop reason; otherwise use a set snapshot only to supply `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)` or a clear snapshot only to supply `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)` as the existing reason label of later unstarted destinations. Independently of the snapshot value, then apply the invocation-global internal-stop matrix above: for a central slot emit no collection result or distribution queue, row, zero-target classification, count, aggregate, or summary; for a destination slot preserve every established factual row or no-row classification, otherwise assign the current candidate exactly one `FAILED` result, and return exit `1` without starting a later destination. The boundary snapshot is never rendered independently, establishes no cancellation outcome or exit `130`, and, for a central slot or a last destination with no later unstarted destination, adds no cancellation diagnostic or other presentation. Independently of the cancellation snapshot and cleanup or release classification values, fix the one logical `FileStreamConstructionFailed` entry set after the safe boundary and consume its one-shot materialization attempt only after the result matrix is conclusive; terminal `FcfOutputCompleted` supplies one whole item, catchable assembly failure or conclusive `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` silently supplies none, terminal `FcfOutputTransportFailed` follows the advisory partial-transcript rule, and neither cleanup, raw close, nor captured or unavailable last-error-code evidence creates another diagnostic or enters the fixed item. A valid captured or unavailable post-`false` code may only append the optional `closeLastError` field to the separate already-authorized single `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` ownership diagnostic under the canonical required-release rule. A shared interop-helper, state-machine, or invariant defect uses the invocation-global internal preparation stop, and every valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses that same stop; neither becomes candidate-local even after conclusive release. Only a slot-specific OS or provider cause independently established outside either valid managed construction-terminal path is then classified by handle and ownership proof rather than by exception category. When it is conclusive that no native handle returned, or when both the wrapper prerequisite—managed-wrapper construction was never begun, `SafeFileHandleConstructionHazardAbsent` is conclusive, a fully constructed pair has conclusive retirement, or `SafeHandleOnlyHazardNeutralized` is conclusive—and a nonzero sole native `CloseHandle` return for the provisional or acquired handle are proved, a central-slot error is an unchanged global failure that emits no collection result or distribution candidate rows, while a destination-slot error normally makes only the current candidate `FAILED` and later candidates continue. One destination-only exception applies when a final read-only observation conclusively proves both that ownership was never acquired and that the lexical candidate's physical parent disappeared or retargeted away from the prospective slot: distribution uses the aggregate disappearance warning with no row and performs no retry, retarget, marker inspection, or workspace inspection. If either fact is unproved, the candidate remains `FAILED`. When a native handle may have returned and neither conclusive proof that no native handle was returned nor required client-local release can be proved, central acquisition reports one invocation-level ownership failure; destination acquisition makes the current candidate `FAILED` and stops target processing. On the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path it uses only the later-unstarted label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`; on every other conclusively classified path that reaches this matrix it marks later candidates `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. Central acquisition never uses the disappearance exception. For a slot-specific OS or provider cause only, if cancellation was already latched first, its candidate classifications remain fixed and only release uncertainty changes the invocation exit to `1` under the existing first-stop rule. A shared interop-helper, state-machine, or invariant defect always uses the invocation-global internal-stop matrix above.

After initial queue formation and screening, distribution captures central package contents only when at least one candidate still requires workspace-driven repair or an ordinary distribution turn. If no such candidate remains, it emits the stable screening warnings and every established candidate-local `FAILED` row followed by the exact distribution summary and exits `1`; when screening established no result row, it emits the warnings followed by the existing zero-target failure with no summary. This early terminal path creates no `NOT ATTEMPTED` row and performs no central content snapshot, content validation, live content recomparison, snapshot cleanup, or snapshot-phase cancellation classification. It does not waive central repository existence, physical-boundary, identity, or safety failures established before or during screening, and it still requires conclusive final central-slot release; release uncertainty adds the established invocation-level ownership failure without rewriting the screening result. Only when at least one candidate requires workspace-driven repair or a destination turn does the following central-snapshot contract apply.

One invocation-owned raw-byte snapshot is the sole central-package authority for a distribution run; no public generation identifier, manifest field, sidecar version, digest line, timestamp, or file attribute becomes package identity. With the exclusive central repository-slot lease already held, distribution completes initial candidate-path discovery and screening and, before any candidate workspace repair or target mutation, enumerates, byte-copies, and fully validates the complete closed central package snapshot. Cancellation before queue finalization follows the discovery rule above and exposes no partial result. After the queue is fixed, distribution re-enumerates the live central repository and compares every name, entry type, raw byte, and absence of extras with that snapshot. Only when no cancellation cause has already been latched, immediately before accepting the snapshot as stable authority it again proves that no `PendingCollectionTransactionState` exists; snapshot cancellation stops validation without performing this check merely to discover another failure. State that appeared, or whose absence is now unprovable, is a global exit-`1` failure: screening-established candidate-local results remain, every other unstarted candidate becomes `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`, the cause is emitted once, and distribution does not inspect or mutate the workspace. On a conclusively non-remote central slot during its continuously proved ownership interval, the exclusive lease excludes cooperating collection and distribution. On remote or unclassifiable backing it supplies only the required point-in-time best-effort coordination evidence. In either profile, this second observation remains an optimistic fence against a manual edit, Git checkout, old script, or other nonparticipating writer. Cancellation observed after the queue and screening results are fixed but before stable snapshot acceptance stops capture, validation, and live recomparison and performs no destination inspection or mutation. Candidate-local `FAILED` results and warning-skips already established by screening remain, while every other queued candidate is `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)`. Distribution does not continue validation merely to discover a new central failure. Conclusive central-handle and lease release plus partial-snapshot cleanup returns `130` when no earlier failure exists. If snapshot ownership, containment, handles, or central ownership cannot be proved, one invocation-level failure returns exit `1` without changing those cancelled candidate rows or fabricating a target failure. A central-package invalidity or mismatch established before cancellation instead wins as a global exit-`1` failure: known candidate-local failures remain, every other unstarted applicable candidate is `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`, and the invocation-level cause is emitted once. A mismatch is not retried automatically. After stable capture, every ordinary destination comparison, workspace reseed, create-new staging package, and current-attempt New inventory or fingerprint derives only from those fixed bytes; distribution never rereads live source per target. The accepted snapshot is the sole package authority for both ordinary destination work and repair through a pre-existing `DistributionTransactionWorkspace`; no prior destination record byte, phase, fingerprint, or stored path is recovery authority. The complete fingerprint remains opaque implementation and temporary current-attempt evidence. Distribution never repairs interrupted collection state itself: a pending collection workspace is the independent hard gate above even when the canonical source is an otherwise valid complete generation, while an invalid canonical source is likewise a global failure; both direct the user to run `COLLECT_COMMON_MODS` for current-source latest-wins repair or manual guidance. It retains the exclusive central slot through every candidate outcome. If final central-slot release cannot be proved, all factual destination rows—including cancellation-derived `NOT ATTEMPTED` rows—remain unchanged, committed destinations are not rolled back, no synthetic target result is added, and one invocation-level central-ownership failure returns exit `1`. A retained central marker remains an established warning-only cleanup result only when the terminal same-handle observation and cleanup authority are conclusive, its one disposition attempt establishes an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admits that exact cause, and the still-unarmed handle is then conclusively released. Once handle release is proved, inability to remove only a proved non-authoritative snapshot separately remains the established warning-only cleanup result. This intentionally permits exit `1` with zero candidate `FAILED` rows.

The initial collection and distribution wrappers have deterministic human-readable text and authoritative process exit codes but no versioned machine-readable result or format option. Their stdout and stderr are not a parser contract; a future JSON surface uses a separately versioned envelope rather than promoting these lines into a machine schema. Distribution emits one terminal line per applicable result in lexical candidate order using `SUCCEEDED`, `UNCHANGED`, `FAILED`, or `NOT ATTEMPTED (<reason>)`; the initial reason tokens are `CANCELLED` and `GLOBAL FAILURE`. Collection emits no summary or count line. Distribution has exactly one case-sensitive, CR/LF-free summary form: `DIST: summary succeeded=<SucceededCount> unchanged=<UnchangedCount> failed=<FailedCount> notAttempted=<NotAttemptedCount>`. Each placeholder is replaced by an invariant ASCII decimal matching the whole-value grammar `0|[1-9][0-9]*`; signs, leading zeroes, grouping, localized digits, and whitespace are forbidden. Field order, token case, ASCII punctuation, and single spaces are fixed; placeholders are replaced rather than emitted, and no optional, total, reason-specific, warning, or exit field exists. `notAttempted` combines `CANCELLED` and `GLOBAL FAILURE` rows. After all count inputs are frozen, the main flow invokes one preprepared narrow count-finalization boundary exactly once. Before counting, that boundary validates exactly one frozen queue-ordinal candidate-result slot for every queued lexical candidate, keyed by queue ordinal and its discovery-frozen candidate identity rather than rendered path text or physical identity; each slot must appear exactly once and contain exactly one closed result-row classification or one warning-only no-row classification. The boundary then derives, validates, and freezes one immutable ordered `(SucceededCount, UnchangedCount, FailedCount, NotAttemptedCount)` tuple from those frozen established DIST result-row classifications rather than rendered or captured text. Every member is an explicitly typed nonnegative `Int64` in the inclusive range `0` through `9223372036854775807`; each established row contributes to exactly one member, every increment and the four-member sum use checked arithmetic, and that sum must equal the independently fixed established DIST result-row cardinality. Warning-only no-row slots, warnings, diagnostics, aggregates, cleanup facts, and queue entries without a result row contribute nothing. The tuple sum need not equal the discovered queue size, and each lexically distinct physical-alias row contributes independently through its own queue slot. No renderer or later finalizer re-derives a count or rereads rows. Only a conclusive normal boundary return with the exact immutable tuple and matching frozen inputs supplies count authority. A conclusive normal failed boundary outcome—whether it reports a missing, duplicate, conflicting, or undefined slot or classification, a mutable or misordered tuple, a negative or unrepresentable member, checked overflow, checked-sum mismatch, or another row/count invariant mismatch, or absorbs a contract-catchable arithmetic, tuple-construction, or validation exception inside that boundary—establishes the same invocation-global internal result-finalization stop before summary authority, preserves every factual row, warning-only no-row classification, fixed reason, continuity state, and repository, emits no summary, and returns exit `1`. That failure does not itself establish, clear, or replace `OutputTransportFailure`; any independently established earlier or later output failure retains its existing rule. A binder or outer-call failure, an exception escaping the boundary, boundary nonreturn, or unknown or unproved completion establishes neither tuple nor result-finalization stop and permits no summary, diagnostic, authoritative exit, or later action even if an incidental local tuple appears valid. Never wrap, clamp, saturate, retry, recount, or derive a replacement tuple from output. The summary is not a result row, contributes to none of its own counts, and receives its sole line terminator only from the stdout line writer. The exact case-sensitive, CR/LF-free COLLECT result-line set is closed to `COLLECT: result=SUCCEEDED` and `COLLECT: result=UNCHANGED`. The exact DIST set is closed to `DIST: path="<EscapedCandidatePath>" result=SUCCEEDED`, `DIST: path="<EscapedCandidatePath>" result=UNCHANGED`, `DIST: path="<EscapedCandidatePath>" result=FAILED`, `DIST: path="<EscapedCandidatePath>" result=NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)`, and `DIST: path="<EscapedCandidatePath>" result=NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. Field order, ASCII punctuation, spaces, token case, and outer quotes are fixed; placeholders are replaced rather than emitted, no optional field exists, and the stdout writer alone contributes one line terminator. `<EscapedCandidatePath>` uses only the discovery-frozen fully-qualified lexical candidate-path `System.String`. Traverse its UTF-16 code units without case change, separator change, Unicode normalization, canonicalization, physical re-resolution, or locator substitution: encode `"` as `\"`, `\` as `\\`, U+0000 through U+001F, U+007F, U+0085, U+2028, U+2029, and every unpaired surrogate as literal `\uXXXX` with four uppercase hexadecimal digits; copy valid surrogate pairs and every other code unit ordinally. This presentation escaping never admits an invalid or unproved candidate path and leaves no raw CR, LF, NEL, line separator, paragraph separator, unpaired surrogate, or unescaped quote or backslash. A `FileStreamConstructionFailed` safe-completion snapshot, or a `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary` snapshot when no earlier explicit reason existed, may supply only that existing reason token on later unstarted destination rows; neither emits a separate cancellation result, diagnostic, warning, count, aggregate, summary, notification, or other output, and neither has a public effect for a central slot or a last destination with no later unstarted row. Once the queue is fixed, cancellation before even the first destination turn still emits one `NOT ATTEMPTED (CANCELLED)` row for every queued candidate not already failed or warning-skipped. A candidate whose previously discovered marker and workspace are both absent at its turn instead appears only in the aggregate disappearance warning and contributes to none of the four result counts. If release of its acquired destination slot cannot be proved, that no-row classification and warning remain while one invocation-level ownership failure stops later candidates with exit `1`; a valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only its dedicated-boundary label, the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path uses only the label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, a capture-abnormal path reaches no public result rule, and every other conclusively classified path that reaches this public result rule uses `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. Collection reports its single output as `SUCCEEDED` or `UNCHANGED`; conclusive cancellation and a non-cancellation operation failure before target result commitment emit neither, and an invocation-level collection cleanup failure fabricates no `FAILED` row. Discarding prior safe collection-workspace contents is part of the current collection attempt rather than a fifth recovery state. If collection has already established a factual repository result but final central-slot release cannot be proved, it preserves that result line, does not roll back New, emits one separate invocation-level central-ownership failure, and returns exit `1`. Under the destination marker-disposition internal-stop rule, only a current destination without an established classification receives its existing phase-specific non-cancellation operation-failure `FAILED` result; no established row or no-row classification is rewritten. Otherwise, invocation-level ownership or cleanup failure does not fabricate or rewrite a collection or target row, so exit `1` does not imply that the `FAILED` count is nonzero. A destination-slot release failure after a factual result preserves that row, emits no synthetic `FAILED`, stops with exit `1`, and uses the terminal-specific first-stop rule: a valid `FileStreamConstructionFailed` path uses only its dedicated-boundary label, the exact non-FCF conclusive-normal-`false` path uses only the label preserved or selected by `NonFcfCloseHandleFalseSafeCompletionBoundary`, a capture-abnormal path reaches no public result rule, and every other conclusively classified path that reaches this public result rule reports later unstarted candidates as `NOT ATTEMPTED (GLOBAL FAILURE)`. Except for the three path-free diagnostics `FileStreamConstructionFailed`, `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure`, and `OutputTransportFailure` below, warnings and failures name relevant paths without attempting to infer which earlier replacement caused a disappearance. `UNCHANGED` requires the complete closed inventory and every raw file byte to equal the captured New package and causes no target rename or timestamp churn. Ordinary result lines use stdout and warnings or errors use stderr. The path-free ownership diagnostic has exactly four structural human-only forms after substituting values: `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure: scope=CENTRAL`, `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure: scope=DESTINATION`, `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure: scope=CENTRAL closeLastError=<CloseLastErrorValue>`, or `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure: scope=DESTINATION closeLastError=<CloseLastErrorValue>`. `<CloseLastErrorValue>` is exact `0x[0-9A-F]{8}` for a captured signed-`Int32` bit pattern, including `0x00000000`, or literal `UNKNOWN` for the absorbed contract-catchable code-unavailable terminal. The scope field is first, the optional last-error field is last, angle-bracket tokens are replaced rather than emitted, and the materialized string contains no CR or LF. This deterministic text is not a versioned parser schema, does not enter the separate `FileStreamConstructionFailed` item, and changes neither diagnostic cardinality nor any classification. The best-effort output-transport diagnostic has exactly one human-only form, the literal `OutputTransportFailure: stream=STDOUT`, and is eligible only after a stdout-originated `OutputTransportFailure` when no stderr failure is already conclusive. It is emitted only by terminal `OutputTransportDiagnosticCompleted`; `OutputTransportDiagnosticSuppressed` emits nothing, and `OutputTransportDiagnosticFailed` may leave no, partial, or complete advisory bytes but creates no recursive diagnostic or output attempt. `FileStreamConstructionFailed` owns one logical path-free internal-diagnostic slot and emits zero or one whole stderr item. When materialization succeeds, the item has exactly one of these human-only forms: `FileStreamConstructionFailed: stage=FILE_STREAM_CONSTRUCTION type=<TypeValue> hresult=<HResultValue>` or `FileStreamConstructionFailed: stage=FILE_STREAM_CONSTRUCTION type=<TypeValue> hresult=<HResultValue> | stage=SAFE_HANDLE_ONLY_DISPOSE type=<TypeValue> hresult=<HResultValue>`. `<TypeValue>` is the exact ordinal case-preserved, nonempty, CR/LF-free `Exception.GetType().FullName` or literal `UNKNOWN`; `<HResultValue>` is literal `0x` plus eight invariant uppercase hexadecimal digits for the exact `Int32` bit pattern or literal `UNKNOWN`. Stage is never `UNKNOWN`, and `hresult=UNKNOWN` never includes `0x`. Field fallback follows the terminal-specific rule above. Catchable whole-item assembly failure or conclusive `FcfOutputAbandonedBeforeWrite` emits zero whole items with no reassembly, fallback line, partial item, or materialization diagnostic; only terminal `FcfOutputCompleted` establishes one whole local item. Once `FcfOutputWriteInFlight` is conclusive, failure, deadline, nonreturn, or unproved completion uses `FcfOutputTransportFailed`, `OutputTransportFailure`, and the advisory partial-transcript rule, while pre-write abandonment never does. This deterministic support and test text remains human-only rather than a machine-readable schema. In every BAT mode the child PowerShell stdout and stderr handles are inherited unchanged. The BAT entry points preserve the PowerShell exit code across direct-interactive-console UI and return it immediately when that UI is disabled. Their optional neutral post-operation lines are `The operation has finished.` and `This window will close automatically in 1 minute. You may close the window or press CTRL+C.`; neither asserts success. If either BAT standard input or standard output is redirected or unproved, the wrapper adds no BAT-owned text or delay.

When initial screening admitted at least one actionable candidate but every candidate later becomes warning-skipped, distribution emits the aggregate warning, no target rows, and exact line `DIST: summary succeeded=0 unchanged=0 failed=0 notAttempted=0`, then returns exit `0` if no failure or cancellation exists and all required ownership releases are conclusive. It synthesizes no `FAILED`, `UNCHANGED`, or `NOT ATTEMPTED` result, and any verified workspace-supersession information remains count-free. This late zero-row result is not the initial zero-target configuration failure. Existing cancellation, failure, and ownership-uncertainty precedence remains unchanged.

Post-result cleanup may remain warning-only and preserve the ordinary exit only within a strict cleanup-only boundary: the canonical repository must exactly match verified current New or unchanged bytes and closed inventory, commitment or restoration must be conclusive, the applicable OS lease must satisfy its lease-specific required-release proof, and, at outcome classification, every retained cleanup artifact must be proved non-authoritative, physically bounded, ordinary, and unchanged from its captured identity. For collection, this boundary covers a proved safe `CollectionTransactionWorkspace` that became non-authoritative after the invocation committed current New or `UNCHANGED`; a later invocation never treats its contents as historical authority and may discard it only after accepting its own stable current authoring snapshot. Pre-result cleanup uncertainty, a case-only or multiple workspace match, a non-directory or reparse entry, an escaping boundary, or identity uncertainty remains failure. A workspace retained after failed publication and controlled restoration remains pre-result failure evidence and never qualifies for the post-result warning-only boundary, even when target restoration itself is conclusive. For a distribution destination, every workspace cleanup attempt occurs while the destination-slot lease remains held; after cleanup ends, that candidate releases the lease and the completed attempt never touches the workspace again. A later lexical candidate that proves the same physical slot may independently reacquire the lease and treat the safe workspace as its latest-wins input. If the current New or `UNCHANGED` result is committed, release succeeds, and a proved ordinary, contained, recursively reparse-free workspace remains, it is warning-only; on the next invocation it remains disposable latest-wins repair input rather than a historical-record hard gate. A destination workspace retained because publication failed or restoration, resulting state, or filesystem cleanup could not be proved remains evidence for the candidate's single `FAILED` result and never qualifies for this post-result warning-only boundary. A CommonModules lease marker enters this warning-only boundary only when its terminal same-handle observation and cleanup authority were conclusive, its one disposition attempt established an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome`, the contract's closed warning-eligible pre-transition cause set admitted that exact cause, and the still-unarmed handle was then conclusively released; retention after `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` or unproved release never enters this boundary. Distribution reports an invocation-owned central snapshot separately at invocation scope. During cancellation before stable source-snapshot acceptance, retaining only a proved invocation-owned, disjoint, non-authoritative snapshot workspace likewise remains a warning using the invocation-scope identity-safe retained-locator presentation and does not replace exit `130`. Uncertainty about snapshot ownership, transaction-workspace containment, reparse state, open handles, or central-lease release remains invocation failure. When the New or `UNCHANGED` result is already committed, that invocation failure changes the exit and adds an ownership diagnostic but does not retract the factual result line or roll back the repository. Each non-marker cleanup receives bounded transient retries whose count and delay are internal and has no option; CommonModules marker disposition remains one-shot and has no retry option. After result commitment, cancellation may stop optional cleanup only once the retained artifact already satisfies the applicable warning boundary; destination distribution makes that classification while its destination-slot lease remains held, then releases ownership, after which that completed candidate attempt never touches the workspace again. One target aggregates its retained transaction paths in deterministic order into one warning, while a retained central snapshot has one separate warning. At outcome classification, a missing artifact counts as cleaned; a replaced, reparse, unreadable, out-of-bounds, or otherwise unproved artifact outside a proved disposable workspace remains failure or manual-only rather than warning. A presentation-only locator change observed after that classification follows the separate final-diagnostic identity rule and never retroactively changes the established outcome. `RepositorySlotOwnershipContinuityFailure` is the overriding exception: an affected artifact never enters this cleanup-only warning boundary, receives no terminal filesystem re-observation, and is not mutated after the latch.

Before inspecting or resetting pre-existing collection transaction state, collection acquires the central slot's fixed sibling lease and captures, validates, and live-rechecks the complete current authoring snapshot while leaving the canonical target and every case-insensitive `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.collect-workspace` locator match unchanged. Snapshot invalidity, instability, cancellation before admission, or inability to prove source or central ownership stops unchanged. After snapshot acceptance, collection enumerates those direct sibling matches. Zero matches permits creation of a fresh ordinal-exact ordinary workspace; exactly one ordinal-exact ordinary non-reparse directory may be recursively proved physically contained and reparse-free, then reset as wholly disposable. A case-only spelling, more than one match, a non-directory entry, reparse root or descendant, escaping boundary, or unproved containment or identity leaves every match untouched and fails before workspace or target mutation. Collection creates every fresh current-attempt record, staging directory, and backup destination with create-new semantics inside the proved workspace and uses only the accepted snapshot to compare, replace, or recreate the canonical target. Prior contents, record syntax, phase, ID, fingerprint, stored paths, orphan status, names, and contradictions are not interpreted and grant no preservation right. Collection never follows a stored path or deletes outside the workspace. If current New or `UNCHANGED` is committed but workspace cleanup cannot finish, the factual result and established cleanup-warning or uncertainty rule applies; no retained workspace content becomes a later historical-recovery hard gate.

Distribution has no corresponding historical-record hard gate. Before destination comparison or mutation it acquires that slot's lease, proves the exact `DistributionTransactionWorkspace` boundary when present, and uses the accepted current central snapshot to discard and reseed every safe workspace child without interpreting old record contents. Fresh current-attempt staging uses create-new semantics inside the workspace and is verified before destructive target mutation. Failure to prove or safely reset the reserved workspace makes only that candidate `FAILED`; it never authorizes deletion outside the workspace. A marker retained after an `UnarmedMarkerDispositionOutcome` and conclusive required release remains reusable and never constitutes busy ownership by itself.

Immediately before a distribution candidate's first destructive target mutation, the still-held destination-slot lease does not by itself carry earlier path authority forward. Distribution freshly proves that the lexical candidate path still reaches the same pinned physical slot, that the canonical-root locator set contains either exactly one accepted root or zero matches while the exact safe `DistributionTransactionWorkspace` repair locator remains, that the slot remains physically disjoint from central and recursively reparse-free, and that every current-attempt artifact is contained inside the proved workspace. A safe workspace locator may authorize recreation only when its own canonical-root locator has a proved zero-match absent state; it never authorizes another path. Because current-attempt workspace mutation and verified staging precede this final fence, an absent, unprovable, or retargeted locator no longer qualifies for the aggregate disappearance warning. Distribution remains bound to the pinned slot, neither retries nor retargets, and uses the established non-cancellation pre-target failure cleanup with one `FAILED` result; known unsafe evidence uses the same failure boundary. Warning-only disappearance with no row remains available only when the discontinuity is established before the first workspace-reseed or current-transaction filesystem mutation, under the earlier destination-slot release rule.

Ordinary distribution target authority is only an existing exact `common_modules_repo` marker observed beneath an immediate child of the resolved search root. An exact safe `common_modules_repo.vba-dev.dist-workspace` locator contributes its derived lexical path to the initial candidate queue and temporarily authorizes latest-wins recreation of only its own proved zero-match absent canonical target after interruption. The workspace never creates permanent opt-in: after successful cleanup, a later absence with no new workspace is not recreated. Distribution does not infer another destination from a `vba-project.json`, repository name, arbitrary sibling layout, or heuristic. The canonical central source path is normally excluded; any other candidate whose physical repository boundary equals, contains, or is contained by the central boundary is an unchanged `FAILED` result. The zero-target configuration failure applies only when initial discovery and screening yield neither a usable candidate path nor a known candidate-local `FAILED` result, and that branch emits no summary. A candidate path that disappears during later sequential processing is warning-skipped and does not retroactively create zero-target failure. A future workflow that creates ordinary destinations requires an explicit distribution-target registry and separate contract. Collection remains allowed to create its explicitly selected canonical output when absent.

Collection and distribution never follow a symbolic link, junction, or other reparse point as authoring source, canonical repository, repository-slot lease marker, generated-package content, staging, backup, transaction evidence, or transaction-workspace entry. Initial distribution screening normally excludes the canonical central source path, but records any other candidate whose physical repository boundary overlaps that source boundary, any candidate whose canonical target or recursive target inventory contains a reparse entry, or any conclusively present workspace locator whose root or static boundary is unsafe, as unchanged `FAILED`; later candidates continue. Initial distribution screening does not enter a workspace. After stable current-source acceptance and central-slot ownership, collection recursively proves the complete `CollectionTransactionWorkspace` subtree physically contained and reparse-free before reset or target mutation. After stable central-snapshot acceptance and destination-slot lease acquisition, distribution applies the same proof to the complete `DistributionTransactionWorkspace` subtree and repeats the target and central-disjointness checks. Distribution deliberately performs no pairwise physical identity, alias, or containment graph across destination candidates. Lexically distinct candidates may therefore reach the same physical slot and be attempted separately, or an earlier replace-all may make a later nested path disappear; the latter is warning-skipped without causal attribution. Neither workspace reset follows a stored path or deletes through changed, escaping, or unproved evidence.

The complete repository graph also rejects every direct dependency edge from a runtime role (`testOnly: false`) to a test role (`testOnly: true`). Such an edge would let build and test include both entries while publish retained a dependent whose required source it excluded. Runtime-to-runtime, test-to-runtime, and test-to-test edges remain valid; checking every direct edge is sufficient because every transitive runtime-to-test path contains a first violating direct edge. Collection and the stable repository-snapshot validation used by `new excel`, CommonModules Add, and CommonModules Update fail before project mutation. Build and publish continue to trust the manifest-recorded installed classifications and do not gain a repository-consistency check.

Initial CommonModules use one deterministic order throughout creation without pretending that every member of a dependency cycle can precede its dependent. The complete repository graph is collapsed into maximal strongly connected **CommonModuleDependencyComponent** values. A self-dependency remains invalid. Direct roots follow canonical repository row order, and selecting any component member includes the whole component while `requested` remains a per-entry fact. Each component enumerates outgoing dependency components by scanning its members in repository row order and each member's `Dependencies` in declaration order, ignoring intra-component edges and retaining the first occurrence of every external component. Root traversal is depth-first over that order, emits dependency components before their dependent component, and emits members inside each component in canonical repository row order. A case-insensitive entry identity enters the sequence only at its first encounter; encountering it later as a direct root changes its final `requested` fact to `true` without moving it. Source copy, the committed manifest `commonModules` array, text output, and nested-manifest JSON all use this same sequence. `new excel` does not apply a separate alphabetical root or presentation sort. The runtime-to-test dependency rule above necessarily rejects a component mixing runtime and test roles.

When the canonical CommonModules repository exists, `new excel` captures one complete stable repository snapshot into invocation-owned staging after it owns the project lease and has established initial-target safety but before creating project artifacts. The capture includes the repository manifest and every selected `.bas`, `.cls`, `.frm`, and matching `.frx` source unit, then re-reads the selected live inputs and fails unchanged if the repository changed during capture or any unit is incomplete. Root selection, dependency closure, RequiredReferences, `testOnly`, copied source bytes, and the planned manifest are all derived solely from the validated staged bytes. Later repository changes do not alter that invocation, and the command neither mixes live reads with staged data nor automatically retries into another generation. After every snapshot handle is released, deletion receives bounded retries. A retained staging workspace before the initial manifest success boundary belongs to failure or cancellation cleanup and never becomes a success warning; after committed creation, a retained workspace is non-authoritative housekeeping state and preserves success with the established warning below. Only a repository that is conclusively absent before capture uses the warning-bearing no-CommonModules path; inaccessible, invalid, or unstable repository state remains a failure.

Common option aliases follow .NET CLI conventions where they do not create ambiguous command surfaces. `--name` has `-n`, `--output` has `-o`, `--document` has `-d`, and `--format` has `-f`. `--project` has no short alias because it is a context selector rather than an output or resource name. `--from` and `--to` also have no short aliases because `-f` belongs to `--format` and `--to` is intentionally distinct from `--output`.

`new excel` defaults to `--format text`, accepts `--format json` with the common `-f` alias, and advertises output schema version `1.0` through capabilities. The extension-owned required CLI contract includes `commandSchemaVersions["new excel"]: "1.0"` before guided creation can invoke it. Its JSON result is an exhaustive projection of the committed initial project state rather than a project-path receipt: it includes the created document and source template, every installed CommonModule with its final `requested`, `testOnly`, and `orphaned` facts, and every selected reference with its final `requested` fact. The command emits one `complete: true` object only after project creation and mandatory ownership cleanup are trusted; failure or cancellation emits no partial success object. A guided flow uses this result directly and does not run follow-up list commands or reinterpret the created manifest to discover the outcome.

The schema `1.0` result has required top-level properties in producer order: `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, `scope: "project"`, the normalized absolute **RequestedProjectRoot** as `project`, primary `document`, `operation: "new"`, `template: "excel"`, `complete: true`, `warnings`, the requested-root-relative normalized absolute `manifestPath`, and `manifest`. The nested `manifest` is the complete schema-`1` **ProjectManifest** object that was committed, not a second command-specific projection: it retains its numeric manifest `schemaVersion`, project and primary-document names, complete document map, any selected repository and explicit command defaults, project-relative `/`-separated paths, and the final ordered CommonModules and reference arrays. The output schema version and nested manifest schema version are independent contracts. Consumers ignore object-property order but preserve and validate array order. The result adds no duplicated project name, absolute source-template field, created-file inventory, aggregate count, hash, **ProjectRootIdentity**, lease identity, or temporary path; callers resolve committed relative paths against `project` when needed.

The initial schema `1.0` `new excel` producer has exactly three success-warning codes, emits each at most once, and orders present entries by fixed rank. First, `commonModulesRepositoryNotFound` uses `CommonModules repository was not found; the project was created without shared modules.` only when the canonical sibling repository is conclusively absent; the committed manifest then has no repository selection or CommonModules and contains only the baseline workbook references. Second, `commonModulesSnapshotCleanupFailed` uses `The project was created, but its non-authoritative CommonModules snapshot workspace could not be removed: "{P}".`, where `P` is the normalized absolute retained staging-root path and every snapshot handle is already proved released. Third, `leaseMarkerCleanupFailed` uses `The project was created and its project lease was released, but the lease marker could not be removed: "{P}".`, where `P` is the normalized absolute marker path and release ownership is already proved. Every warning object requires nonempty string `code` and `message` properties and may carry unknown additive properties. JSON carries warnings only in its result array and duplicates none to stderr; text writes them to stderr. Consumers remain open to unknown future warning codes.

An inaccessible, unreadable, invalid, incomplete, or resolution-failing repository is not equivalent to absence and fails creation. `newProjectTargetChanged`, `newProjectCleanupIncomplete`, `manifestMutationBusy`, missing-reference resolution failure, and inability to prove owned Excel-process or lease release are likewise nonzero failures without a success object. Cancellation before the atomic initial manifest move rolls back under the established creation contract; after that move, project success wins the late request without `cancellationDeferred` or another cancellation warning.

Default text output is a human-readable creation receipt rather than the former single line. It starts with `Created Excel VBA project "{projectName}".`, then prints the normalized absolute **RequestedProjectRoot** as `Project`, its manifest path as `Manifest`, the document name, and project-relative `Source set`, `Source template`, `Build target`, and `Publish target` paths. The latter two are explicitly targets because creation does not materialize bin or publish workbooks. Ordered `CommonModules` lines label each final entry `requested` or `dependency` and show its name and module file; ordered `References` lines label each entry `requested` or `CommonModules` and show its stored name. A zero-entry section prints `(none)` rather than disappearing. The final summary reports total and provenance counts for both collections with grammatical singular or plural wording. This presentation preserves manifest array order, derives no additional machine contract, exposes no **ProjectRootIdentity**, and leaves warnings on stderr; automation uses JSON.

The guided creation caller invokes `new excel --format json` exactly once and validates the result before claiming success. It requires the fixed schema, scope, operation, template, and completion discriminants; request-matching normalized project root and manifest path; agreement between top-level `document` and the nested manifest's primary document; and the internally consistent initial Excel-document, path, CommonModules, reference, ordered-set, and warning shapes. The command envelope and each warning object are additive-open, and unknown warning codes remain compatible, but their additions cannot relax a known required property, discriminant, ordering rule, or warning-to-state invariant. Each warning still requires nonempty string `code` and `message` properties. Recognized warnings are unique and require their exact messages and state relationships; after unknown codes are filtered out, the recognized-code subsequence must retain the established fixed order. Unknown warnings may appear anywhere, remain in received order, contribute to the displayed warning count, and are never deduplicated, sorted, recased, or reinterpreted. The nested `manifest` is different: it must satisfy the closed, exact-case schema-1 **ProjectManifestSchema** at every owned object level, so any unknown manifest property makes an exit-zero result untrusted. The consumer does not reconstruct the repository dependency closure or VBE reference resolution, reread the manifest to rediscover the outcome, or run List or Doctor as a result check.

That trusted-result check is exact for facts derivable from the submitted request and the initial template contract. The envelope must carry `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, `scope: "project"`, `operation: "new"`, `template: "excel"`, `complete: true`, the requested **RequestedProjectRoot**, its root-level `vba-project.json` as `manifestPath`, and the submitted project name as `document`. The nested manifest must use numeric schema version `1`, repeat that exact name as `projectName`, `primaryDocument`, and the sole document key, omit `commandDefaults`, and define that one document as `kind: "excel"` with exact paths `src/<name>`, `src/<name>/<name>.xlsm`, `bin/<name>.xlsm`, and `publish/<name>.xlsm`. `commonModules` and `references` must both be present. CommonModule and reference entries must satisfy their required-field, case-insensitive uniqueness, ordered-set, and final-state invariants, and every initial CommonModule must have `orphaned: false`.

The repository-warning relationship is also locally provable. `commonModulesRepositoryNotFound` requires an omitted `commonModulesRepository` and an empty CommonModules array; a selected canonical sibling repository requires exact manifest spelling `../common_modules_repo`. Guided creation does not independently read the repository, source template, CommonModules source bytes, registry, or VBE to verify which baseline references, root categories, dependency closure, RequiredReferences union, canonical reference identities, classifications, or source bytes the CLI selected. Those planner and resolver results remain the exhaustive CLI projection's authority rather than a second TypeScript implementation.

The initial VS Code entry for that workflow is command ID `vbaTools.newExcel` with the user-visible title `VBA Tools: Create Excel VBA Project`. It remains visible in an Empty Window, an ordinary workspace, and a workspace that already contains one or more workbook-backed projects; the initial contribution has no context-dependent `when` clause. Availability therefore does not imply that the active editor already belongs to a project. Invocation begins with the environment preflight below, and the extension continues to delegate authoritative creation to `vba-dev new excel` rather than automating Excel itself.

The extension declares limited Restricted Mode support. Source viewing and language assistance that executes no workspace VBA and launches neither `vba-dev` nor Excel may remain available, while `vbaTools.devtool.path` is an untrusted-workspace restricted configuration and every managed `vba-dev`, Excel/VBIDE automation, Doctor, debug-adapter, and vba-dev-terminal launch is blocked before **CompanionExecutableResolution**. `VBA Tools: Create Excel VBA Project` remains discoverable in Restricted Mode, but invocation first shows one warning, `Excel VBA project creation is unavailable in Restricted Mode because it starts vba-dev and Microsoft Excel. Trust this workspace or run the command from a trusted Empty Window.`, with ordered actions Manage Workspace Trust and Open Empty Window. Dismissal does nothing. Neither action trusts a workspace implicitly or resumes creation in either window, and no process or Output entry is created. Detecting Restricted Mode at command entry discards any prior CompanionExecutableResolution and reusable guided-preflight pass for that window; a later trusted invocation starts with fresh executable resolution and Doctor. The extension gates each invocation with the current VS Code trust value. The Workspace Trust API exposes an in-process grant event but no revocation event, so a window reload, extension deactivation, or process loss that removes trust while a child remains active follows the established abrupt caller-loss and crash-recovery contract rather than being reclassified as cooperative cancellation.

Guided creation is single-flight per VS Code window from command entry through every preflight Retry cycle, project-name and parent-folder input, path-reentry cycle, `new excel` execution, and terminal-result classification. Invoking the command again during that interval starts no second preflight or creation, queues nothing, does not change or focus the active UI, and shows one actionless information notification: `Excel VBA project creation is already in progress in this window.` Ownership ends when input or preflight is cancelled; a blocking preflight notification is dismissed or chooses an action other than Retry; or creation reaches success, failure, cancellation, or untrusted-result classification. A terminal notification or later navigation action may remain visible after ownership ends. Other VS Code windows remain independent, while an actual same-target collision remains the CLI's **ProjectManifestMutationLease** responsibility.

Before asking for a project name or output folder, guided creation runs `vba-dev doctor --scope environment --format json` under cancellable progress. This explicit scope performs no upward manifest discovery and checks only project-automation prerequisites: supported Windows, Excel COM startup, actual `VBProject` access through the active Excel/VBIDE environment, and establishment and cleanup of the owned automation process. It does not run `vba-debug-adapter doctor`, inspect native VBE debugging, change Trust Center or registry settings, or create a project artifact. Every required check must pass in a complete trusted Doctor result. A failed, unverified, incomplete, or malformed result ends the creation flow before input and offers Open Setup Instructions, Retry, and Show Output; there is no Run Anyway action. Cancelling the preflight is silent. Passing this early UX check does not replace `new excel`'s authoritative validation against the Excel process used for actual creation.

The environment-scope result contains exactly one of each required check in this fixed order: `platform.windows`, `excel.comStartup`, `excel.processOwnership`, `excel.vbideProjectAccess`, and `excel.processCleanup`. A check that cannot run after a conclusive prerequisite failure is still present with `status: "skipped"`. If an Excel process was started, `excel.processCleanup` is attempted and reported regardless of the VBIDE-access outcome. Guided creation binds to these stable IDs rather than their human-readable messages. A missing, duplicate, reordered, or additional row makes the evidence incomplete or untrusted. Proceeding requires every required check and the overall result to be `pass` with `complete: true`.

The project CLI's Doctor schema `1.0` carries request context explicitly. Environment scope has `scope: "environment"` and `project: null`; normal project scope has `scope: "project"` and the normalized canonical absolute project root, with no selected-document field. Guided creation rejects any other pair before interpreting check outcomes. The independent debug-adapter Doctor keeps its own schema and does not acquire these project-CLI fields.

For every blocking environment-preflight result, Open Setup Instructions opens a rendered VS Code Markdown preview of the installed extension's version-matched `README.md` at Getting Started > 2 - Prepare Excel in the current window. It does not open the raw Markdown editor, external browser, or a network-hosted latest version, and it reuses an already open preview when possible. It does not route different check IDs to different pages or require network access; the bundled section covers supported Windows and desktop Excel, trusted VBIDE access, and process-ownership or cleanup troubleshooting, and may link onward to Microsoft documentation that opens externally only after the user selects it. The failed stable IDs and complete evidence remain in VBA Tools Output. Opening or dismissing the instructions does not launch Excel, open Trust Center, change the registry, or retry; only an explicit Retry action starts a fresh preflight. If preview navigation fails, the preflight outcome remains blocking and the extension shows one action-failure notification, `VBA Tools could not open the Excel VBA setup instructions.`, with Retry and Show Output. Retry repeats only that preview navigation.

A blocking preflight produces exactly one terminal notification after the progress notification closes. A trusted, complete overall `warning` uses a warning notification, `Excel VBA project prerequisites need attention.` A trusted overall `fail` or `unverified`, or any trusted result with `complete: false`, uses an error notification, `Excel VBA project prerequisites are not ready.` Missing, malformed, schema- or request-context-mismatched, internally inconsistent, or abnormally terminated Doctor output uses an error notification, `VBA Tools could not verify Excel VBA project prerequisites.` In all three cases the ordered actions are Open Setup Instructions, Retry, and Show Output. The notification does not enumerate checks, counts, or messages, and VBA Tools Output remains closed unless selected. A status aggregation inconsistent with its checks is untrusted rather than accepted according to its apparent overall status. Explicit preflight cancellation remains silent and produces no terminal notification.

A local preflight cancellation request immediately commits the guided workflow not to open project input and makes that invocation's result ineligible for the reusable preflight pass, but the extension still waits through the child `close` event. Exit `130` is silent. If cancellation was requested first and the command nevertheless returns exit `0` with a trusted complete `pass` or `warning`, including when the stdin cancellation frame could not be delivered, the workflow also ends silently without caching the result. Exit `0` with untrusted output, exit `1`, another nonzero result, or abnormal termination retains the ordinary blocking notification even after a local cancellation request, so cancellation cannot hide Doctor infrastructure, result-integrity, or cleanup failures. Exit `130` is silent even when no extension-local request was observed. Thus the local request owns whether guided input may continue, while the terminal result still owns whether a failure must be surfaced.

The extension may reuse a guided-creation environment preflight only when its Doctor result is schema-valid and trusted, has `complete: true` and overall `status: "pass"`, and contains every required environment check with `status: "pass"`. A complete `warning` result remains valid Doctor output for general consumers, but it is not a successful **GuidedProjectCreationPreflight** and is neither reusable nor allowed to proceed to project input. The extension binds an eligible pass to the current immutable **CompanionExecutableResolution** result for this VS Code window's Extension Host activation, rather than treating the executable path, `toolVersion`, or `contractVersion` as cache identity. Any new companion-executable resolution invalidates the pass even when it selects the same absolute path and reports the same versions. The pass is not persisted or shared with another window, and an Extension Host reload discards it. Starting Retry first invalidates any reusable pass and always starts a fresh Doctor invocation; a warning, failure, unverified or skipped required check, incomplete or cancelled invocation, or malformed result leaves no reusable pass. Reuse only avoids a redundant early Excel probe; `new excel` still validates the actual Excel process used for creation and fails safely if readiness changed afterward.

After project input begins, cancelling the name, folder, or pre-invocation path flow preserves an existing eligible pass. A trusted `new excel` success, including a warning-bearing or late-cancellation success, also preserves it, as does exit `130` after creation rollback and mandatory cleanup are proved. Exit `1`, any other nonzero or abnormal termination, and exit `0` with an untrusted creation result invalidate it because the extension cannot distinguish a target or repository problem from environment degradation without parsing failure prose. Creation success does not manufacture or refresh a Doctor pass: it only retains the original result because the creation receipt does not contain the five environment-check proofs. Cancellation-frame delivery follows the terminal outcome. All earlier CompanionExecutableResolution, Retry, activation, persistence, and cross-window invalidation rules remain authoritative.

After preflight, guided creation asks for the project name first and then asks the user to select its parent directory. It constructs the exact project root as the child `<parent>/<project-name>` and invokes `vba-dev new excel --name <project-name> --output <exact-project-root> --format json`; it does not rely on the process working directory or CLI name derivation. This happy path deliberately aligns the project-root folder name, manifest project name, primary document name, and workbook base name. Those are tooling and filesystem identities and do not rename `VBProject.Name`, worksheet or workbook document modules, or other VBA identities. Direct CLI callers retain independent `--name` and `--output` control when they need different names.

Guided creation and direct CLI creation share **ProjectCreationPathValidation**, an exact version-`1` pre-artifact contract that composes a host-neutral **ProjectNameLexicalContract** with the host-specific **ExcelWorkbookPathContract**. Future Word, PowerPoint, or Access creation may reuse the lexical layer without inheriting Excel's path restrictions. A project name is interpreted as its original well-formed UTF-16 sequence: an unpaired surrogate is invalid, while valid surrogate pairs are retained; NFC, NFD, trimming, sanitization, case conversion, and every other spelling rewrite are forbidden. Unicode and internal whitespace are allowed, but either a leading or trailing member of the fixed Unicode `White_Space` set (`U+0009`-`U+000D`, `U+0020`, `U+0085`, `U+00A0`, `U+1680`, `U+2000`-`U+200A`, `U+2028`-`U+2029`, `U+202F`, `U+205F`, and `U+3000`) is invalid. The exact Unicode control ranges `U+0000`-`U+001F` and `U+007F`-`U+009F` are invalid; format characters outside those ranges remain permitted.

`vba-dev capabilities --format json` advertises that shared request-validation contract as `featureVersions["projectCreation.pathValidation"]: "1.0"`, and the extension-owned CLI contract requires that exact version before it performs guided validation or invokes creation. The feature version covers project-name preservation and rejection, Excel bracket handling, 218-UTF-16-code-unit measurement, first-reason precedence, and the shared cross-runtime validation corpus. It does not cover guided UI wording, target transaction semantics, or the `new excel` success payload; those remain extension behavior, project-command behavior, and `commandSchemaVersions["new excel"]`, respectively. A configured CLI that omits or mismatches the required feature follows the established session-wide bundled fallback, and creation does not start if the bundled CLI also fails compatibility validation.

The same name must be a single Windows basename. It is invalid when empty; equal to `.` or `..`; containing `<`, `>`, `:`, `"`, `/`, `\`, `|`, `?`, or `*`; or ending in a dot. The portion before the first dot is compared with `OrdinalIgnoreCase` against exactly `CON`, `PRN`, `AUX`, `NUL`, `COM1`-`COM9`, `COM¹`-`COM³`, `LPT1`-`LPT9`, and `LPT¹`-`LPT³`, so an extension-like suffix such as `NUL.tar.gz` does not make the name valid. This comparison recognizes a reserved spelling but never rewrites the input. The name is not a VBA identifier, so MS-VBAL identifier rules and the 31-code-point module-name limit do not apply.

**ProjectNameLexicalContract** returns only the first applicable stable reason in this fixed order: `projectNameEmpty`, `projectNameIllFormedUnicode`, `projectNameDotSegment`, `projectNameContainsPathSeparator`, `projectNameContainsWindowsInvalidCharacter`, `projectNameContainsUnicodeControlCharacter`, `projectNameHasLeadingOrTrailingWhitespace`, `projectNameEndsWithDot`, and `projectNameUsesReservedDeviceName`. Separating path separators from the remaining Windows-invalid characters and checking Unicode controls before boundary whitespace makes overlapping inputs deterministic; for example, a tab is reported as a control character rather than whitespace, `.` as a dot segment rather than a trailing dot, and `CON.` as a trailing dot before reserved-device recognition.

Guided name input maps those reasons to exact inline messages without showing the identifiers: `Enter a project name.`; `Project name contains an invalid Unicode sequence.`; `Project name cannot be "." or "..".`; `Project name cannot contain "/" or "\\".`; `Project name contains a character that Windows does not allow in a file or folder name.`; `Project name cannot contain control characters.`; `Project name cannot start or end with whitespace.`; `Project name cannot end with a dot.`; and `Project name cannot use a reserved Windows device name, even with an extension.`, respectively. The Excel-layer name message is `Project name cannot contain "[" or "]" because Excel does not reliably support them in workbook paths.` All are blocking InputBox errors and none alters the entered spelling.

**ExcelWorkbookPathContract** first rejects `[` or `]` in any Excel-facing path component with `excelPathContainsUnsupportedCharacter`, then rejects a path beyond its length limit with `excelPathTooLong`. Because every new Excel project uses its project name in folder and workbook components, guided creation applies the first check to the name while the InputBox is still open rather than pretending brackets are Windows-invalid basename characters. After parent-folder selection, it lexically normalizes the exact absolute project root without resolving symlinks or substituting short names and checks the derived `src/<name>/<name>.xlsm`, `bin/<name>.xlsm`, and `publish/<name>.xlsm` absolute paths. A bracket inherited from the selected parent rejects that parent without rewriting it. Each complete path must contain no more than 218 UTF-16 code units, including its drive or UNC prefix, separators, basename, and `.xlsm` extension but excluding a terminating NUL. A supplementary Unicode scalar therefore contributes two units. This UTF-16 unit is the deterministic product interpretation of Excel's documented inclusive 218-character limit because the Excel documentation does not specify a Unicode counting unit; Windows Excel integration pins BMP, supplementary-character, UNC, and 217/218/219-boundary cases.

A guided path violation starts no CLI process and creates no artifact. A project-name violation, including an Excel bracket inherited from the name, remains in the InputBox with the corresponding human-readable reason. A bracket inherited only from the selected parent opens a modal error dialog, `The selected parent folder cannot be used because its path contains "[" or "]", which Excel does not reliably support.`, with ordered actions Choose Another Parent and Cancel; Change Name is absent because it cannot correct that path. A 218-unit violation opens a modal error dialog, `One or more generated workbook paths exceed Excel's 218-character limit.`, with ordered actions Choose Another Parent, Change Name, and Cancel because either input can shorten the result. Dismissing either dialog is Cancel. Choose Another Parent retains the name and uses the rejected or overlong parent only as the next folder dialog's initial location. Change Name retains the parent, reopens the prior name selected for editing, and after a valid edit revalidates that parent directly rather than forcing a redundant folder selection. It adds no generic `MAX_PATH` 260 rule and does not try to bypass Excel with an extended `\\?\` path. `new excel` repeats the Excel-facing rules and remains authoritative for the complete pre-artifact plan, including actual target-volume component limits rather than a hard-coded 255, CommonModules paths, lease and temporary paths, and every other filesystem or Excel restriction.

The TypeScript and .NET test suites consume one language-neutral version-`1` JSON vector corpus whose distinct project-name and Excel-workbook-path sections contain ordinary string cases, raw UTF-16-code-unit cases for isolated surrogates, derived-path lengths, and the expected first stable reason. Neither implementation may substitute its runtime's evolving trim, Unicode-category, or platform filename helpers for that contract. The reason identifiers are validation and support vocabulary, not a machine-readable `new excel` failure envelope: CLI validation failure writes the same first reason and human-readable explanation to stderr with no success JSON, while the Extension obtains its own structured validation result and never parses CLI stderr.

For a name failure, CLI stderr prefixes the corresponding exact InputBox sentence with `{reason}: `. For an Excel-path failure, both direct CLI stderr and guided creation's still-closed VBA Tools Output identify only the first offending normalized absolute path in fixed source-template, bin, then publish order. The bracket line is `excelPathContainsUnsupportedCharacter: Excel workbook path contains "[" or "]", which Excel does not reliably support: "{P}".` The length line is `excelPathTooLong: Excel workbook path exceeds the 218-character limit ({N} UTF-16 code units): "{P}".` Guided dialogs do not expose the path, measured units, reason identifier, or UTF-16 terminology and do not add Show Output; the evidence remains available when the user opens the channel independently.

An absent target root and a pre-existing empty directory are both eligible without confirmation. The extension does not enumerate or independently classify the target as empty, marker-only, reusable, conflicting, or unreadable, because that observation is racy and cannot establish lease ownership. It invokes `new excel` and leaves existing-manifest, foreign-content, marker, concurrent-creator, and final emptiness authority to the **ProjectManifestMutationLease** and **InitialProjectTarget** transaction. A rejected target follows the ordinary single creation failure with Show Output. Guided creation offers no Overwrite, Use Anyway, merge, adoption, cleanup, or automatic deletion action.

The Project Name InputBox uses title `Create Excel VBA Project`, prompt `Enter a project name. The project folder, document, and workbook will use this name.`, no initial value, and `MyProject` only as a placeholder; it neither derives a value from the workspace nor loads input history. Its initial untouched blank state does not pre-emptively show a red error. Attempting to accept blank begins the blocking `Enter a project name.` error, and after the user starts editing, the shared lexical and Excel-name checks validate live with Error severity. The input is ordinary visible text, has no Back or history control, never trims, normalizes, sanitizes, or recases spelling, and advances only with a valid value. The prompt deliberately does not imply that `VBProject.Name`, worksheet identity, or host-component identity follows the project name. The following VS Code standard folder dialog uses `Select Parent Folder for "{name}"` as its title and `Create Here` as its confirmation label. Its initial location is the `file:` workspace folder containing the active resource, otherwise the sole `file:` workspace folder when exactly one exists, otherwise the dialog's default. Because the standard dialog has no workflow Back control and does not distinguish Cancel from Escape, either action there, or cancellation of the name input, silently ends the whole workflow before CLI invocation; the extension does not wrap Cancel as Back.

Guided creation accepts as its parent only a `file:` URI that yields a Windows filesystem path available to the same local CLI process. Drive paths and UNC paths are eligible. Remote and virtual resources are never guessed, converted, downloaded, or copied into a local project root, and a non-`file:` workspace folder or active resource is not an initial-location candidate. A remote or virtual workspace does not by itself hide or disable the command because the user may still select an eligible filesystem folder. If the folder dialog defensively returns a non-`file:` URI, guided creation starts no CLI, retains the project name, and shows the modal error `Select a folder on the Windows file system. Remote and virtual workspace folders cannot be used to create an Excel VBA project.` with ordered actions Choose Another Parent and Cancel. Dismissal means Cancel; the rejected URI is not reused as a dialog location, and Change Name and Show Output are absent because neither can make that resource eligible.

The flow persists name and parent only within that one invocation. A parent-bracket rejection offers Choose Another Parent and Cancel. A 218-unit rejection offers Choose Another Parent, Change Name, and Cancel: the first reopens the folder dialog at the last parent, and the second reopens the name input with the prior spelling selected for editing before directly revalidating the retained parent. Cancellation, successful completion, or any later invocation retains no name or parent history. Once the derived path is valid, the extension shows no separate confirmation screen and immediately enters cancellable progress for the CLI invocation; target transaction safety makes a confirmation-style overwrite grant both unnecessary and misleading.

An uncached environment preflight uses one cancellable, indeterminate notification titled `VBA Tools: Checking Excel VBA project prerequisites`; it closes before Project Name input begins. Reusing an eligible cached pass skips that notification and opens the input directly. Creation then uses a separate cancellable, indeterminate notification titled `VBA Tools: Creating Excel VBA project "{name}"`. The extension reports no percentage and does not infer stages by parsing stdout or stderr. It captures the invoked command and complete stdout and stderr in VBA Tools Output without opening the channel automatically.
