A future stage display requires a separately versioned structured-progress contract rather than new interpretations of terminal prose.

After the user requests creation cancellation, the progress message becomes `Cancellation requested; waiting for vba-dev to finish…`; repeated cancellation requests have no further effect. If delivery of the cancellation frame fails, the message changes to the separately established `Cancellation request could not be delivered; waiting for vba-dev to finish.` state. The notification remains open until the child `close` event has completed stdout and stderr capture and the authoritative terminal result can be classified. Neither the local cancellation request nor delivery completion closes progress early or substitutes for the command's exit and trusted-result contract.

An exit-zero result that is malformed, request-mismatched, or internally inconsistent means the command may have committed creation but returned an untrusted result. The caller shows one error with exact text `Excel VBA project creation may have completed, but its result could not be verified. Inspect the target and VBA Tools Output.` and Show Output as its only action. It makes no success or failure claim, offers no navigation, Retry, rollback, target deletion, or fallback inspection, and does not suppress independent manifest observation where applicable.

A trusted guided-creation success does not automatically replace the current VS Code window, add a workspace folder, open a file, reveal Explorer, or run Doctor, Build, List, or another command. The navigation choice converts the trusted result's **RequestedProjectRoot** and `manifestPath` to local `file:` URIs and compares the manifest lexically against every current local `file:` workspace folder under case-insensitive Windows path semantics with component boundaries. Containment offers `Open Manifest` in the current window; every other result offers `Open Folder in New Window`, preserving the current editing session. The extension does not resolve junctions, symbolic links, short names, alternate UNC spellings, or **ProjectRootIdentity**, and remote or virtual workspace folders are not containment candidates. A physically identical target reached through another lexical alias therefore remains outside for this navigation-only decision without weakening creation ownership or safety. Dismissing the notification changes neither window nor workspace state. This location-sensitive action does not equate a **WorkbookBackedProject** with a workspace folder; it only offers a convenient explicit transition after creation.

Failure of that later user-selected navigation action never changes the already trusted creation outcome. `Open Manifest` failure shows `Excel VBA project was created, but its manifest could not be opened.`; `Open Folder in New Window` failure shows `Excel VBA project was created, but its folder could not be opened in a new window.` Each is one action-failure notification with Retry and Show Output. Retry repeats only the same VS Code navigation request, never `new excel`, Doctor, validation, rollback, deletion, workspace addition, or another fallback navigation. Output records the project path, requested navigation target, and VS Code API error. Dismissal leaves the created project untouched. This second notification is attributable to the explicit post-success action rather than a duplicate terminal creation notification.

Guided creation neither triggers nor suppresses the existing activation-scoped first-run Doctor prompt and never writes its prompted or do-not-ask-again state. Creating a manifest within the current window does not itself prompt. If the user explicitly opens an outside project through Open Folder in New Window, the newly activated window may show its ordinary one-time `Run Doctor?` prompt when that workspace has not already handled it; the prompt invokes the same combined Doctor workflow as the Command Palette and still requires explicit consent. This is not duplicate environment preflight: the early preflight establishes only project-automation prerequisites, whereas ordinary Doctor can inspect the completed project and the independently owned debug-adapter health.

Guided creation presents exactly one terminal notification for a trusted success. With no CLI warnings and no cancellation-delivery failure it uses an information notification whose text is `Created Excel VBA project "{projectName}".` and whose sole action is the applicable location action above. With one or more CLI warnings it uses one warning notification whose text is `Created Excel VBA project "{projectName}". 1 warning.` or `Created Excel VBA project "{projectName}". {N} warnings.`, retains that location action, and also offers Show Output. If its caller-local stdin cancellation write failed before that trusted success, it uses the same single warning notification, preserves the CLI warning count without incrementing it, and appends `Cancellation request could not be delivered.`. A zero-warning delivery failure therefore uses exactly `Created Excel VBA project "{projectName}". Cancellation request could not be delivered.` rather than displaying `0 warnings.`; a nonzero case retains the applicable warning-count sentence before the transport sentence. It offers the location action and Show Output, never emits a separate success and warning pair, includes no CommonModules count, reference count, or absolute path in the toast, and does not open VBA Tools Output automatically. Complete details remain in captured output and JSON.

`new excel` has an explicit terminal-outcome contract rather than letting a caller infer the outcome from whether it requested cancellation. Exit `0` means trusted committed success and is the only outcome that emits the complete success JSON; a cancellation request that arrives after the initial manifest move therefore still returns success. Exit `130` means cancellation won before that move and the command proved complete rollback together with release of its owned Excel process and project lease. It emits no stdout JSON and writes `Project creation was cancelled.` to stderr. A reported operational failure uses exit `1`, and every other abnormal or nonzero termination except `130` is likewise a failure to the guided caller. This includes `newProjectCleanupIncomplete`, target conflict, cleanup or ownership uncertainty, and failure after a cancellation request. Only explicit exit `130` is silent in guided creation; failure produces one error notification with Show Output. The caller's local cancellation flag requests cooperative cancellation but never overrides the terminal exit and trusted-result classification.

The `new excel --format json` schema `1.0` is a success-only result contract. Exit `0` writes exactly one complete success object followed by a newline. Exit `130`, exit `1`, and every other nonzero or abnormal termination write no stdout result; cancellation uses the established stderr sentence, while operational failures use human-readable stderr diagnostics. Stable diagnostic spellings such as `manifestMutationBusy`, `newProjectTargetChanged`, and `newProjectCleanupIncomplete` remain available for support and tests, but the extension does not parse them as a dispatch protocol. Incomplete cleanup reports the original failure, every retained normalized absolute path known to the invocation, and manual-recovery guidance on stderr. Schema `1.0` has no `complete: false`, `failure`, `safeToRetry`, or `rollbackComplete` shape. A future safe-retry workflow requires a separately versioned failure envelope that proves unchanged-target or complete-rollback state rather than inferring it from a failure code.

The human-readable `newProjectCleanupIncomplete` diagnostic writes the original creation failure first, then `newProjectCleanupIncomplete: Project creation cleanup was incomplete.`, the canonical absolute target root, the known retained normalized absolute paths one per line, and recovery guidance. Known paths are case-insensitively unique and ordered by OrdinalIgnoreCase then Ordinal rather than by internal creation or deletion timing. If the invocation cannot conclusively bound the retained set, it does not print a guessed list and instead says `Retained paths could not be determined conclusively. Inspect the entire project target before retrying.` The final guidance says `Inspect the retained paths before retrying. Move or remove only content you have independently verified is safe; vba-dev will not change it automatically.` This stderr presentation is not a machine schema. Guided creation neither parses it nor adds Open Target Folder or a deletion action; its only failure action remains Show Output.

After CLI invocation has begun, a creation failure shows exactly one error notification, `Excel VBA project creation failed for "{name}".`, whose only action is Show Output. Guided creation neither retains the submitted name and parent for a retry path nor parses stderr to classify the failure, and it never retries, reopens the inputs, deletes the target, or invokes another command automatically. A nonzero result can represent partial creation, incomplete cleanup, external target change, or uncertain resource ownership, so repeating the same invocation is not assumed safe. This restriction does not remove Retry from the artifact-free environment preflight or Change Name and Choose Another Parent from pre-invocation path rejection. A future safe creation retry requires a separately versioned machine-readable failure result that conclusively proves an unchanged target or complete rollback; prose diagnostics are not that proof.

Common modules are copied from a canonical `common_modules_repo`. `new` creates a usable project even when the repository is missing, but reports a warning; the Excel-free static check and project-scope `doctor` report the missing repository as a project problem. Project-scope `doctor` also checks manifest-defined **VbaProjectReferences** for every document without changing workbooks. For **DocumentSourceSet** source files, the static check and project-scope `doctor` report case-insensitive duplicate extension-including `.bas`, `.cls`, or `.frm` file names as failures because flat source identity cannot map them to a single workbook import target. They report a warning for any `.frx` file that has no matching same-directory `.frm` but has a same-name `.frm` elsewhere in the source set, because that sidecar looks displaced rather than intentionally standalone. `.frx` files that have no same-name `.frm` anywhere in the source set are ignored. For **InstalledCommonModule** entries, they verify that names exist in the CommonModules manifest, that dependencies required by `requested: true` roots are present, and that copied source files are consistent with the manifest. Missing or unknown required entries are failures. Unreachable `requested: false` entries and source file drift start as warnings. CommonModules dependencies and classifications are expected to come from a machine-readable manifest owned by `xls-common-devtools`, not from hard-coded `vba-dev` rules or scraped product documentation.

`vba-dev doctor --scope environment` is the project-independent subset used for project-automation readiness. It neither discovers nor accepts a project/document context and omits manifest, source, template, output, CommonModules, selected-reference, and materialization-profile checks. Default Doctor behavior retains the full applicable project-and-environment report. Environment scope remains a public CLI diagnostic rather than an extension-owned Excel probe, so direct callers can perform the same preflight without VS Code.

Resource-specific operations follow .NET CLI-style noun-first command groups where possible instead of using one top-level verb that infers a resource from positional names. CommonModules are added with `common-module add`, listed with `common-module list`, and updated with `common-module update`. `common-module add` accepts one or more CommonModules names in a single invocation and is scoped to one document source set. `common-module list` is also document-scoped, defaults to the primary document when `--document` is omitted, identifies the selected document in its output, defaults to human-readable text, and supports `--format json` for automation. `common-module update` updates already-installed CommonModules entries as a project-wide operation; the initial implementation does not need per-module update selection. `common-module remove` is not part of the initial command surface because dependency ownership, shared dependencies, and locally edited copied files need separate deletion semantics. **VbaProjectReferences** use `reference add`, `reference list`, and `reference remove`. These reference commands are scoped to one document definition except for the explicit environment fallback of `reference list --available` when no project or document was selected and upward manifest discovery finds nothing. `reference add` and `reference remove` accept one or more reference names in a single invocation. `reference list` identifies the selected document in project scope, defaults to human-readable text, and supports `--format json` for automation. Because the command surface is not released yet, earlier top-level forms such as `add <module>`, `add module`, `add reference`, `update`, and `remove reference` do not need compatibility support. `reference remove` removes **VbaProjectReferences** from the manifest only and succeeds for references that are already absent.

`Visual Basic For Applications` is the sole reserved `VbaProjectReferenceSelection` name, compared under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, because its **VbaStandardLibraryReference** is always active independently of manifest selection. The closed ProjectManifest schema and runtime validation reject it in every document reference array. `reference add` and `reference remove` reject that requested name before catalog resolution, lease acquisition, or mutation and report exactly `Visual Basic For Applications is always active and cannot be added to or removed from project reference selection.` A selected-reference list therefore never contains it, and available-reference inventory, dynamic Tab completion, `ReferenceAddQuickPick`, and `ReferenceRemoveQuickPick` filter it rather than presenting an impossible selection. Language Server injects the standard library independently. `VBA` is a catalog qualifier, not a reserved alias; Excel, Office, OLE Automation, and every other baseline or live protected reference remain ordinary selectable references. `new excel` initial selection and CommonModules RequiredReferences obey the same invariant. The command surface is unreleased, so manifests using the reserved selection are invalid and require migration rather than compatibility behavior.

`common-module add` and `common-module update` also default to `--format text` and accept `--format json`; `-f` is the common alias. Their capabilities entries advertise output schema version `1.0`. JSON output is one complete result object emitted only after atomic success or a trusted no-op. A failed or cancelled invocation emits no partial success object, so VS Code, guided flows, and other automated callers never parse human-readable mutation prose to determine applied changes.

`host-class list` is a read-only, document-scoped noun-first command. It follows
ordinary project discovery, selects the requested document or the primary
document when `--document` is omitted, identifies that document in its output,
defaults to human-readable text, and supports `--format json` for
`HostClassProjectionLifecycle`. Its command-specific JSON schema `1.1` is
advertised by `capabilities --format json` as
`commandSchemaVersions: { "host-class list": "1.1" }`; the independent
`featureVersions: { "hostClass.list": "1.0" }` capability remains unchanged.
The command inspects the
selected source template in a dedicated hidden **AutomationExcelProcess** and
changes neither the workbook, source, manifest, nor consumer-owned projection
storage.

`host-class list` never opens the source template in place. It copies the
template into a unique invocation-owned **HostClassInspectionWorkspace**. To
bind COM to the exact newly Job-owned Excel process, the process bootstrapper
may first open one generated macro-free `.xlsx` containing no project bytes.
That bootstrap is used only for process ownership, is neither inspected nor
saved, and is closed and deleted before the private source copy is opened. The
command then sets and verifies force-disabled automation security and disabled
Excel Events, requires zero open workbooks, opens only the private source copy
read-only, and requires exactly one open workbook. It imports no project source,
changes no references, never saves the copy, rereads or rehashes no original
input after the start-time copy, releases the **AutomationExcelProcess** before
removing the workspace, and returns no projection if the temporary baseline
cannot be prepared.

Machine-readable `host-class list` output is held until the command proves
release of its **AutomationExcelProcess**. Failure to prove release is a
command-level failure and emits no JSON projection. After release, workspace
deletion receives bounded retries; if only deletion remains unsuccessful, the
command reports the retained absolute path as a housekeeping warning while
preserving the projection and successful exit status. Normal failure and
cancellation use the same cleanup ordering.

After request scope is established and owned-process release succeeds,
`host-class list --format json` emits one schema-valid result even when a
class-local or enumeration failure makes the command exit nonzero. Every
enumerated class has status `resolved` or `unverified`; the required top-level
`classEnumerationComplete` is true exactly when the complete, unambiguous
class-identity set was enumerated. Top-level `complete` is true only when
`classEnumerationComplete` is true and every class projection is `resolved`.
The result also carries required request-context fields `project`, `document`,
and `sourceTemplate`: the canonical absolute project root, the
manifest-resolved document name, and the canonical absolute source-template
path selected at invocation start. Machine consumers may commit `resolved`
entries independently. An `unverified`
entry preserves the same class's **LastKnownGoodHostClassProjection**, or leaves
it indeterminate when none exists. When enumeration is incomplete, omitted
classes are unknown and never represent authoritative empty Event surfaces.
Malformed JSON, schema or request-context mismatch, or process-release failure
makes the entire invocation untrusted.

Schema `1.1` emits `vbaProjectName` and `sourceTemplateFingerprint` only as a
pair. They are the actual valid `VBProject.Name` observed from the inspected
private copy and the uppercase SHA-256 fingerprint of those exact workbook
bytes. If either value cannot be established, both fields are absent. The pair
remains legacy observational transport during migration; Module Rename and
project-name diagnostics ignore it and obtain containing-project authority from
their own request-scoped static source-template read. Class entries continue to
supply Host Event and current form-ownership evidence independently.

Within the top-level project and document request scope, every class entry
carries an identity containing the inspected `VBComponent.Name` and component
kind `form` or `document`. Consumers compare the name case-insensitively while
retaining the casing reported by the projection. Temporary paths, source file
paths, worksheet display names, component ordinals, and COM identities are not
identity fields. Source association is a consumer responsibility and requires
an explicit matching `Attribute VB_Name` plus a compatible component kind; the
ordinary source file-name fallback for **ModuleIdentity** does not bind a host
class.

Every `resolved` class entry also carries required
`intrinsicEventSourceName`, the inspected VBE Object-box name that qualifies
intrinsic handler procedures. Consumers form a handler name from that value,
one ASCII underscore, and the Event name: for example `Worksheet_Change`,
`Workbook_Open`, or `UserForm_Initialize`. Comparison is case-insensitive while
the inspected casing is retained. The command establishes the value from the
VBE-equivalent object/Event association and never derives it from
`VBComponent.Name`, component kind, source file name, or optional base host type
identity. Failure to establish it makes the class `unverified` with
`reasonCode: "intrinsicEventSourceNameReadFailure"`. Because the command is
unreleased, schema `1.1` requires this field and provides no compatibility
default or alias.

A class identity may occur at most once in one enumeration. Two observations
with the same component kind and case-insensitively equal `VBComponent.Name`
are not coalesced, even when their projected contents match, because the public
identity cannot distinguish their components. The command omits that identity,
adds top-level `classEnumerationFailure`, sets `complete: false`, and exits
nonzero while continuing to inspect other unique identities. This conflict
alone does not add `inspectionStateUntrusted`; that diagnostic requires
separate evidence that the shared Excel/VBIDE state is untrustworthy. Equal
names with different `form` and `document` kinds remain distinct identities.

Every `resolved` class entry contains the full Event signatures observed during
inspection rather than only a base host type identity. That inspected payload
is authoritative for the consumer and is not rehydrated from a reference
catalog. An entry may also carry a catalog-resolvable base host type identity
for provenance or navigation, but failure to supply or resolve that optional
identity does not invalidate otherwise complete inspected Event data.

Machine-readable Event entries are structured rather than display-label-only.
They carry the Event name and ordered parameter metadata for name, type
reference, passing mechanism, array shape, available `Optional` or `ParamArray`
flags, and optional documentation. Parameter names and rendered labels do not
form Event-handler compatibility identity, and labels are derived by each
consumer. This public command DTO does not reference or serialize the language
server's internal `VbaCallableSignature` type.

Within one host-class identity, case-insensitive Event name is the unique Event
identity. Same-name observations are not overloads and never become a
conditional callable family. The command may coalesce them only when parameter
count, canonical types, passing mechanisms, array shape, `Optional` or
`ParamArray` shape, and both availability values agree. Differences limited to
Event or parameter casing, parameter names, or optional documentation are
presentation differences and are normalized deterministically. Any callable
contract or availability conflict makes the complete class `unverified` with
`reasonCode: "eventEnumerationFailure"`; the command does not serialize
multiple same-name signatures.

Coalescing retains one complete observed presentation rather than combining
fields from different observations. It first prefers an observation with
nonempty documentation. Among equally documented candidates, it compares the
tuple of Event name, ordered parameter names, and documentation by
`OrdinalIgnoreCase` and then `Ordinal`, and retains the minimum candidate as a
whole. This choice affects only presentation because callable contract and
availability equality have already been established.

Projection order is canonical rather than inherited from COM or VBIDE
enumeration. Class entries are ordered by component kind and then
`VBComponent.Name` using `OrdinalIgnoreCase` followed by `Ordinal`; resolved and
unverified entries are not partitioned by status. Each class's Events are
ordered by Event name with the same comparisons, while parameters retain their
inspected ordinal positions. Human-readable and JSON output use the same class
and Event order, and enumeration ordinals are not serialized.

Event collection membership means the Event is structurally present. Every
machine-readable Event entry also carries required boolean
`authoringAvailable` and `existingHandlerRecognizable` values. The former
controls ordinary completion and retains eligibility evidence for future
new-handler creation; the latter controls association, navigation, and
signature guidance for an existing handler. The values may differ. They expose
inspected behavior rather than raw TypeLib flags, which are not the machine
consumer contract.

Both booleans are required on every Event in a `resolved` class. The command
does not default unavailable evidence to false or true. If either value cannot
be established, the entire class entry is `unverified`, top-level `complete` is
false, and the consumer preserves the class's
**LastKnownGoodHostClassProjection**. An inspected false/false pair remains a
valid resolved Event because its collection membership independently records
structural existence.

Parameter type data is a discriminated portable identity. Intrinsic VBA types
use canonical type names. TypeLib types use a case-insensitive type name with
retained display casing plus library GUID, major and minor version, and LCID.
An unresolved type retains only its display name and never establishes
canonical equality, including with another same-text unresolved type.
Human-visible reference names, VBA qualifiers, and registry paths are excluded
from type identity. The command DTO does not serialize the language server's
current `VbaTypeReference` name-and-qualifier representation.

A successfully observed but opaque parameter type uses the unresolved form
without making its class entry `unverified`. When class and Event enumeration
and every parameter's structural inspection complete, the entry remains
`resolved`; consumers may use its Event name and known structure but treat type
compatibility involving the unresolved reference as indeterminate. A metadata
read failure, incomplete enumeration, or untrusted inspection instead makes the
affected class `unverified`. Therefore unresolved type references do not by
themselves make top-level `complete` false, and only an `unverified` entry
preserves the corresponding **LastKnownGoodHostClassProjection**.

An `unverified` class entry contains exactly its identity, status, stable
`reasonCode`, and human-readable `message`; it contains no Event collection or
other partial projection payload. Machine consumers never commit observations
made before that class failed. A future diagnostic-only partial-observation
field requires a new host-class schema version and must remain distinct from
the authoritative resolved payload.

The initial host-class schema recognizes exactly these class-local
`reasonCode` values: `eventEnumerationFailure`,
`intrinsicEventSourceNameReadFailure`, `signatureReadFailure`,
`availabilityReadFailure`, `inspectionTimeout`, `inspectionAborted`,
`cancelled`, and fallback `inspectionFailure`. The human-readable message may
identify an Event or inspection stage, but consumers do not parse it. Failure
to enumerate every class identity adds top-level diagnostic code
`classEnumerationFailure` and leaves omitted classes unknown. Failure to
prepare the source-template baseline or prove process release remains
invocation-invalidating and is not represented by an unverified class reason.

Once project and document scope are known, cooperative cancellation emits a
schema-valid terminal result when process release and serialization succeed.
Already resolved classes remain unchanged; the in-progress class and every
known unprocessed class are `unverified` with
`reasonCode: "cancelled"`. Classes not discovered before incomplete enumeration
remain omitted and unknown. The result contains top-level diagnostic
`operationCancelled`, has `complete: false`, exits nonzero, and does not add
`classEnumerationFailure` or use `inspectionAborted` merely because the user
cancelled. Failure to prove process release or serialize that terminal state
produces no usable JSON.

When shared host-class inspection state becomes untrustworthy, the command does
not start replacement Excel within the same invocation. The class whose
inspection caused the loss of trust uses its most specific reason, including
`inspectionTimeout` or fallback `inspectionFailure`; every known later
unprocessed class uses `inspectionAborted`. Earlier finalized `resolved`
entries remain usable only when the command can establish that the failure did
not affect them. The response adds top-level diagnostic
`inspectionStateUntrusted`, has `complete: false`, and exits nonzero. Failure to
prove process release, or inability to prove earlier-entry isolation, makes the
entire JSON result unusable.

`host-class list` reuses the ordinary 30-second Excel-process-start deadline,
the 300-second workbook-open deadline with its manifest override, and the
5-second cooperative-cleanup grace period. Complete host-class identity
enumeration has one 60-second deadline, and each class receives a fresh
60-second deadline for its complete Event, signature, and availability
inspection. There is no command-wide or per-Event deadline. A per-class
deadline marks the causal class `inspectionTimeout` and every known later
unprocessed class `inspectionAborted`. An identity-enumeration deadline instead
adds top-level `classEnumerationFailure` and `inspectionStateUntrusted`; omitted
classes remain unknown.

When `classEnumerationComplete` is true, a machine consumer removes every
previously committed identity absent from the result, even if a listed
class-local failure makes `complete` false. When it is false, unreported
classes retain any **LastKnownGoodHostClassProjection** because absence is
unknown. Duplicate identity, enumeration timeout, and cancellation before
enumeration completes make it false; class-local Event inspection failure does
not. The command emits no deletion tombstones, and consumers do not infer
enumeration authority from diagnostic codes.

The consumer owns a monotonically increasing refresh generation for each
selected project document. It may commit a result only while the invocation's
generation remains current and `project`, `document`, and `sourceTemplate`
match its current canonical request context. A superseded or mismatched result
changes neither resolved projections nor deletion state. The generation is not
a CLI option or output field, and host-class schema `1.1` contains no
consumer-specific request ID, mtime, or inspection timestamp. Its optional
source-template fingerprint binds the actual project name to the inspected
bytes but is not consumer freshness state.

The consumer schedules host-class inspection when a project document first
becomes active, when manifest reconciliation adds or removes a document or
changes its effective source-template identity, when the selected
source-template file is created, changed, or deleted, and when the consumer
explicitly requests a refresh. Removing the document or changing to a different
source-template identity advances the generation, cancels in-flight work, and
removes the old projection before any new refresh. A content change at the same
template path advances the generation but preserves last-known-good state if
refresh fails; temporary absence at that path reports unavailable state and
also preserves last-known-good. Exported source edits, reference-selection or
catalog changes alone, editor changes, command completion, and bin or publish
output changes are not refresh triggers unless they also change the selected
source template.

The extension uses one host-class refresh scheduler across all project
documents and permits at most one running `host-class list` invocation.
Automatic template and manifest triggers use a one-second trailing-edge
debounce before the effective request context is resolved; initial activation
and explicit refresh bypass it. Each canonical project document retains only
its latest pending generation. A new trigger advances that generation
immediately, replaces an older queued request for the same document, and
requests cooperative cancellation when that document is already running.
Replacement never starts before the superseded CLI exits and its owned Excel
process cleanup completes. Any partial terminal result from the superseded
generation is discarded whole. Other documents remain FIFO and are not
cancelled or overtaken by same-document replacement. Queue time has no
deadline. Extension shutdown cancels running work and drops the pending queue.

The lifecycle performs no timer-based automatic retry after a schema-valid
partial or `unverified` result, source-template preparation or Excel automation
failure, timeout, schema mismatch, or process-release failure. Cancellation and
supersession likewise do not create retry work; supersession already has the
newest generation queued. Recovery begins only from a later lifecycle trigger
or explicit consumer refresh, which creates a new generation. Explicit refresh
bypasses debounce but still obeys single-flight scheduling and cleanup
ordering. Host-class schema `1.1` adds no retryability or backoff fields.

The extension contributes `VBA Tools: Refresh Host Events` with command ID
`vbaTools.hostClasses.refresh`. It uses the ordinary project and document
chooser, creates a new generation for one selected document, bypasses debounce,
and enters the same single-flight queue. Its progress notification is
cancellable. Success produces no toast. An explicitly requested failure shows
one error notification with `Show Output`; a background failure never produces
a popup.

Cancelling that explicit progress removes only the same document's queued
request or requests cooperative cancellation of only its running invocation.
It does not cancel another document's running or queued work. If process release
and serialization succeed and the cancelled invocation still owns the current
generation, its schema-valid terminal partial result follows the ordinary
commit contract: resolved entries may commit, cancelled or unverified entries
preserve last-known-good state, and absence removes an old identity only when
`classEnumerationComplete` is true. Invalid output preserves all prior state.
Supersession instead discards the complete obsolete-generation result. User
cancellation creates neither an error notification nor automatic retry.

The extension shows host-class status only while work is queued or running or
attention is required. A status-bar click opens VBA Tools Output and never
retries implicitly. Output records generation, project and document, trigger,
lifecycle transitions, resolved and unverified counts, identity deletions,
reason codes, and last-known-good use. This operational state does not create
source diagnostics, a dedicated Project Health view, or a `vba-dev doctor`
result.

The extension folds CLI results and retained state into an immutable
document-wide effective snapshot rather than forwarding raw results or class
deltas. The versioned `vba/hostClassProjectionSnapshot` LSP notification
carries a document-local monotonically increasing revision, canonical request
context, and `present` or `cleared` state. A present snapshot contains
`classEnumerationComplete` and every `current`, `lastKnownGood`, or
`indeterminate` class entry; only the first two carry a complete projection.
The language server atomically replaces or clears that document, rejects stale
revision or mismatched context, and treats the notification as a coalescible
workspace mutation. The extension replays desired snapshots after language
client restart, after enqueuing manifest synchronization. Operational messages
remain extension-owned, and editor requests never wait for this transport.

Only a `current` effective-snapshot entry is semantic authority. A
`lastKnownGood` entry may supply advisory completion, hover, Signature Help,
existing-handler association, and navigation, but it leaves the host Event
surface indeterminate for validation, type conclusions, and
meaning-preserving mutation. Stale evidence cannot establish compile-style
diagnostics and a mutation requiring current host evidence fails with
`analysisIncomplete`. An `indeterminate` entry supplies no projected Event
candidate. Status reports last-known-good use without annotating every editor
item.

The complete `vba-dev` command surface is defined by one `System.CommandLine`
`RootCommand` graph in the executable-facing CLI layer, following the structural
precedent of FirewallRuleToolkit. That graph owns parsing, validation, help,
version output, and static and dynamic completion. The current custom command
catalog and parser are replaced before release; they are not retained beside a
second completion grammar. CLI actions adapt `ParseResult` values to
application requests, while application and domain services remain independent
of `System.CommandLine`. Stable machine output such as `capabilities --format
json` remains an explicit command contract.

PowerShell completion is registered by evaluating the output of
`vba-dev completions script pwsh`. The generated script supports Windows
PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7, uses the native argument-completer mechanism,
and queries `vba-dev` directly for dynamic candidates. It does not require
`dotnet-suggest`, edit user profiles, depend on VS Code terminal initialization,
or require a separately installed PowerShell module. The initial implementation
may query `vba-dev` for every completion request; a later hybrid script may
embed static commands and options while preserving the same public setup
command and deriving all completion behavior from the same `RootCommand`
graph.

**InstalledCommonModule** entries belong to the document definition in `vba-project.json`, under `documents.<document-name>.commonModules`, as objects with required `name`, `moduleFile`, `requested`, `testOnly`, and `orphaned` fields. `name` is the extensionless **CommonModuleName**, while `moduleFile` is the extension-including exported source file name used for flat **DocumentSourceSet** identity. `requested` distinguishes explicit roots from installed dependencies, `testOnly` records the publish classification captured from CommonModules metadata, and `orphaned` records that the retained name was conclusively absent from the stable, completely validated repository snapshot used by the latest successful CommonModules reconciliation. Dependency-expanded entries each retain their own source identity and classifications.

Because this manifest contract is not yet released, these required fields replace the earlier development shape without incrementing `schemaVersion` beyond `1`. The tool does not provide backward-compatible parsing or migration for manifests that omit them; development manifests using the earlier shape must be regenerated or edited before use.

The project manifest is the source of truth for both the installed set and publish classification. `build` imports the current source set without consulting CommonModules repository metadata or performing CommonModules consistency checks. `publish` also does not consult that metadata; it excludes source files whose manifest entries record `testOnly: true`, matched by `moduleFile`. A manifest-listed CommonModule remains installed when its copied source differs from the currently configured CommonModules repository. That content drift does not block `build` or `publish`; `doctor` may report it as a warning, and only an explicit `common-module update` replaces the source and refreshes the recorded installation metadata.

An InstalledCommonModule whose retained identity is absent from the latest successfully validated CommonModules repository becomes an **OrphanedInstalledCommonModule** rather than being inferred as a rename. Its manifest entry and source remain in the effective installed set: `build` continues to import it, and `publish` continues to follow its recorded `testOnly` classification. A differently named repository entry is a separate CommonModule and is installed only when selected explicitly or reached through the latest dependency closure; repository proximity or simultaneous disappearance and appearance creates no successor relationship. Orphaning is advisory groundwork for a future fully revalidated purge, not current removal or disablement. A directly requested orphan is never an automatic-removal candidate, and a dependency orphan is retained until a future purge operation proves removal safe.

`new excel` and a newly installed Add entry write `orphaned: false`. Update sets `orphaned: true` only after its complete, valid, invocation-stable repository snapshot conclusively lacks that installed name. If the same identity later reappears, Update clears the marker only as part of a successful source and metadata refresh. Missing or inaccessible repository state, invalid manifest data, incomplete validation, ambiguous identity, or a repository change during snapshot capture fails the command and preserves every prior marker. Consequently `orphaned: false` records the latest successful reconciliation outcome rather than promising live repository availability. The manifest stores no orphan timestamp, repository version or fingerprint, reason object, or inferred successor.

A manifest-listed **InstalledCommonModule** also retains a **ManagedModuleIdentity**. Editor semantic Rename does not rewrite its `name` or `moduleFile`, mutate the configured CommonModules repository, or silently remove the manifest entry. A repository entry with a different CommonModuleName is a different CommonModule rather than an implicit identity migration; the retained copy changes identity only after an explicit detach converts it into project-local source. Ordinary member Rename and local source-content drift remain separate concerns.

One CommonModuleName identifies at most one exported source identity in a valid repository snapshot. A case-only difference in `moduleFile` or authoritative `ModuleIdentityMetadata` is the same identity but a new canonical spelling: repository-backed reconciliation refreshes the installed manifest `name` and `moduleFile`, the existing source basename, an optional `.frx` basename, and the complete source-unit bytes to that spelling while retaining the containing directory and applying the ordinary `requested`, `testOnly`, and `orphaned` rules. New creation and ordinary Add use repository spelling from the outset; a force replacement of an existing case-insensitive source match applies the same canonical refresh. Recasing belongs to the preplanned source-unit mutation, uses the existing per-file atomic-placement and partial-failure/recovery contract rather than promising a new multi-file atomic boundary, and fails before mutation when the destination identity is occupied or ambiguous. Because the canonical source bytes also change, the existing `sourceUpdated` result kind represents this refresh and the module result's final `name`, `moduleFile`, and `sourceSetRelativePath` carry the new spelling. A substantive difference for the same extensionless name, such as `Feature.bas` versus `Feature.cls` or `Feature.frm`, remains a repository identity conflict rather than metadata refresh, orphaning, or retirement-plus-replacement. Add or Update fails before mutation and preserves the current source, manifest entry, and orphan marker. A repository publisher that intends old-module retirement plus a new module must give the new source a distinct CommonModuleName and update dependency declarations accordingly; only then may Update retain the old name as orphaned and install the new name independently.

In the initial command surface, Excel-free `vba-dev check` and project-scope `vba-dev doctor` are the CommonModules consistency-checking commands; `build` and `publish` neither invoke them nor depend on their result. Both separate repository synchronization from installed-source integrity, while project Doctor additionally composes selected-reference, workbook-materialization, and active environment evidence. An `orphaned: true` entry whose recorded `moduleFile` identifies exactly one structurally valid source unit is a warning rather than a failure. A non-orphaned entry absent from a completely validated current repository, or an orphaned entry whose same identity has reappeared, is likewise a stale-reconciliation warning that directs the user to `common-module update`; neither diagnostic mutates the marker. Installed source content that differs from an available repository entry is a warning.

An invalid project or CommonModules manifest, a missing or inaccessible configured repository that prevents complete inspection, a missing installed source unit, or duplicate or otherwise ambiguous installed source identity remains a failed diagnostic. Neither check nor Doctor manufactures per-entry orphan facts from an unavailable, invalid, or incomplete repository. If any `requested: true` root is orphaned, the prior dependency graph is unavailable and the diagnostics conservatively treat reachability of retained dependency entries as indeterminate. Only when every directly requested root resolves against the complete current repository may a `requested: false` entry outside the resulting union closure receive a future-prune-candidate warning; the initial command surface still removes nothing. `vba-dev check` exposes these as static project checks without changing command ownership.

The initial contract does not add a persistent CommonModules package lock, pin a CommonModules package version or artifact hash, or require per-source content hashes. The transient **ProjectManifestMutationLease** marker coordinates manifest mutation only and is not CommonModules package or version authority.

The CommonModules repository manifest also owns each entry's direct external-reference requirements. Canonical source and distributed `common-modules-manifest.tsv` bytes use strict UTF-16LE with a required BOM, CRLF line endings, and a trailing line ending. Readers reject a missing BOM, UTF-16BE, UTF-8, ACP-derived text, or decoding replacement rather than guessing or falling back, and collection preserves the validated manifest bytes. Its exact four-column TSV header is `ModuleFile<TAB>Categories<TAB>Dependencies<TAB>RequiredReferences`. Every data row contains the fourth column, whose complete cell is a standalone JSON array of strings: no requirements use `[]`, one may use `["Microsoft Scripting Runtime"]`, and multiple names remain separate JSON elements rather than a comma-delimited subformat. Standard JSON string escaping therefore keeps commas, quotes, and backslashes inside externally owned human-visible names unambiguous. Every decoded name is nonempty and already trimmed, names are unique within the cell under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, and declaration order is preserved; invalid shape or duplicate data invalidates the manifest rather than being silently dropped, trimmed, or deduplicated. The case-insensitive canonical human-visible name `Visual Basic For Applications` is forbidden because its **VbaStandardLibraryReference** is always active outside manifest selection and cannot become a selected dependency; readers reject it rather than silently ignoring or persisting it. Other baseline references such as Excel, Office, and OLE Automation remain valid requirements and preserve existing direct intent when already selected. Each row declares only the external references used directly by its own source entry, not the repeated transitive requirements of its CommonModule dependencies. Installation resolves the complete source dependency closure and then takes the ordered `OrdinalIgnoreCase` union of every included row's declarations; repeated direct use by different rows is valid, and the first encountered declaration retains spelling and order.

The canonical line sequence is zero or more leading whole-line comments, the exact header once, one or more contiguous data rows, and the required final CRLF immediately after the last row. A comment begins with `#` in the physical line's first code unit and contains no tab, Unicode control character, or trailing whitespace. Blank or whitespace-only lines, leading whitespace before `#`, inline comments, comments after the header, a repeated or differently cased header, and any additional trailing line are invalid. Only a physical line whose first code unit is `#` is a comment, so `#` inside a `RequiredReferences` JSON string remains data. Canonical source and accepted consumer input use this same grammar; no reader normalizes a more permissive form before collection byte-copies it.

This byte-encoding contract does not authorize repository configuration changes. Neither the CommonModules collection workflow nor `VbaDev` creates or modifies `.gitattributes` for `common-modules-manifest.tsv` or `vba-project.json`; repository owners independently decide whether and how Git stores, diffs, or renders those files.

CommonModules collection performs the complete canonical manifest validation before its first distribution-output mutation; parsing only `ModuleFile` or relying on a separately invoked validator is insufficient. Any encoding, header, column, JSON-cell, category, duplicate, dependency, or required-reference structural error fails collection and preserves the prior output. The validator and collector share one PowerShell parsing and validation meaning, and a successful collection byte-copies the already validated UTF-16LE manifest. `VbaDev` independently validates the same public grammar when consuming a distributed repository rather than trusting producer provenance. The complete flat package inventory is also canonical: every source decodes and round-trips under strict Windows-932 without BOM inference, its authoritative `VB_Name` agrees ordinal-exactly with the manifest basename and declared source kind, and its extensionless name is nonreserved, no longer than 31 Unicode code points, and matches `^\p{L}[\p{L}\p{Nd}_]*$`. Cross-language versioned valid and invalid fixtures keep the PowerShell producer and .NET consumer interpretations aligned.

`new excel`, `common-module add`, and `common-module update` append any missing effective requirements with `requested: false` while preserving every existing reference and never downgrading `requested: true`. The initial command surface does not remove a reference merely because current repository metadata no longer requires it; `requested: false` is conservative groundwork for a future fully revalidated auto-remove feature, not present deletion authority.

Before CommonModules source or manifest mutation, every required reference name missing from the latest selected document's effective reference selection must reach the same conclusive VBE-equivalent resolution used by `reference add`; an already-selected name remains environment-independent authority and is not re-resolved. A registry-unique requirement uses the registry-only path. A registry-ambiguous requirement uses a fresh temporary copy of that document's selected source template and an owned **AutomationExcelProcess**, so `common-module add` and project-wide `common-module update` may start Excel only for documents that actually need such a probe. The complete set resolves before the first CommonModules source mutation, and unavailable, ambiguous, unverified, failed, or pre-boundary-cancelled resolution aborts without source or manifest changes. Resolved canonical names are appended with `requested: false`. `new excel` performs the equivalent resolution against its already owned initial workbook before initial manifest commit; failure follows the established creation rollback contract.

CommonModules Add and Update do not hold **ProjectManifestMutationLease** during registry resolution or a potentially long Excel/VBE ambiguity probe. From the valid invocation-start manifest and, only when an uninstalled Add target or an installed Update target requires repository work, a complete stable invocation-start repository snapshot, they derive a provisional plan, conclusively resolve every then-missing effective requirement, and complete every owned probe cleanup first. Installed-only Add and zero-target Update require no repository snapshot or reference-resolution preflight. Each resolution result becomes invocation-local **CommonModulesReferenceResolutionEvidence** keyed by the document, required name, and that document's selected canonical source-template path. Cancellation during this preflight changes no project state and acquires no lease.

After acquiring the lease, the command reloads the latest valid manifest and first partitions the latest targets without consulting the repository. If no repository-backed target remains, as for installed-only Add or zero-target Update, it skips repository capture. Otherwise it captures a complete stable repository snapshot into invocation-owned staging before deriving the final dependency closure, RequiredReferences union, repository source set, file-operation plan, and planned manifest; those final facts use only the staged bytes and never mix repository generations. Reference-resolution evidence is reusable only when the rebased target-document set and each document's ordered missing-requirement sequence are identical to the invocation-start plan and the same canonical source-template selection remains authoritative. Any added, removed, or reordered missing requirement, any added or removed target document, or a relevant source-template selection change aborts the whole command before source mutation and directs the user to rerun. The command neither starts a probe while holding the lease nor releases the lease to probe and automatically retry. Registry or environment changes after conclusive preflight are outside the invocation snapshot, matching Reference Add. `new excel` remains the exception that may resolve against its already owned initial workbook while establishing the initially leased project.

Every failure of the latest required-reference plan to match reusable preflight evidence is the single nonzero command outcome `commonModulesRequiredReferencePlanChanged`, rather than a resolver status or several race-specific codes. Its stable primary message is `CommonModules required-reference planning changed while references were being resolved. No source or manifest changes were made. Rerun the command.` It covers a new final missing requirement, a requirement that became selected concurrently, concurrent removal of a requirement that was selected at invocation start, an ordering change, a target-document-set change, a relevant canonical source-template selection change, and any other exact-plan mismatch.

Failure diagnostics list affected documents under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` with an `Ordinal` tie-break. Within each document they list required names in the final plan's first-seen requirement order; a changed template selection reports its prior and latest canonical paths before the affected names. These details are human-facing stderr and VBA Tools Output context, not a partial machine-readable success schema. The command releases the lease, exits nonzero without a CommonModules success object, performs no source or manifest mutation, and does not retry automatically. Invocation-start resolution that is itself unavailable, ambiguous, unverified, failed, or cancelled retains its ordinary resolver failure rather than being mislabeled as a concurrent-plan change.

`common-module add` updates the manifest and copies the selected CommonModules source files into the target **DocumentSourceSet** in one command. It resolves CommonModules dependencies from the `xls-common-devtools`-owned machine-readable manifest and installs required dependencies together with the explicitly requested entries. The manifest records whether each entry was explicitly requested with `requested: true` or installed as an automatically resolved dependency with `requested: false`, so future auto-remove or prune behavior can avoid treating dependency removal semantics as a breaking manifest change. Adding an already-installed entry succeeds without adding duplicate manifest entries or copying files again. If an already-installed dependency entry is later named explicitly in `common-module add`, the manifest upgrades that entry from `requested: false` to `requested: true` without recopying files. When the entry is not installed but a target source file with the same exported file name exists anywhere in the **DocumentSourceSet**, `common-module add` fails by default to avoid overwriting project-owned source. `--force` permits overwriting during add when exactly one matching exported file name exists; when no matching file exists, add copies to the **CommonModulesDirectory** using the entry's extension-including file name; when multiple matching file names exist, add fails because the flat source identity is ambiguous. `common-module update` reconciles source units for manifest-listed **InstalledCommonModule** entries in place when exactly one matching exported file name exists, restores missing entries to the **CommonModulesDirectory** using the entry's extension-including file name, and fails when multiple matching file names exist. Copy and update output reports the actual `DocumentSourceSet`-relative destination path, including `common-modules/` for new placements and any existing nested path for in-place changes. For invocations that affect multiple entries, including dependency-expanded `common-module add`, the command computes the complete manifest and file plan for every affected entry before it mutates the manifest, deletes sidecars, or copies files; any preflight failure aborts without partial manifest or file changes. Manifest writes happen after all planned file deletes and copies succeed, so file operation failures do not leave a manifest that points at missing or partially written CommonModules source. If file delete or copy fails after file mutation begins, the command reports that the manifest was not saved and source files may have been partially updated, and it does not attempt automatic file rollback. If manifest saving fails after successful file operations, the command saves the validated planned content beside `vba-project.json` as a crash-atomic UTF-16LE-with-BOM **ProjectManifestRecoveryArtifact** whose create-new name begins `vba-project.failed-` and contains a UTC timestamp and collision-resistant random suffix; it prints only that committed recovery path for manual recovery and does not attempt automatic file rollback or application. If recovery persistence also fails, the command reports that failure together with the canonical save failure and does not print the planned manifest JSON to the console. When a CommonModules entry is a `.frm` form, its `.frm` and optional matching `.frx` are one source unit. Add and Update reconcile that unit together: a sidecar-only creation, replacement, or deletion is part of the same source change, and output identifies the `.frm` destination rather than exposing a separate low-level sidecar event. Duplicate `.frx` files alone do not define exported source identity.

Update compares the repository and target source units by exact raw bytes and sidecar presence before planning a target mutation. It creates a missing unit and atomically replaces or removes files only when the unit differs. Exact equality performs no source write, deletion, or timestamp churn and does not produce `sourceUpdated`; when no manifest fact changes either, that exhaustive module result is `unchanged`. This byte comparison does not normalize text encoding or line endings and does not treat an equal decoded projection as equal source authority.

At invocation start and again during final rebase, CommonModules Add partitions normalized requested names against the latest installed set. An already installed entry is manifest authority for direct intent and is neither repository-resolved nor source-inspected merely to promote or confirm it. Therefore an installed `requested: false` orphan may be promoted to `requested: true` while retaining `orphaned: true`, and an installed `requested: true` orphan is a trusted no-op; both retain the aggregate orphan warning. Add never refreshes existing source, clears `orphaned`, or adopts reappeared metadata, because Update owns repository reconciliation. An invocation whose every requested name is already installed can complete its promotions or no-op without an available CommonModules repository. If any requested name is uninstalled, Add requires the complete valid repository and resolves the full missing-name dependency closure before mutation; failure of that repository work aborts the entire batch, including otherwise valid promotions.

CommonModules mutation text output treats manifest-only changes as first-class rather than deriving success from copied paths. It emits an operation-specific line for every installed CommonModule, updated CommonModule source path, dependency-to-direct-request promotion, test-only classification change, orphan transition, and newly added CommonModules-required reference, then separate counts for module installation, source update, direct-request promotion, test-only classification change, orphan transition, required-reference addition, and unchanged CommonModule targets. The unchanged count uses the same dependency-expanded Add closure or Update-wide target set as the exhaustive machine-readable `modules` results rather than counting only original command arguments; its human label is `Unchanged CommonModules`. A newly orphaned target is rendered as `Marked CommonModule as orphaned`, while an already orphaned unchanged target is still rendered as `Retained orphaned CommonModule`. A required reference already present in the document is not reported as added. `No CommonModules changes.` is reserved for an operation whose rebased result changes neither source nor manifest, even though a retained orphan and its warning may still be reported.

The corresponding CommonModules mutation JSON result is the authoritative machine-readable projection of that committed success or trusted no-op. Its required top-level properties are `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, `scope: "project"`, normalized absolute `project`, nullable `document`, `operation`, `complete: true`, `warnings`, and `documents`. `operation` is `add` or `update`. Add places the selected document name in `document` and returns exactly one same-named document result; Update places `null` in `document` and may return results for multiple documents. The fixed project scope identifies manifest authority rather than the number of documents the operation spans.

Every document result has its `document`, an exhaustive `modules` array, and a separate change-only `referenceChanges` array. Add's `modules` contains every entry in its dependency-expanded affected closure, while Update's contains every InstalledCommonModule it targeted in that document. Every module result has exactly the required final-state properties `name`, `moduleFile`, `requested`, `testOnly`, `orphaned`, `status`, and `changes`; `status` is `unchanged` exactly when `changes` is empty and is `changed` exactly when one or more structured changes are present. Required-reference changes are document-level because several modules may share one requirement and no single module owns its addition. Top-level aggregate counts, `changed`, and `noOp` fields are omitted: consumers derive them from the exhaustive module and reference-change results. A result is a trusted no-op when every module is unchanged and every document's `referenceChanges` is empty; Update with no InstalledCommonModule targets returns an empty `documents` array.

Each `referenceChanges` entry has exactly required `kind: "added"`, canonical committed `name`, and `requested: false`. It represents only a reference newly appended by this CommonModules mutation. A reference already present in the effective document selection is omitted regardless of whether its existing manifest entry is directly requested; CommonModules mutation neither promotes it nor repeats it as `alreadyPresent`. Entries preserve manifest append order and expose no GUID, version, registry path, candidate set, or resolver diagnostics. The complete selection and resolution inventory remains owned by `reference list`. An unknown reference-change `kind`, any value other than `false` for `requested`, a duplicate added name under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, or order inconsistent with the committed append delta is a schema mismatch.

CommonModules mutation JSON and text use one deterministic result order rather than independently alphabetizing presentation. Add trims requested names, deduplicates them under `OrdinalIgnoreCase`, and preserves their first-occurrence input order. It processes those requests in that order: an already installed name enters the result at its established position without repository traversal, while an uninstalled name expands its validated repository closure through the canonical **CommonModuleDependencyComponent** order above. Newly discovered entries enter at their first component encounter; a later explicit occurrence may change final `requested` state but never moves an existing or newly placed result. Update orders document results by document name under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` with an `Ordinal` tie-break, and orders each document's module results by the final rebased manifest `commonModules` order. Existing entries retain their latest-manifest positions, while newly discovered entries append in canonical component order; spanning an existing and a new member does not reorder established manifest state merely to make the component contiguous. Documents without target modules are omitted, so an Update with no targets has `documents: []`. `referenceChanges` retain committed manifest append order, each module's `changes` retain their fixed kind order, and human text renders documents, modules, changes, and reference additions in the same order as JSON.

The schema `1.0` module-change union recognizes exactly five `kind` values. `installed` has required `sourceSetRelativePath` and represents the new manifest entry plus initial source-unit placement; it is the installed module's only module change because initial `requested`, `testOnly`, and `orphaned` facts are included in installation. `sourceUpdated` has required `sourceSetRelativePath` and represents creation or raw-byte reconciliation of an existing source unit, including any form-sidecar-only change. `directRequestPromoted` has no additional property and represents only `requested: false` to `true`. `testOnlyChanged` has required final Boolean `testOnly`. `orphanedChanged` has required final Boolean `orphaned`; an already orphaned target that remains so has no repeated transition and may be unchanged. An existing module lists every simultaneous change in the fixed order `sourceUpdated`, `directRequestPromoted`, `testOnlyChanged`, `orphanedChanged`.

Each `sourceSetRelativePath` is the actual DocumentSourceSet-relative source-unit destination, uses `/` separators, and is nonempty without a leading slash, an absolute-root form, or `.` or `..` path segments. A form result names only its `.frm` path. The output exposes no source/repository absolute path, raw-byte fingerprint, hash, or separate `.frx` event. An unknown module `status` or change `kind`, a missing required kind-specific property, a forbidden kind-specific property, an invalid change order, or an inconsistent final Boolean is a schema mismatch. Consumers ignore unknown additive object properties, while unknown warning codes remain schema-valid and retain their human-readable messages.

This schema represents source and manifest changes without collapsing simultaneous effects into prose and includes any non-fatal success warnings such as deferred cancellation. If one or more final target results have `orphaned: true`, successful Add or Update emits exactly one aggregate `orphanedCommonModulesRetained` warning while remaining `complete: true` with exit code zero. This warning is present even when every orphan was already marked and the mutation is otherwise a trusted no-op; it disappears for a reappeared identity only after its source and metadata refresh and `orphaned: false` commit succeed. No JSON success fragment is emitted while planning, copying, saving, recovering, failing, or cancelling.

The schema `1.0` producer recognizes exactly four CommonModules success-warning codes, emits each at most once, and orders present entries by the following fixed rank rather than discovery or exception order. First, `orphanedCommonModulesRetained` uses `Retained {M} orphaned CommonModule[s] across {D} document[s]; no source was removed.`, where `M` is the number of emitted final module results with `orphaned: true`, `D` is the number of their document results, and the rendered message selects grammatically correct singular or plural words. Second, `cancellationDeferred` uses `Cancellation was requested after CommonModules source mutation began; the command completed and committed the mutation to preserve project consistency.` Third, `commonModulesSnapshotCleanupFailed` uses `The CommonModules mutation completed, but its non-authoritative snapshot workspace could not be removed: "{P}".` Fourth, `leaseMarkerCleanupFailed` uses `The CommonModules mutation completed and released its project lease, but the lease marker could not be removed: "{P}".` Each `P` is the normalized absolute retained path. Messages are human-facing: consumers never parse their counts or paths. The initial producer set is closed while consumers remain open to unknown future warning codes.

VBE ambiguity-probe cleanup that prevents conclusive reference resolution, source or manifest atomic-mutation failure, uncertain recovery-artifact persistence, and inability to prove project-lease release are command failures rather than success warnings. Only snapshot deletion after successful mutation and snapshot-handle release, and marker deletion after proven lease release, qualify as the two housekeeping warnings above.

Each CommonModules mutation warning emitted by schema `1.0` contains exactly a stable `code` and a human-readable `message` and describes only a non-fatal condition that does not weaken trust in the complete result or committed state. An ordinary no-op is represented by an unchanged module result, not a warning. A condition that makes the result, commit, recovery determination, or cleanup required for trustworthy completion uncertain fails the command; only explicitly non-critical housekeeping may remain a warning. In JSON mode stdout contains exactly the one success object plus its trailing newline, its warnings appear only in that object's `warnings` array, and successful stderr is empty. In text mode normal result lines use stdout and warnings use stderr. Failure or pre-success cancellation uses a nonzero exit and stderr diagnostics without a partial JSON success object. Consumers ignore unknown warning codes, retain their messages, and tolerate unknown additive object properties because neither determines success or module outcomes.

An exit-zero CommonModules mutation result is trusted only after the consumer validates the recognized schema version, request context, operation, required shapes, recognized discriminants, uniqueness and ordering constraints, and consistency between final state, status, and changes. If that result is malformed or untrusted, source and manifest changes may already have committed. The extension reports `CommonModules command completed but returned an untrusted result. Inspect the manifest and VBA Tools Output.` with Show Output, emits no success or no-op summary, and neither retries, rolls back, falls back to another CLI, nor runs a follow-up list to reconstruct the outcome. This post-execution distrust does not prevent the manifest lifecycle from observing an actual committed change.

That consumer validation boundary deliberately avoids a second CommonModules planner. Common validation covers the schema and request context, required property types, warning shape, unique document and module identities, recognized status and change discriminants, status/change/final-state consistency, and every ordering invariant derivable without repository traversal. Add requires its top-level `document` to match the selected document, exactly one same-named document result, and exactly one case-insensitive module result with final `requested: true` for every trimmed, case-insensitively distinct explicit request; additional dependency results are valid. Update requires top-level `document: null`, unique ordered document identities, and a nonempty unique module result set for every returned document. The extension does not read the CommonModules repository or latest manifest to recompute Add's dependency closure, Update's complete target set, module dependency order, or committed reference append order. The CLI's exhaustive projection is authoritative for those facts, and producer contract tests prove their completeness and deterministic ordering.

The extension emits exactly one terminal notification for a trusted CommonModules mutation result. It derives `C` from module results with `status: changed`, `U` from those with `status: unchanged`, `R` from all `referenceChanges`, and, for Update, `D` from returned document results; a module with several change kinds contributes only once to `C`. Add identifies the selected document and uses `CommonModules updated for {document}: {C} changed, {U} unchanged`, appending `; {R} reference[s] added` only when `R` is nonzero. Update identifies the normalized project root by its folder basename and uses `CommonModules update completed for {projectFolder}: {C} changed, {U} unchanged across {D} document[s]`, likewise appending only a nonzero reference clause. Rendered messages select correct singular and plural words.

When `C` and `R` are both zero, Add uses `No CommonModules changes were needed for {document}.` and a nonempty Update result uses `No CommonModules changes were needed for {projectFolder}.`; Update with `documents: []` instead uses `No installed CommonModules were found in {projectFolder}.` An empty warning array produces one information notification. A nonempty array produces one warning notification containing the same outcome followed by the grammatically correct `1 warning.` or `{N} warnings.` and offering Show Output. A caller-local stdin cancellation write failure likewise converts a trusted success to that single warning notification, appends `Cancellation request could not be delivered.`, and offers Show Output without changing the CLI warning count. Neither case produces a separate success notification or opens VBA Tools Output automatically. Full ordered module changes, reference additions, warning messages, and any cancellation-delivery error remain in Output. Success does not run a follow-up `common-module list`; manifest observation and diagnostic refresh remain independent of outcome reconstruction.

CommonModules Add and Update are **CommonModulesMutationIntent** operations against the latest project state, not replacements of an invocation-start manifest clone. They may compute a complete read-only plan before lease acquisition to reject obvious failures early, but that plan never authorizes mutation. After acquiring **ProjectManifestMutationLease**, the command reloads and validates the latest manifest, recomputes the installed-set and document source-path facts, and determines whether any repository-backed target remains. Only then, when repository work is required, it captures the complete stable repository snapshot and derives all final repository, dependency, RequiredReferences, source-byte, destination, file-operation, and planned-manifest facts from that staged snapshot while holding the lease. Add reapplies its requested names to the latest selected document, treating an already installed entry as the established no-op or `requested` upgrade and requiring repository authority only for names that remain uninstalled; Update uses the latest installed set for every latest document and, when that set is nonempty, requires its complete repository reconciliation. Unrelated manifest changes survive. A missing selected document, invalid latest manifest, or any required repository or source-plan failure aborts before file mutation. The command neither falls back to the pre-lease plan nor loops through automatic retries.

CommonModules Add and Update perform a final cancellation check immediately before their first planned source-file deletion or copy. Cancellation that wins before that **CommonModulesMutationCommitmentBoundary** changes neither source nor manifest. Once the first file mutation begins, the command latches and defers a later cancellation, completes the entire planned file operation set, and reaches either atomic manifest commit or the established recovery-result determination while retaining the lease. The commitment boundary is not success: any copy, manifest, external-edit, or recovery failure still reports its real transaction failure. If manifest commit succeeds after a deferred request, the command returns success with the non-fatal `cancellationDeferred` warning so it never misreports an applied change as cancellation. Any plan with no source-file mutation, including direct-request, reference-only, test-only, or orphan-only manifest changes and a complete CommonModules no-op, has no file-mutation commitment boundary and remains cooperatively cancellable until its ordinary atomic-commit or trusted-no-op success boundary. An ordinary caller waits for the consistency-critical section rather than force-terminating it; abrupt process loss remains the separately documented crash-recovery case.

Every in-lease replan with repository-backed targets materializes a **CommonModulesMutationSnapshot** before deriving its final repository-dependent plan or mutating a target. It captures the selected CommonModules repository manifest, `.bas`, `.cls`, `.frm`, and matching `.frx` bytes into invocation-owned staging, then re-reads every selected live input and aborts unchanged if the repository changed during capture. The command derives the final dependency and file plan only from the validated staged bytes, so subsequent repository edits cannot alter the invocation and repository generations cannot be mixed. The plan also records whether each target source or sidecar existed and, when present, its exact raw-byte fingerprint. Immediately before each planned delete, create, or replace, the command requires the applicable target precondition still to hold. `common-module add --force` and `common-module update` authorize replacement only of the observed target version, not an edit made after planning. An absent target uses create-new semantics; an existing file is written to a flushed sibling temporary file and atomically replaces only the still-matching target. A conflict before the commitment boundary fails without source or manifest changes. If an earlier planned file has already changed, a later conflict preserves the externally changed file, fails without manifest commit, reports the partially updated paths and manual verification guidance, and performs no automatic rollback. As with manifest replacement, a non-cooperating writer in the narrow interval after final comparison and before the individual filesystem mutation remains outside the guarantee.
