The shared CommonModules package-snapshot staging owns its directory and files through one Windows-only `ExactFileSystemObjectOwnership` session implemented inside `VbaDev`, without depending on another workspace tool. A receipt is privately constructed only from create-only or trusted stable proof and binds its normalized route, ordinary object kind, volume, and non-sentinel 128-bit file identity; file receipts additionally bind a single-link length and SHA-256. Cleanup opens the recorded route without following reparses and with no sharing, re-proves the file identity, link count, length, and content or the directory identity and emptiness, then requests disposition against that same handle. File disposition remains tentative until a same-handle postcondition proves the receipt evidence and the recorded link removal; if that proof rejects the object, cleanup clears disposition and proves the exact identity at the original route before reporting retention. Failure to prove that rollback is fatal rather than a false retained result. Replacement, rename, reparse, alternate hard-link, content-change, non-empty-directory, foreign-session, retired-receipt, and incomplete-proof cases are retained without recursive or path-only fallback deletion. A recorded route is already removed only when a no-follow lookup proves that name absent and the still-anchored exact object reports zero accessible links; a live reversible delete disposition remains inconclusive, while any later external restoration or recreation is a new change after that observed cleanup boundary.

One invocation owns one non-authoritative CommonModules mutation snapshot workspace. After a trusted mutation commit or complete no-op and after releasing every snapshot handle, the command applies bounded deletion retries. If only that workspace remains undeleted, the committed source and manifest result stays successful with exit code zero and `complete: true`, and the command adds one `commonModulesSnapshotCleanupFailed` warning whose message identifies the retained normalized absolute directory. The workspace is not a recovery artifact and is not represented as a module change. Before a success boundary, retained snapshot state is reported only as additional failure or cancellation diagnostic context and never produces a partial success object. Any uncertainty in source mutation, manifest commit, or recovery-result determination remains command failure rather than this housekeeping warning.

**VbaProjectReferences** belong to the document definition in `vba-project.json`, under `documents.<document-name>.references`, as objects with required `name` and `requested` fields. `requested: true` records reference intent independent of CommonModules; `requested: false` records an entry introduced only by CommonModules and makes it conservatively eligible for a future fully revalidated auto-remove feature. The initial command surface never auto-removes such an entry merely because current repository metadata no longer requires it. `reference add` edits the manifest only and promotes an already-listed `requested: false` entry to `requested: true`; workbook-backed commands such as `build` and `publish` apply every manifest-defined reference regardless of this flag. `reference remove` deletes the selected manifest entry regardless of `requested` and does not consult CommonModules metadata, preserving an environment-independent repair path; a later CommonModules Add or Update may re-add a still-required name with `requested: false`. Reference names are stored as the human-visible `Reference.Description`-style library name. User input is trimmed and matched case-insensitively, but the manifest stores the reference name as a plain description string. Reference names are unique within one document definition. The manifest does not mark references as protected.

`reference add` records dependency intent and does not preflight the complete
exported `ModuleIdentity` set or the source template's `VbaProjectName` for a
project or object-library name conflict. A registry-unique reference therefore
retains the existing registry-only path and may be added even when current source
will require repair before materialization. Language Server validation and
build-materializing commands own that compatibility feedback. If
an ambiguity probe itself cannot resolve a candidate because its required fresh
VBE baseline rejects the name, resolution fails, the command reports the probe
failure, and the manifest remains unchanged; this incidental VBE rejection is
not replaced by a separate source preflight.

Reference-name equality uses `OrdinalIgnoreCase` after trimming leading and
trailing whitespace. A new `reference add` stores the deterministic registry
representative rather than the caller's casing: among trimmed
case-insensitively equivalent descriptions, the ordinal-minimum spelling is
representative. Adding an already-listed name preserves the existing manifest
spelling. It is a successful no-op when the entry already has `requested:
true`, and promotes `requested: false` to `requested: true` otherwise.
`reference remove` matches case-insensitively.

Registry description matching is only the first reference-resolution stage.
When one name matches more than one registered TypeLib identity and the caller
does not already own a materialization workbook, `vba-dev` starts a dedicated
hidden Excel/VBE process and tests each candidate through
`References.AddFromGuid`. Every candidate starts from a fresh temporary copy of
the same baseline definition: the source template for the document selected by
`--document`, or the manifest's `primaryDocument` when `--document` is omitted.
The returned `Reference` identity is
authoritative: requested candidates that VBE maps to the same returned identity
are coalesced. The coalescing key is the returned GUID, compared
case-insensitively, plus the returned major and minor version. Returned
`FullPath` and `Description`, and registry LCID and path, remain diagnostic
metadata rather than identity-key fields; an empty returned `FullPath` does not
invalidate an otherwise usable result. A candidate-specific `AddFromGuid`
rejection that leaves the owned Excel/VBIDE environment operational marks only
that candidate as unusable. A candidate-local inspection failure leaves that
reference unverified but permits later candidates and names to continue only
when the fresh baseline closes successfully and the owned Excel/VBIDE process
remains trustworthy. Any unverified candidate prevents automatic selection for
its name, even when another candidate succeeded.

A project-scope probe never substitutes a blank workbook when the selected
source template is missing, cannot be copied, cannot be opened, or cannot expose
its VBA project. Registry-only resolution does not inspect the source template
at all, so an invocation with no ambiguous name may still succeed. If any name
requires a probe and its project baseline cannot be prepared, every
probe-dependent name is `unverified` with `reasonCode: "probeAborted"`, while
already conclusive registry-only entries remain in the output. The response
adds a `probeBaselineUnavailable` diagnostic, is `complete: false`, and exits
nonzero. Failure to create or access the blank workbook required by environment
scope uses the same classification. A failure before Excel starts does not add
`probeProcessUntrusted`; if an already started process also becomes
untrustworthy, both diagnostics may appear.

The 60-second reference-normalization deadline applies separately to each
`References.AddFromGuid` attempt and its returned-identity inspection. It resets
for each candidate and whenever a library lineage falls back to its next lower
registered version. Excel process startup, opening each fresh baseline, and
process cleanup retain their separate stage deadlines, and there is no
whole-command deadline. If a timeout, process loss, VBIDE lifecycle failure, or
baseline-cleanup failure makes the probe process untrustworthy, the invocation
does not launch a replacement Excel process. It marks the current reference and
every not-yet-probed name that still requires VBE disambiguation as unverified,
retains results already resolved without that failed probe, and stops further
VBE work. A list response is then `complete: false` and exits nonzero; an
explicit later invocation starts a fresh probe. The entry whose attempt lost
probe trust reports the specific cause, such as `probeTimeout` or
`cleanupFailure`. Each later probe-dependent entry that was not attempted
reports `probeAborted`, retains every candidate identity known from registry
discovery, and does not claim to have experienced the original failure itself.
The same response includes one top-level `probeProcessUntrusted` diagnostic
identifying the shared-process failure.

Cancellation stops the complete command rather than continuing with later
names. Once project or environment scope is established, cooperative
cancellation emits a schema-valid response when cleanup and serialization can
complete: already conclusive entries remain unchanged, while the in-progress
entry and every intentionally unprocessed remaining entry are
`unverified` with `reasonCode: "cancelled"`. The response adds an
`operationCancelled` diagnostic, is `complete: false`, and exits nonzero.
Cancellation alone does not produce `probeAborted` or
`probeProcessUntrusted`. If cleanup also loses probe trust, the specific cleanup
or probe failure takes precedence for affected entries and its diagnostic may
appear alongside `operationCancelled`. Cancellation before scope establishment,
external termination, or another failure that prevents serialization may
produce no JSON; consumers already treat that invocation as untrusted.

Only after every candidate for one name has been evaluated conclusively is one
distinct usable result adopted, no usable result reported as unavailable, or
more than one distinct usable result reported as ambiguous. The probe closes
each copy without saving, removes all temporary state, and does not modify or
save the project's source template, bin workbook, or publish output. GUID,
version, LCID, and path remain environment-specific resolution results and are
not persisted in the project manifest. TypeLib registry version and LCID keys
are interpreted as hexadecimal values in accordance with the Windows TypeLib
registration contract. On supported Windows versions, discovery reads the
merged, shared `HKEY_CLASSES_ROOT\TypeLib` view once; it does not union
`Registry32` and `Registry64` views. Registered `win32` and `win64` paths remain
candidate metadata, but the x64 `vba-dev` process does not use its own bitness
as a proxy for the installed Excel/VBE environment and does not discard
identities on that basis. When registry matching leaves multiple identities,
the VBE probe above is the authority for which identities that Excel environment
can actually add. Multiple registered versions with the same GUID form one
library lineage rather than separate ambiguity candidates. Registry-only
resolution represents that lineage by its highest registered version. When a
lineage participates in a VBE probe, it tries registered versions in descending
order and falls back only after the higher version is conclusively rejected.
The returned GUID/major/minor identity remains authoritative and may therefore
canonicalize an older requested version to a newer installed version.

The same VBE-equivalent resolution contract is used wherever a command must
validate or materialize a manifest-defined reference: `reference add`,
`reference list`, `build`, `publish`, the build stage of ordinary `test`,
snapshot `test`, and `vba-dev doctor`. `reference add`, either `reference list`
mode, and Doctor's independent selected-reference diagnostics use the dedicated
fresh baseline above when registry ambiguity requires Excel. `build`,
`publish`, either test build, and Doctor's build/publish materialization profiles
instead probe ambiguity against the cleaned disposable workbook they already
opened, restore its logical reference inventory after every candidate, and may
adopt the concrete GUID/major/minor identity of a same-name active reference.
None of these callers opens a source template merely to look for that shortcut:
zero registry matches are `unavailable`, and one registry identity is adopted
directly. A same-name reference supplies its identity only after ambiguity has
already required the applicable fresh or in-session VBE probe.
`reference remove`, dynamic completion, `test --no-build`, `import`, and
`export` do not otherwise resolve or probe references.

Each document's manifest `references` array is an ordered set. Every entry has a Boolean `requested`; every `name` is nonempty, contains no leading or trailing whitespace, and is unique under `OrdinalIgnoreCase` within that document. Manifest validation rejects a violation with the document and conflicting spellings before any project command performs partial work. It does not trim, choose a surviving spelling, deduplicate, or infer missing intent automatically; the user repairs malformed selection structure explicitly and Doctor may diagnose it. `new`, CommonModules mutation, and `reference add` always produce this invariant. `reference remove` remains a repair path for structurally valid names that are unavailable, ambiguous, or otherwise undesirable, not a tolerant editor for an invalid manifest. Because the contract is unreleased, no migration path accepts the earlier name-only shape.

Ordinary `reference list` is a read-only document-scoped command that resolves
every manifest-defined reference for the selected document without changing the
manifest. It supports human-readable text and `--format json`; background
language-server catalog refresh consumes only the JSON contract. If every
manifest reference is `resolved`, including the empty reference set, the command
succeeds. If any reference is `ambiguous`, `unavailable`, or `unverified`, it
emits every reference result and returns a nonzero exit code. This is distinct
from `reference list --available`, whose input universe is unadded registry
descriptions and whose conclusive ambiguity or unavailability remains a
successful list result.

Both ordinary and available list modes are pure **VbaProjectReferenceResolutionInventory** operations. They do not parse exported source or inspect a source template merely to decide whether a resolved reference project/library name conflicts with a current `ModuleIdentity`. Such a conflict does not remove an entry, change `resolved` to another status, alter exit status, or add a compatibility annotation to text or JSON. Dynamic completion and the Add Reference QuickPick likewise retain a resolved candidate regardless of current source names. `reference add` may record that dependency intent, after which Language Server validation and materialization preflight provide project-compatibility feedback. Environment-scope and project-scope available inventories therefore retain the same resolution meaning.

`VBA Tools: Add Reference` replaces its exact-`Reference.Description` input box with a project-and-document-scoped **ReferenceAddQuickPick**. The extension requests `reference list --available --format json` and accepts candidates only from a schema-valid, request-matching result with `complete: true`. Only `resolved` entries become selectable QuickPick items, using the exact canonical `name` returned by the inventory when invoking `reference add`. Conclusive `ambiguous` and `unavailable` entries remain visible in VBA Tools Output but are not represented as selectable or disabled-looking items. If no resolved entry remains, the command reports that no resolvable reference is available. An `unverified` entry, `complete: false`, malformed JSON, schema mismatch, or context mismatch aborts before presenting a partial picker and offers Show Output. Cancelling before selection makes no manifest change. The Command Palette provides no manual-name fallback; direct CLI `vba-dev reference add <name>` remains the explicit free-text path.

The initial **ReferenceAddQuickPick** continues to exclude every name already present in the effective manifest selection, including entries with `requested: false`. Before auto-removal exists, promoting such an entry changes recorded intent but not runtime reference selection, and presenting it under Add Reference would imply that it is currently absent. Direct CLI `reference add` remains the initial path for an intentional promotion. A future auto-remove feature may add a separately named directly-requested management action instead of overloading the available-reference picker.

Each selectable item uses the canonical `name` as its label and `TypeLib <major>.<minor>` as its description. QuickPick filtering matches both fields. It does not add a detail line for the GUID or a path: the GUID remains available in the JSON result and VBA Tools Output without making every selectable row two lines high, while paths are not part of the resolved-entry contract. Acceptance passes the canonical name retained on the item and never reconstructs command input from the rendered label or description.

After shared project and document selection completes, the extension creates and immediately shows the `ReferenceAddQuickPick` with `busy: true`, selection disabled, and a discovery placeholder while the available inventory runs. It does not precede the picker with a separate progress notification. Hiding the picker before inventory completion, including by Esc, propagates cooperative cancellation to `reference list --available`; the extension awaits command-owned probe and process cleanup, reports no cancellation error, and never later presents the cancelled result. A trusted complete result replaces the busy state with selectable resolved items. Inventory failure or an untrusted result closes the picker and presents an error action for Show Output.

The picker enables `canSelectMany` and requires at least one selected entry before acceptance. It passes all selected canonical names to one `reference add` invocation in available-inventory order rather than click order. At invocation start, Add partitions the requested names against the valid selected document manifest. Already-selected names use their stored selection as environment-independent authority and are not resolved; an entry already carrying `requested: true` is a provisional no-op, while one carrying `requested: false` is a provisional promotion. Every missing name is resolved conclusively, sharing one command-owned VBE probe lifecycle where ambiguity requires it, before mutation. It writes the manifest once only after every initially missing selected name is conclusive. An ambiguous, unavailable, unverified, failed, or pre-success-cancelled missing name leaves every selection unchanged. At final rebase, an initially selected entry that remains `requested: false` becomes `requested: true` with status `promoted`, while an entry that is already `requested: true` becomes `alreadyPresent`; if another writer removed an invocation-start selection, the complete operation fails with `referenceSelectionChanged` rather than restoring an unverified name. An initially missing resolved name added concurrently follows that same latest-state distinction: `requested: false` is promoted and `requested: true` is `alreadyPresent`. Any still-new resolved names retain inventory order in the single committed manifest update. Neither race creates a partial result.

Accepting the multi-selection closes the QuickPick and starts `reference add` under a separate cancellable progress notification, so discovery and mutation have distinct cancellation boundaries. Cancellation that wins before the atomic manifest commit is silent and leaves the manifest unchanged. Once that commit succeeds, a late cancellation does not convert the operation to cancellation or roll back the committed selection; the command finishes owned cleanup and reports success. Command output remains in VBA Tools Output. Success produces one concise notification that identifies the selected document, while failure offers Show Output and leaves the manifest unchanged. The extension does not immediately run a configured `reference list` after success; `VbaProjectReferenceCatalogLifecycle` observes the manifest and refreshes only when the effective reference name/order selection changes, so a promotion alone does not rebuild an identical catalog.

`VBA Tools: Remove Reference` likewise replaces its exact-name input box with a project-and-document-scoped multi-select **ReferenceRemoveQuickPick**, but its candidate authority is deliberately different. It contains every entry in the selected document's manifest-defined reference selection, preserving stored spelling and manifest order, regardless of whether ordinary reference resolution would classify that name as resolved, ambiguous, unavailable, or unverified. Candidate discovery reads no registry data and starts no Excel/VBE or source-template work, so removal remains a repair path for a broken environment or bad selection. The picker requires at least one selected entry and submits the retained names to one atomic `reference remove` invocation. An empty selection reports that the selected document has no configured references. The Command Palette has no free-text fallback; direct CLI `reference remove <names...>` remains available.

The picker obtains that authority from `reference list --no-resolve --format json`, not by parsing `vba-project.json` in the extension and not by waiting for ordinary configured-reference resolution. `--no-resolve` remains a document-scoped list operation, preserves manifest order and stored spelling, supports both text and JSON output, and performs no registry, Excel/VBE, or source-template work. It is mutually exclusive with `--available`, which necessarily has a registry-originated candidate universe. Ordinary `reference list` retains its established resolution-inventory behavior.

The no-resolution JSON is a third branch of the same `reference list` schema-version `1.0` union, discriminated by `mode: "selection"`. It retains the required top-level `schemaVersion`, `scope`, `project`, `document`, `mode`, `complete`, `warnings`, and `references` properties. `scope` is always `project`; `project` and `document` identify the normalized resolved request context; every successful result has `complete: true` and normally an empty `warnings` array. Each reference entry contains only its stored `name`, in manifest order, and deliberately has no resolution `status`, `identity`, candidates, reason code, or message. A malformed manifest, unresolved project or document context, or inability to establish the complete selection exits nonzero without emitting a partial selection JSON object. `ReferenceRemoveQuickPick` accepts only a schema-valid `mode: "selection"`, `complete: true` result whose project and document match its request.

After project and document selection, `ReferenceRemoveQuickPick` opens immediately with `busy: true`, no selectable items, and the placeholder `Loading configured references…` while it runs that one no-resolution JSON command. It does not add a separate progress notification for this read-only discovery. Hiding or cancelling the picker cooperatively cancels the subprocess, completes cleanup silently, and suppresses any result that arrives afterward. A command failure, malformed schema, incomplete result, or request-context mismatch closes the picker and reports an error with Show Output. A complete empty selection closes it and informs the user that the selected document has no configured references.

For a trusted nonempty result, the picker clears its busy state, enables `canSelectMany`, and uses `Select references to remove` as its placeholder. No item is initially selected because removal must be explicit. Each item uses the exact stored `name` as its label and search text, in manifest order, with no description, detail, resolution status, version, or GUID. The retained entry name, rather than reparsing rendered text, is passed to removal.

The selection inventory is a UI snapshot, not a manifest version token. On acceptance, the separate `reference remove` invocation resolves the latest valid project and document manifest state available at its own start and applies the selected names case-insensitively to that state. A selected name already absent is a successful no-op; a selected name still present is removed; references added later or otherwise unselected are preserved in their current order. If every selected name is already absent, the command succeeds without a write. A missing document or invalid latest manifest fails without mutation. The extension does not automatically refresh, reopen, or ask the user to reconfirm a stale picker.

Accepting a nonempty removal selection closes the QuickPick and starts `reference remove` under a separate cancellable progress notification. Cancellation while waiting for `ProjectManifestMutationLease` or otherwise before atomic manifest commit is silent and leaves the manifest unchanged. Once commit succeeds, a later cancellation does not convert the result to cancellation or roll back the removal; the command completes owned cleanup and reports success. Command output remains in VBA Tools Output. Success produces one concise notification that identifies the selected document, while failure offers Show Output and leaves the manifest unchanged. The extension does not immediately run a configured `reference list` after success; the effective manifest reference-selection change drives the existing `VbaProjectReferenceCatalogLifecycle` refresh.

Both `reference add` and `reference remove` accept `--format text|json`, defaulting to human-readable `text` for direct CLI use. Their schema-versioned JSON mutation result distinguishes names that changed the latest manifest selection from requested names that became successful no-ops during rebase. Add further distinguishes a newly effective reference from an existing reference whose direct intent was promoted. The extension always requests and validates JSON rather than parsing human-readable lines or treating the originally selected count as the changed count. An Add result containing only additions produces `Added N references to <document>.`; one containing only promotions produces `Marked N references as directly requested for <document>.`; a mixed result reports both counts without calling every change an addition. Remove produces `Removed N references from <document>.`; an all-no-op result produces `No reference changes were needed for <document>.` Neither outcome triggers a follow-up list solely to calculate the notification.

The JSON success object has required `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, `scope: "project"`, normalized absolute `project`, selected `document`, `operation`, `complete: true`, `warnings`, and `results` properties. `operation` is `add` or `remove`. Input names are trimmed and deduplicated case-insensitively while retaining first-occurrence order and spelling; `results` contains exactly one entry for each such request in that order. Every entry has `requestedName`, nullable `storedName`, and `status`. Add permits `added`, `promoted`, and `alreadyPresent`, each with the exact name committed or already stored in the latest manifest. `promoted` means that the existing entry changed from `requested: false` to `requested: true`; it is a manifest change but not a newly effective reference. Remove permits `removed` with the exact removed manifest spelling and `alreadyAbsent` with `storedName: null`. The arrays are exhaustive, so consumers derive added, promoted, removed, and unchanged counts rather than accepting redundant aggregate fields. The command emits this object only after the complete atomic mutation or complete no-op succeeds. Resolution failure, context conflict, external-edit conflict, lease failure, and pre-success cancellation exit nonzero without a partial success object.

Default text output renders the same exhaustive result set for people rather than printing only changed entries or one ambiguous no-change sentence. It starts with the selected document and operation, then emits one humanized `Added`, `Marked as directly requested`, `Already present`, `Removed`, or `Already absent` line per result in request order, and ends with separate added, promoted, removed, and unchanged counts as applicable. A nonnull exact `storedName` is the primary display name; when it differs ordinally from `requestedName`, the line appends the requested spelling parenthetically. `alreadyAbsent` displays its `requestedName`. An all-no-op command still emits all result lines and its summary. This prose is not a machine contract; automation uses JSON.

Each mutation warning contains exactly a stable `code` and human-readable `message` and describes only a non-fatal condition that does not weaken trust in the complete result or manifest commit. An ordinary no-op is represented only by its result status. A condition that makes the result, commit, or owned cleanup required for trustworthy completion uncertain fails the command instead of becoming a warning. In JSON mode stdout contains exactly the one success object plus its trailing newline; its warnings appear only in that object's `warnings` array and are not duplicated on stderr. In text mode normal result lines use stdout and warnings use stderr. Failure uses a nonzero exit and stderr diagnostics without a partial JSON success object. Mutation-schema consumers ignore unknown warning codes and retain their messages because warnings never determine success or per-name outcome.

The extension emits exactly one terminal notification for a successful mutation. An empty warning array uses an information notification with the established added, promoted, removed, and unchanged outcome as applicable, or the established no-change message when every result is a no-op. A nonempty warning array instead uses one warning notification, not separate success and warning notifications: it reports the same operation-specific outcome together with the warning count, applies correct singular or plural wording, and offers Show Output. VBA Tools Output does not open automatically. Warnings neither roll back the successful mutation nor suppress ordinary manifest observation; a promotion alone does not change the effective reference-name/order fingerprint.

An exit-zero mutation result is trusted only when the extension validates the recognized schema version, project scope, normalized request-matching project and document, invoked operation, `complete: true`, warning shapes, operation-specific status and `storedName` nullability, and an exact case-insensitive one-to-one partition of the normalized submitted names with no missing, duplicate, or extra result. Consumers ignore unknown additive properties at every object level and unknown warning codes, but reject an unknown schema version, scope, operation, status, wrong type, missing required property, or status-inconsistent property. If an executed mutation exits zero but its result is malformed or untrusted, the manifest may already have committed: the extension reports `Reference command completed but returned an untrusted result. Inspect the manifest and VBA Tools Output.` with Show Output, emits no success or no-op count, and neither retries, rolls back, nor falls back to another CLI. This post-execution distrust does not prevent the manifest lifecycle from observing an actual committed change.

Reference mutation has two equivalent success boundaries. A changed rebase performs its final cancellation check before atomic manifest commit, and commit establishes success. An all-no-op rebase performs its final cancellation check before recording the trusted complete no-op decision, and that decision establishes success without a write. Cancellation observed before the applicable boundary is silent and leaves the manifest unchanged. Cancellation arriving afterward cannot replace commit or the complete no-op with cancellation; the command finishes owned cleanup and returns the exhaustive success result.

Every `ProjectManifest` persistence path uses one shared crash-atomic commit contract rather than direct in-place `File.WriteAllText`. The complete intended manifest is serialized and validated before persistence, written with the required encoding and trailing newline to a uniquely named sibling temporary file, flushed to stable storage, and checked for cancellation before commit. An existing manifest is committed through a same-volume atomic replacement; an initially absent manifest uses an atomic move that does not overwrite a concurrently appeared target. Failure, cancellation, or inability to perform the atomic operation before that boundary preserves the prior manifest and never falls back to direct overwrite. Once replacement or move succeeds, a later cancellation cannot undo success. Pre-commit temporary files receive best-effort cleanup. Pure manifest-only mutations need no recovery file because the original survives a failed commit; operations that already changed other project files retain their separately established recovery-file behavior. This contract governs crash safety only; concurrent-edit detection and rebasing are separate decisions.

A **ProjectManifestRecoveryArtifact** follows the same serialization, validation, encoding, trailing-newline, flush, and same-volume atomic-move guarantees. Its final sibling name begins `vba-project.failed-`, includes a UTC timestamp and collision-resistant random suffix, and is always created without overwrite; an improbable collision selects another unique final name instead of replacing prior recovery evidence. The command first creates and flushes a unique sibling temporary file, then atomically moves it to that final name. Only a completed final move establishes manual recovery authority. A temporary file surviving abrupt termination is not a recovery artifact and is never reported as one. Recovery content is never applied automatically. If recovery persistence also fails, the command reports both failures without printing the planned manifest JSON. The owning **ProjectManifestMutationLease** remains held through this recovery-result determination.

Reference mutation uses operation-specific pre-commit rebasing rather than whole-manifest optimistic rejection or a persisted manifest version. After resolving every requested Add name that was missing from the invocation-start selection, `reference add` reloads and validates the latest manifest immediately before commit. The command preserves unrelated document and manifest changes and requires every invocation-start-present request to remain present. A latest entry with `requested: true` is `alreadyPresent`; one with `requested: false` is promoted without rewriting its spelling. An initially missing resolved name that is now present follows the same latest-state distinction, while only still-missing resolved names are appended in the established selection order. When at least one name still needs appending, the selected document must retain the invocation-start canonical source-template path because that resolution result would enter the commit; a changed selection fails the complete operation. When no name needs appending, source-template selection change is irrelevant: promotions still use atomic manifest commit, while an entirely `alreadyPresent` result establishes a complete no-op. Removal of an invocation-start-present requested name produces `referenceSelectionChanged`; a removed document or invalid latest manifest always fails. The invocation-start source-template snapshot remains the resolution baseline for an actual addition; unchanged canonical path selection does not cause a second byte or content-fingerprint check. `reference remove` reloads before commit and removes only selected names still present while preserving every other latest change, and source-template changes are irrelevant to that removal authority. A removed document or invalid latest manifest fails. Both paths perform one crash-atomic commit when the rebased delta adds, promotes, or removes an entry.

All `VbaDev` writers for one **ProjectRootIdentity** serialize their final mutation window through one cross-process **ProjectManifestMutationLease**, preventing two independently rebased manifests or two alias-selected creators from replacing each other. Reference Add and Remove do not hold the lease during registry discovery, VBE ambiguity probing, QuickPick display, or other read-only work; they acquire it immediately before the latest-manifest reload and retain it through rebase and crash-atomic commit. CommonModules Add and Update may finish read-only planning first, but acquire the same lease before reloading the latest manifest, fully replanning their mutation, and performing the first source-file delete or copy, retaining it through manifest commit or recovery-result determination. `new excel` establishes its prospective root identity and, only when necessary, creates the missing path from the nearest existing ancestor through the requested target root so the root can host the manifest-sibling marker; those invocation-recorded directories are its only pre-lease filesystem mutations. It acquires the identity-converged lease before creating `src`, `bin`, `publish`, the source workbook, copied CommonModules, or any other project artifact, then revalidates that `vba-project.json` is absent, the root contains no entry except its owned marker, and the filesystem identity still matches. It retains the lease through the initial manifest's crash-atomic move or, when failure or cancellation wins before that move, through rollback of unchanged in-target project artifacts. Lease release, safe removal of its still-unowned marker, and empty invocation-created root and ancestor cleanup follow before terminal outcome classification. A concurrent creator through the same or an alias path therefore waits for the lease and then fails against the completed or otherwise nonempty target instead of merging with or overwriting it. Waiting for ownership is cooperatively cancellable; cancellation or lease-acquisition timeout before ownership changes nothing except that `new excel` may already have created the otherwise empty directory chain needed to host the marker. Process termination releases exclusive ownership, and an unowned leftover marker cannot permanently block a later invocation. This lease coordinates `VbaDev` writers only; an independent editor that does not participate remains a separate concurrent-edit case.

Before `new excel` creates a project artifact, it completes every available non-Excel validation and computes and validates the complete initial CommonModules copy plan. It records each file and directory it creates, including every previously missing component between the nearest existing ancestor and the target root. If failure or cancellation wins before the initial manifest's atomic move, the command latches that original outcome, first proves release of its owned Excel process, and, while retaining the lease, removes only unchanged command-created project artifacts inside the target other than the lease marker and root. It then proves lease-handle release, removes its marker only when it is still unowned, and removes the invocation-created empty target root and ancestors leaf-to-root, stopping at the first pre-existing or nonempty directory. It never recursively deletes the target root or removes unknown, pre-existing, externally changed, or newly nonempty content; a directory that existed before the invocation remains even when empty. Only completely proved cleanup reports the original failure or, when cancellation was the original outcome, exit `130`. Inability to prove owned Excel or lease release, or any retained command-owned marker, artifact, or empty invocation-created directory that should have been removable, returns exit `1` with the original outcome, `newProjectCleanupIncomplete`, every retained absolute command-owned path, and manual recovery guidance. A target root or ancestor retained solely because preserved foreign content makes it nonempty is expected `newProjectTargetChanged` state rather than cleanup failure. When foreign conflict and owned cleanup failure coexist, the command reports both classifications and all retained paths. The initial manifest's successful atomic move is the project-creation success boundary and is never rolled back; after that boundary, an otherwise trusted result may retain exit `0` with `leaseMarkerCleanupFailed` only when lease release is proved and marker unlink alone fails.

Project artifact ownership is held by one invocation-scoped `ExactFileSystemObjectOwnership` session. Each rollback-eligible project file or directory receives an opaque receipt at its exclusive creation; the initial workbook creator returns the final workbook's create-only receipt from that same session. Diagnostic `InitialWorkbookArtifactEvidence`, path strings, hashes, and reconstructed native identity never grant rollback authority. The tracker retains receipt order and workflow-specific cleanup classifications, while the shared module owns identity, ordinary-object, single-link, exact-byte, same-handle disposition, and non-destructive observation proof. A changed, replaced, reparse, hard-linked, foreign-session, or unprovable artifact is preserved and reported for manual inspection. A failed partial creation is removed only when the creation handle still proves its exact bytes and identity; retained partial state is reported without an automatic cleanup retry. Session disposal releases every retained handle on success and failure without deleting committed project artifacts. Existing directories never acquire rollback authority merely because a child was created inside them.

The private Excel staging directory has a separate create-only directory receipt and session. Immediately after Save, a pending capture fixes the saved bytes and native object anchor while allowing the Excel producer's existing write handle. That pending value is not a receipt and grants no deletion authority. After owned Excel release, strict same-object, ordinary/single-link, exact-byte validation under a deny-write/delete handle must succeed before the session issues its one stable-capture receipt. Neither final materialization nor staging cleanup recaptures authority from the current path. If a tentative native deletion cannot be proved rolled back, `TargetChanged` and `RollbackUnproven` remain independent facts. The command reports incomplete cleanup and an inconclusive retained set, preserves the original failure chain, and does not claim successful preservation.

Initial workbook automation runs as a narrow `AutomationExcelProcessRuntime`
scenario. The shared runtime owns startup, private desktop, bounded COM stages,
process cleanup, and STA retirement; the scenario establishes and verifies the
existing baseline and saves to the creator-owned staging artifact. The creator
consumes terminal lifecycle evidence before completing ownership capture or
materializing a final workbook. Neither a saved workbook nor process exit alone
establishes successful creation when dispatcher retirement is unproved. The
scenario cannot commit or roll back a project. Those decisions, including exact
rollback before manifest commitment and success preservation after commitment,
remain with `NewProjectCommand`; no public output or result schema changes.

Abrupt process or operating-system termination may prevent that cooperative rollback. The initial contract does not add a durable project-creation journal, automatic resume, or force-cleanup option, and the advisory lease metadata never becomes recovery authority. A later `new excel` may reuse a target that contains only an unowned lease marker. If the manifest is absent and any other entry remains, it fails without deleting or overwriting content, identifies the target as possibly but not provably left by an incomplete creation, and tells the user to inspect, move, or remove that content or choose another output. If the manifest exists, initial creation already crossed its atomic success boundary; rerunning `new` reports the existing project and may direct the user to Doctor for health verification rather than recreating it.

The directory proven empty except for the owned lease marker after acquisition is the invocation's **InitialProjectTarget**. `new excel` records every file and directory it creates together with each file's type and post-creation raw-byte fingerprint. Immediately before the initial manifest's atomic move, it recursively enumerates the target and requires its complete state to consist only of the owned marker, invocation-owned temporary state, and the expected unchanged project artifacts. Any foreign entry or missing, replaced, type-changed, or content-changed artifact fails with `newProjectTargetChanged`; even an apparently unrelated file is neither merged nor adopted because the command creates a project only in an empty target. The command performs no automatic retry. It withholds the manifest, rolls back only still-unchanged command-owned artifacts under the established cleanup contract, preserves every foreign or changed path, and reports those retained paths and rerun guidance. Foreign content by itself, including an invocation-created directory retained only to preserve that content, does not produce `newProjectCleanupIncomplete`; that diagnostic is reserved for failure to remove command-owned rollback state or prove required release. If both conditions occur, diagnostics retain both classifications. A non-cooperating writer in the narrow interval after final inventory comparison and before manifest move remains outside the guarantee, while the initial manifest move itself always refuses overwrite.

`VbaDev` does not coordinate that independent case through VS Code dirty state, auto-save, editor hooks, or a persisted manifest version. Inside the lease it fingerprints the exact raw bytes of the latest manifest used for mutation and compares the on-disk bytes again immediately before atomic replacement. A complete no-op performs the equivalent comparison after its final cancellation check and immediately before recording its no-op success boundary. An observed mismatch is an external-edit conflict and is never overwritten or reported as a trusted no-op. Pure Reference Add or Remove discards any temporary output and fails without manifest or project-file changes or automatic retry. CommonModules Add or Update preserves the externally edited manifest, writes the already established planned-manifest recovery file, and fails with an explicit warning that source files may already reflect its copy plan and that recovery requires a manual merge. Initial project creation never overwrites a manifest that appears before its atomic move. This optimistic fence does not claim serializability against a non-cooperating writer that enters the narrow interval after final fingerprint comparison and before atomic replacement or no-op success; full coordination is guaranteed only among `VbaDev` writers that honor the lease.

The lease is represented by the sibling path `vba-project.json.vba-dev.lock`, opened for write with an OS sharing mode that excludes another writer for the lifetime of the mutation window. After acquisition and before project mutation, the owner writes and flushes one complete advisory JSON object with `schemaVersion: "1.0"`, a random `leaseId`, `machineName`, `processId`, UTC ISO-8601 `processStartTimeUtc`, a stable argument-free `command` name, UTC ISO-8601 `acquiredAtUtc`, and `toolVersion`. It contains no manifest path, username, command arguments, reference or module names, full command line, working directory, or environment values. Failure to establish this metadata releases the handle and fails before mutation. Diagnostics may report any readable fields but never use metadata alone as ownership or stale authority; malformed or partially readable metadata yields a generic busy diagnostic.

A contender polls cancellation-aware for at most 30 seconds. Expiry returns a nonzero `manifestMutationBusy` outcome identifying the canonical manifest path and any readable safe owner metadata; it never force-removes the marker or terminates the owner. Process termination releases the file handle. Where supported, the owner requests OS delete-on-close semantics as crash cleanup, without making that behavior the ownership authority. A marker left without an owning handle is reclaimed through trusted stable capture and exact receipt-authorized deletion before create-only acquisition, so stale PID liveness heuristics are unnecessary. Release closes the handle and cleans up any remaining unchanged receipt-owned marker; deletion racing a new owner must not disturb that owner. If a successful mutation cannot remove its now-unowned marker, it retains success and adds the non-fatal warning `leaseMarkerCleanupFailed`. Failure to create or exclusively open the marker fails before mutation. The initial contract adds neither a CLI timeout option nor a manifest setting for this wait.

Manifest lease markers use the same `ExactFileSystemObjectOwnership` boundary
as other invocation-owned artifacts. Creation issues a receipt without closing
and reopening the exclusive owner handle. That handle, not JSON metadata,
continues to authorize the live lease. After release, the receipt proves the
unchanged ordinary single-link object and exact content before any explicit
deletion. A copied lease identifier, changed bytes, replacement, alias, reparse
point, or inconclusive observation cannot substitute for that proof. The
receipt's anchor lifetime must not delay established DeleteOnClose cleanup past
the completed owner-release observation. Stale capture uses complete content
evidence; the bounded advisory metadata parser does not become a new stale-file
size limit.

Application owns only the injected session contract and opaque receipt/result
types. Infrastructure owns their private native proof, handle lifetime, hashes,
no-follow identity resolution, and exact deletion. Domain retains pure identity
models and comparisons, with concrete resolution supplied by Infrastructure.
The language server's existing path-identity consumers follow the relocated
public resolver rather than duplicating it; this permitted downstream reference
does not introduce a VbaDev dependency on another product or invoke Excel.
This consolidation adds no recursive cleanup, cohort receipt, generic
transaction framework, portable exact-deletion fallback, extra retry policy,
or stronger concurrent-editor mutation guarantee.

`VbaDev` never creates or edits `.gitignore` or `.git/info/exclude` merely to hide the lease marker, including during `new`. User documentation may recommend the exact optional ignore entry `vba-project.json.vba-dev.lock`. This preserves repository ownership at the cost of a transient untracked marker during a mutation, or a harmless retained marker after exceptional cleanup failure.

In project scope, `reference list --available` switches the document-scoped
list from manifest-defined references to registered human-visible descriptions
that are not yet present in the selected document's manifest. It
deduplicates and subtracts names with `OrdinalIgnoreCase`. Each available group
uses the ordinal-minimum trimmed registry spelling, and output is ordered by
`OrdinalIgnoreCase` with `Ordinal` as the tie-break. One registry scan builds the
grouped candidate catalog for the complete invocation; the command does not
rescan the full TypeLib registry once per name. It applies the same
VBE-equivalent resolver to every remaining name. Names with one registry
identity require no Excel/VBE probe; only names that retain multiple registry
identities enter the ambiguity probe. The command owns one dedicated Excel/VBE
process when at least one such probe is needed, and both ordinary and available
list modes avoid Excel/VBE when registry resolution is already unique.

`reference list --available` alone may fall back to environment scope when
neither `--project` nor `--document` was supplied and upward discovery finds no
`vba-project.json`. It emits a warning and uses a newly created blank Excel
workbook as the fresh baseline only for names that still require a
VBE-equivalent ambiguity probe; registry-unique names still avoid Excel. An
explicit project or document selection that cannot be resolved, a manifest that
exists but is malformed, or a selected document absent from a valid manifest is
a context error and never falls back. Ordinary `reference list`, `reference
add`, and `reference remove` also remain project-scoped and never use this
fallback. The environment-scope input universe is every case-insensitively
distinct registered human-visible description; there is no manifest-backed
subtraction. References already checked by default in the blank workbook remain
in the list. The blank workbook supplies only the ambiguity-probe baseline and
does not become an alternative source of available-name filtering.

The available list does not silently omit names that cannot be selected. Every
discovered available name has one resolution status: `resolved` for one selected
identity, `ambiguous` for multiple conclusively usable identities,
`unavailable` when all candidates are conclusively unusable, or `unverified`
when the probe cannot complete reliably. Conclusive `ambiguous` and
`unavailable` entries are valid available-list results and do not make that mode
fail. If any entry is `unverified`, the command emits the partial list and
returns a nonzero exit code. Registry discovery failure and project/document
context failures that are ineligible for the environment fallback also return
nonzero rather than emitting a misleading successful list.

Registry records whose human-visible description is empty or unreadable cannot
form available-list entries and are skipped. When a readable description has
both valid and malformed registered identities, resolution uses only the valid
identities. When it has no valid identity, the name remains in the output as
`unavailable` with `reasonCode: "noUsableIdentity"` rather than being skipped.
Any individually skipped nameless record or malformed sibling identity
contributes to one aggregate `malformedRegistrationsSkipped` warning containing
the skipped count; it does not by itself make the catalog incomplete. A
registry-root or enumeration failure that may have hidden whole names instead
adds a `registryCatalogIncomplete` diagnostic, sets `complete: false`, and exits
nonzero.

Human-readable output separates resolved names under `Configured references:`
or `Available references:`, according to the selected mode, from `ambiguous`,
`unavailable`, and `unverified` entries under `Resolution issues:`. Environment
fallback writes its warning to stderr without mixing it into the stdout list.
Ordinary and `--available` JSON use one discriminated schema-version `1.0`
object. Its `schemaVersion` value is the string `"1.0"`, and its required
properties are `schemaVersion`, `scope`, `project`, `document`, `mode`,
`complete`, `warnings`, and `references`, plus optional `diagnostics`. `scope`
is `project` or `environment`, and `mode` is `configured` or `available`.
Project scope contains the normalized absolute resolved project root and
selected document name as strings. Environment scope is valid only with
`mode: "available"` and contains `null` for both `project` and `document`.
Every `references` entry contains `name` and `status`, with the following
status-specific required properties:

- `resolved` contains `identity: { guid, major, minor }`.
- `ambiguous` contains `reasonCode: "multipleUsableIdentities"`, a `candidates`
  array with at least two distinct `{ guid, major, minor }` identities, and a
  human-readable `message`.
- `unavailable` contains `reasonCode: "notRegistered"` or
  `"noUsableIdentity"`, a `candidates` array containing every known candidate
  identity, and a human-readable `message`. `notRegistered` has an empty
  `candidates` array.
- `unverified` contains a stable `reasonCode`, a `candidates` array containing
  every identity known before verification stopped, and a human-readable
  `message`. Its reason code classifies the failure as `excelVbeFailure`,
  `probeTimeout`, `identityReadFailure`, `cleanupFailure`, `probeAborted`, or
  `cancelled`. `probeAborted` means the entry was not
  attempted because the shared probe could not be established or an earlier
  failure made its process untrustworthy; it is not used for a candidate attempt
  that caused the process failure.
  `cancelled` means cooperative command cancellation intentionally stopped that
  entry before a conclusive result.

Every public identity uses a lowercase, hyphenated GUID in `D` format without
braces and JSON integer `major` and `minor` values from 0 through 65535,
matching the unsigned TypeLib version fields. Registry and VBIDE spellings are
normalized before output. Identity equality is canonical GUID plus major and
minor. Every `candidates` array contains distinct identities ordered by GUID
ordinal ascending, then major ascending, then minor ascending; this canonical
serialization order is intentionally independent from the resolver's
descending version-fallback order and conveys no preference.

Every `warnings` element contains a stable `code` and a human-readable
`message`. `warnings` is normally empty. Environment fallback adds
`{ code: "projectManifestNotFound", message }`, reports the same warning on
stderr, and may still return `complete: true` with exit code zero when catalog
production succeeds. Skipped individual malformed registrations add one
`{ code: "malformedRegistrationsSkipped", message }` warning with their
aggregate count and likewise may remain complete. A failure that prevents
complete catalog production but does not belong to one reference is represented
by `complete: false` and a non-empty top-level `diagnostics` array. Each
diagnostic contains a stable `code` and a human-readable `message`;
`diagnostics` is omitted when there is no such catalog-level failure. Loss of
trust in a shared ambiguity-probe process adds
`code: "probeProcessUntrusted"` alongside the specific current-entry reason and
any later `probeAborted` entries. Registry enumeration that may have omitted
whole names adds `code: "registryCatalogIncomplete"`. Cooperative cancellation
after scope establishment adds `code: "operationCancelled"` when the command
can serialize its terminal state. Failure to prepare either a selected source
template baseline or an environment-scope blank baseline adds
`code: "probeBaselineUnavailable"`. Registry paths, LCIDs, and stack traces are
not part of this public output. Configured entries retain manifest order, while
available entries are ordered case-insensitively by name. `complete` is false
precisely when an unverified or catalog-level failure made the emitted result
partial; warnings alone do not make it false. The command-specific output
schema version is also advertised by `capabilities --format json`.

A machine consumer may parse a schema-valid response even when the command
exits nonzero. For a complete configured response whose scope, project,
document, and mode match the request, each `resolved` entry is independently
usable, while each conclusive `ambiguous` or `unavailable` entry remains
independently unresolved. The language server accepts only `scope: "project"`
for manifest-reference catalog refresh and never commits environment-fallback
results. An `unverified` entry necessarily makes the response incomplete.
Malformed JSON, schema or request-context mismatch, `complete: false`, or
nonzero exit without a valid response makes the entire invocation untrusted
rather than a source of partial identity updates.

A complete configured response contains each manifest reference exactly once,
matched case-insensitively, with no extra name and in manifest order. A missing,
extra, duplicate, or out-of-order entry is a request-context mismatch and makes
the complete invocation untrusted.

Schema `1.0` consumers ignore unknown additional JSON properties at every
object level, but treat unknown `schemaVersion`, `scope`, `mode`, `status`, or
status-specific `reasonCode` values as a schema mismatch. Missing required
properties, wrong JSON types, and known status-inconsistent properties are also
schema mismatches: a `resolved` entry cannot contain `candidates` or
`reasonCode`, and an issue entry cannot contain `identity`. Unknown warning and
diagnostic codes remain schema-valid because they carry a required
human-readable message and do not enable a successful identity decision.
Warnings do not alter completeness, while every diagnostic-bearing response is
already `complete: false`. A noncanonical or unparsable GUID, an out-of-range
version, a duplicate candidate identity, or noncanonical candidate order is
also a schema mismatch.

The `--available` candidate universe remains registry-originated. It does not
add a reference found only in the source template when no registered TypeLib
description supplies that name; such a reference may be unregistered or broken,
and discovering template-only names would require Excel for every list
invocation. When a registry-ambiguous name is already present in a fresh probe
baseline, that existing `Reference` supplies the concrete returned identity
instead of the resolver trying to add a duplicate reference. This identity
shortcut does not remove the name from environment-scope output merely because
the blank baseline already has it checked. The returned `Reference.Description`
does not replace the configured manifest spelling or the available-list
registry representative; only the returned concrete identity is authoritative.

Tab completion for `reference add` reads the TypeLib registry only. It emits
each case-insensitively distinct `Reference.Description` name once and never
starts Excel or VBE. Completion is discovery rather than final validation, so a
name offered by completion may still fail when the explicit `reference add`
invocation performs its VBE-equivalent ambiguity probe. Such a failure reports
the distinct returned reference identities instead of silently choosing one.

Reference-name completion uses the document selected by `--document`, or the
manifest's `primaryDocument` when the option is omitted. `reference add`
excludes names already listed in that document's manifest and names already
supplied in the current invocation. `reference remove` offers only
manifest-listed names and excludes names already supplied in the current
invocation; its completion path does not read the registry or start Excel or
VBE. Comparisons and duplicate removal are case-insensitive, and candidates are
ordered case-insensitively by name. These filters do not change the command
contract: manually adding an already-present reference or removing an absent
reference remains a successful no-op.

If completion cannot resolve project or document context, it returns no dynamic
reference candidates and writes no diagnostic to standard output or standard
error. This includes a missing or unreadable manifest, an invalid `--project`,
and an unknown `--document`. `reference add` does not fall back to an
unfiltered global TypeLib list. Static command and option completion remains
available, while an explicit command invocation reports the normal actionable
project-resolution error. Registry completion groups trimmed descriptions with
`OrdinalIgnoreCase`, selects the ordinal-minimum spelling for each group, and
orders candidates with `OrdinalIgnoreCase` plus an `Ordinal` tie-break. An
individually malformed or unusable TypeLib registration is skipped without
presenting the explicit-list warning. If a catalog-level registry failure makes
the scan incomplete, completion discards all dynamic reference-name candidates
and writes no diagnostic rather than presenting a partial catalog. Explicit
`reference add`, `reference list --available`, and `doctor` invocations report
the actionable registry condition normally.

`reference add --help` and `reference remove --help` do not evaluate their
dynamic completion sources. They show a stable `<references...>` placeholder
and static argument description without enumerating registry or manifest
values. Dynamic names are calculated only for an actual completion request, so
help output and latency remain independent of project, manifest, and registry
state. Help still derives from the same `RootCommand` graph.

The initial generated PowerShell adapter obtains candidates through
`System.CommandLine`'s `[suggest:<cursor-position>]` directive. `vba-dev` does
not expose a separate public `complete` command or completion-result schema.
The directive's newline-delimited labels are sufficient because reference
display names and inserted values are identical. The adapter converts labels to
PowerShell `CompletionResult` values and owns quoting for spaces, apostrophes,
non-ASCII names, incomplete quoted input, and a cursor within the command line.
Those cases are contract-tested on Windows PowerShell 5.1 and PowerShell 7. The
internal transport may become richer later without changing the public
`vba-dev completions script pwsh` setup command.

The generated script embeds the absolute path of the `vba-dev.exe` process that
created it and invokes that exact executable for suggestions instead of
re-resolving `PATH` on each Tab press. It registers native completion for
`vba-dev`, `vba-dev.exe`, and the generating executable's absolute path, with
PowerShell-safe escaping for spaces and apostrophes. A shell restart or profile
reload regenerates the script after an update or relocation. Completion does
not read VS Code settings or the extension's bundled-tool path.

`build`, `publish`, `test`, and `export` use a dedicated hidden Excel COM instance instead of attaching to a user's existing Excel session. `build` and `publish` create a temporary workbook from the source template, flush existing standard modules/classes/forms, normalize **VbaProjectReferences** to the manifest-defined set, import source deterministically, save to a temporary output, and replace the target output only after success. They recursively discover `.bas`, `.cls`, and `.frm` files anywhere under the **DocumentSourceSet**, treat the result as a flat source file set ordered by extension-including exported file name, fail when exported source file names collide case-insensitively, and do not implicitly exclude hidden or tool-named directories. `build` and `publish` do not validate `.frx` placement as a separate precheck; any same-directory `.frx` handling is left to the underlying form import behavior. Reference normalization removes references that are not listed in the manifest and adds references that are listed but missing, while leaving **ProtectedVbaProjectReferences** in place when Office or VBIDE does not allow removal. If an unlisted protected reference remains in the generated workbook, `build` and `publish` report a warning identifying the document and reference name. Missing or ambiguous **VbaProjectReferences** fail the command before source import, with an error that identifies the document and reference name. `publish` excludes **InstalledCommonModule** entries recorded with `testOnly: true` in the project manifest and excludes project-local source marked with `'#ExcludePublish`.

Every ordinary or source-snapshot `build`, `publish`, and build-before-test path performs source and repeated live **WorkbookMaterializationNamePreflight** checks over the effective source set selected for that output profile. Before Excel starts, it stages, decodes, and parses that selected set, requires authoritative `ModuleIdentity` metadata, and rejects case-insensitive source-to-source identity conflicts. It then copies and opens the source template as an invocation-owned temporary workbook, inspects its actual `VBProject.Name`, active reference names, and component names that generation will retain, and rejects every conflict already conclusive at that point. Importable old components may then be removed from this temporary copy so identities absent from the final artifact cannot obstruct manifest reference addition. Registry-ambiguous manifest references are probed against that same cleaned, open workbook session; each candidate uses the actual identity returned by VBE and restores the session's reference inventory before the next candidate. After reference normalization, the command re-enumerates the prepared active-reference set, including protected references that could not be removed and the identities VBE adopted for added references, and completes the case-insensitive comparison against every staged source identity and retained component. A successful prepared-workbook check permits source import. After `VbeImportVerification` succeeds, the command re-enumerates project, reference, and retained-component authority once more; only identities proved to be the imported components are excluded from the retained set. Invalid or incomplete authority and any conflict at this post-import checkpoint fail before workbook save or output replacement. A source unit excluded from publish through manifest-recorded `testOnly: true` or `'#ExcludePublish` neither participates in publish identity comparison nor blocks publish solely because its metadata is invalid; build and build-before-test still include it under their profiles. Structural failures that prevent deterministic selection of the flat source set, such as duplicate exported file names or an invalid manifest, remain command failures before profile-specific name comparison. These checks do not perform CommonModules consistency checking or claim that the resulting project compiles.

Ordinary Project Build, Publish, source-snapshot Build, the build stage of
snapshot Test, and explicit import materialize through one internal sealed
`WorkbookMaterializer`, using closed data-only
`WorkbookMaterializationIntent.ProjectBuild`,
`WorkbookMaterializationIntent.Publish`,
`WorkbookMaterializationIntent.SourceSnapshotBuild`, and
`WorkbookMaterializationIntent.ExplicitImport` intents rather than
caller-supplied stages, callbacks, or strategies. `ProjectBuild` and `Publish`
retain their distinct source-selection, collision, exclusion, and marker
profiles. `SourceSnapshotBuild` consumes a command-owned snapshot capture and
its immutable admission facts without rereading persistent source or
redetecting encoding. `ExplicitImport` consumes the source authority captured
by `VbaSourceAdmissionIntent.ExplicitImport` and the caller-provided existing
target without resolving project context or normalizing manifest references.
All four intents use the same ordered applicable preflight, staging, automation,
release, validation, cancellation, cleanup, and single-commit policy described
above. Snapshot Test executes the exact committed artifact returned by its
materialization result; its later execution and workspace deletion remain
outside the materializer. Public command results, stdout, stderr, and exit
behavior remain stable. Doctor remains on its existing narrow path until issue
#351. This boundary is not a generic workflow engine, plug-in surface, or DSL,
adds no dependency outside VbaDev, and introduces no reverse product
dependency.

`vba-dev doctor` composes the Excel-free project checks with active evidence without producing an artifact. For each document it prepares build and publish source profiles independently, runs their source-metadata preflight before Excel, and opens one invocation-owned disposable source-template copy for the profiles that can continue. In that single workbook session it inspects initial authoritative project, reference, and retained-component identities; removes replaceable components and unlisted removable references; resolves registry ambiguity against the cleaned open session with candidate rollback; applies the selected manifest references; and re-enumerates the actual final authority before reporting each profile's deterministic complete conflict result. Doctor never imports or verifies source, saves the workbook, or replaces an output, and it closes and removes the disposable copy after both profiles. A conclusive failed or incomplete check makes the command exit nonzero. This live materialization/name preflight adds neither compile verification nor CommonModules repository-consistency checking; Doctor retains its existing Excel-free CommonModules checks. It neither invokes nor substitutes for the independent `vba-debug-adapter doctor` native-debug diagnostic.

Name preflight is fail-once rather than fail-fast for `import` and every materializing build profile. Before Excel starts, the command collects every invalid included `ModuleIdentity` metadata result and every source-to-source conflict that can be proved from the selected source set. Invalid metadata prevents the active phase because no trustworthy identity inventory exists. Once live authority is complete, the command collects every conclusive source-to-retained-component, source-to-containing-project, and source-to-active-reference conflict before returning one complete failure report. Conflict groups follow effective flat exported-source order; a symmetric source-to-source collision forms one group located by its earliest member rather than two reversed reports. Within a source group, other sources follow effective source order, retained components follow case-insensitive then ordinal component-name order, the containing project follows, and references follow final active-reference order. A static-invalidity report uses the same effective source order. The report does not continue into an Excel phase merely to discover consequences of invalid source metadata.

`build` may instead receive an explicit immutable **BuildSourceSnapshot** and direct the generated workbook to a snapshot-specific destination without changing manifest-owned workbooks. Snapshot mode uses the paired options `--source-snapshot <snapshot-directory>` and `--output <workbook-path>`; supplying only one is a usage error. Before starting Excel, `VbaDev` compares case-insensitive, filesystem-canonical identities, including reparse-point aliases. The output must be outside the supplied snapshot directory and every manifest document's **DocumentSourceSet**, and distinct from the resolved `vba-project.json` and every manifest document's source template, bin workbook, and publish workbook; inability to establish that safety fails validation without writing output. Any other caller-owned target, including an existing file, remains eligible for atomic replacement. `VbaDev` atomically replaces that explicit output only after successful generation, preserves any previously completed output on failure or cancellation, and leaves the successful output in place after returning. An invocation without either option retains normal manifest-selected source and bin behavior. This remains a build primitive rather than a debug-session feature: `VbaDev` owns validation, its hidden Excel process, output atomicity, cancellation, and invocation-internal scratch, while the caller owns the supplied snapshot and every successfully produced snapshot output after the invocation returns. `VbaDev` does not host DAP, start visible debug Excel, implement Restart, or remove successful output as part of a later session lifecycle.

`vba-dev capabilities --format json` advertises
`featureVersions: { "build.sourceSnapshot": "2.0",
"test.sourceSnapshot": "2.0" }`. The Build feature covers its paired snapshot
input and caller-selected output, pre-Excel output safety, and atomic
replacement. The Test feature covers its snapshot input, command-owned
workspace workbook, test execution, and workspace cleanup. Both cover the same
source-byte and inventory semantics, cancellation, and owned-process release.
Neither advertises a debug-adapter protocol, and consumers need not require the
CLI tool version or complete command-contract version.

A **BuildSourceSnapshot** is a caller-owned directory containing the complete selected-document source set rather than an overlay. Its recursive `.bas`, `.cls`, and `.frm` inventory and same-directory `.frx` sidecars are authoritative as bytes; their paths preserve the original **DocumentSourceSet**-relative layout as provenance even though build identity remains flat by exported file name. The persistent source set is not read or compared for inventory or content. Normal flat exported-file-name collision and form-sidecar rules still apply. `VbaDev` copies the accepted input into invocation-internal scratch before generation, so caller changes after invocation start cannot alter the running build. The source template, references, and other manifest-defined build inputs remain project-owned. The snapshot producer preserves exact disk bytes for clean source and binary sidecars. Text supports BOM-marked UTF-8, BOM-marked UTF-16 LE or BE, and the operation-fixed active Windows ANSI code page without a BOM. The Windows snapshot workflow reads that code page directly from `GetACP` once; ACP 65001 is canonical UTF-8. A recognized BOM is authoritative, while every BOM-less source uses only the strict fixed ACP, even when its bytes are also valid UTF-8. BOM-less UTF-8 therefore requires ACP 65001. Every clean and dirty text source must strict-decode and re-encode to its original bytes before Excel starts. A decoding or byte-round-trip failure rejects snapshot build and test input before Excel. After valid test execution, unsafe, missing, or ambiguous optional source-location mapping retains its non-failing warning policy.

The raw-byte import portion of this contract was gated by a real-Excel `VBComponents.Import` probe rather than adopted as an undocumented assumption. On a supported Windows host, the probe supplied equivalent non-ASCII standard modules (`.bas`), class modules (`.cls`), and UserForms (`.frm`) with an exported `.frx` sidecar in the active ACP, BOM-less UTF-8, BOM-marked UTF-8, and BOM-marked UTF-16 LE and BE. Document modules were excluded because this API does not replace them. `CodeModule` text had to match `VbaCodeModuleProjection.CodeModuleLines`, and UserForm and sidecar-backed control state also had to match, immediately after import and after save, close, and reopen. The probe recorded the active code page and VBE export encoding, including the observed CP932 export baseline on Japanese Windows. Its result selected direct raw import or the explicit internal representation below; it never authorized a silent fallback or mutation of caller-owned source.

The first gate run used Excel 16.0 with active ACP 932. VBE-exported `.bas`, `.cls`, and `.frm` text strict-round-tripped as CP932 rather than UTF-8, and form export produced an `.frx`. Raw CP932 preserved code, component kinds, and form state for all three inputs immediately and after save/reopen. Raw BOM-less UTF-8 imported but persisted mojibake for non-ASCII code. UTF-8 BOM bytes corrupted the component header, causing class and form files to import as standard modules, and both BOM-marked UTF-16 forms were rejected for every component kind. Consequently the no-transcoding assumption is invalid for non-ACP source.

Every path that reaches `VBComponents.Import` uses a separate
invocation-internal **VbeImportSourceSet**. ADR 0037 applies one sealed
**VbaSourceAdmission** authority to explicit import, ordinary Build, Publish,
and snapshot Build/Test. Snapshot input uses the closed admission
